 ellhat_k=sum_i h_i[(s_i+1)v_ik+s_(i+1)v_(i+1),k].         (10)

For example, the first term in (1) contributes
s'h(v_ik+v_(i+1),k), its increment-square term contributes zero,
and its last term contributes h(1-h)v_ik. Their sum is (10).
Substitute the birth recurrence into (10), then use the two occupation
identities just proved. The result is

             ellhat_k=2L_k+1-M_k-(N_k-N_(k-1))/d_k.        (11)

In detail, before the recurrence contribution the sum is
2L_k+1-M_k. The remaining term is
sum_i s'h_i^2(v_i,(k-1)-v_ik)/d_k
=(N_(k-1)-N_k)/d_k. This checks its sign and its denominator.

Formula (11) is an identity, not a new condition replacing the load
inequalities. It identifies the chronological mixed moment that must
be estimated. Bounding N_k and N_(k-1) separately loses the signed
quantity needed for the desired 2d_k<=ellhat_k<=11d_k/4.

### 5.1 The first required constant-load row is settled uniformly

The row k=2 can be evaluated and bounded without estimating the higher
birth states. Put t=d_0, u=d_1, d=d_2, and theta=1-t/d. The nonzero
values of B_n2 before its extinction are B_22=1 and B_32=theta.
Also B_12=0 and B_42=0. Substitution in (10), using the original
first four steps (t,u,t,d), gives

 ellhat_2=[u(t+u)+t(t+u+1)
                 +(t(2t+u)+d(2t+u+1))*theta]/d.

Collecting its terms, with no inequality, gives

 ellhat_2/d=((t+u)/d)^2+(2t+u+1)/d-t^2(2t+u)/d^3.         (11a)

The actual parameters obey
7/5<t<3/2, 199/100<u<2, and 17/5<d<7/2.
The only new lower bound, d>17/5, follows from
d=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2,
sqrt(2)>141/100, and lambda*epsilon^2<(5/2)/128^2<1/100.

For a lower bound in (11a), its three displayed positive magnitudes
obey, in order,

 ((t+u)/d)^2>(339/350)^2>9/10,
 (2t+u+1)/d>579/350>33/20,
 t^2(2t+u)/d^3<(45/4)/(75/2)=3/10.

Here d^3>(17/5)^3>75/2. Hence ellhat_2/d>9/4.
For the upper bound, the corresponding comparisons are

 ((t+u)/d)^2<(35/34)^2<17/16,
 (2t+u+1)/d<30/17<177/100,
 t^2(2t+u)/d^3>(49/25)(19/4)/43=931/4300>21/100.

The last line uses 2t+u>19/4 and d^3<(7/2)^3<43. Therefore

                   9/4<ellhat_2/d_2<1049/400<21/8.        (11b)

In particular the required interval [2,11/4] holds at k=2 for every
actual m>=128. The unresolved constant-load rows start at k=3.

### 5.2 A clock-slack identity, with the nonmonotone term exposed

Put rho=c-2, alpha_clock=rho/(1+rho), tau_i=s_(i+1), and

 omega_i=alpha_clock*tau_i+1-h_i
        =[rho(s_i+1)+1-h_i]/(c-1)>=0,
 Z_k=sum_i mu_ik*omega_i,
 W_k=sum_i mu_ik*tau_i*omega_i,
 H2_k=sum_i mu_ik*tau_i^2.

Take all predecessor quantities with index-1 to be zero. The sharp
original clock inequality (3a) supplies omega_i>=0. Weighting the
birth flow by tau_i^2 and summing by parts gives

 H2_k-H2_(k-1)=sum_i(2tau_i*h_i-h_i^2)B_ik
              =d_k(2L_k-M_k).

The definitions also give
M_k=alpha_clock*L_k+1-Z_k and
N_k=alpha_clock*H2_k+L_k-W_k. Substitution in (11) yields the exact
signed formula

 ellhat_k=c*L_k/(c-1)^2-1/(c-1)
                  +Z_k/(c-1)+(W_k-W_(k-1))/d_k.           (11c)

The explicit level sum is
L_k=c*d_k/(c-1)+k-1+gamma/(c-1). Thus the first two terms on the
right of (11c) equal

 [c^2/(c-1)^3]d_k+c(k-1)/(c-1)^2
                         +c*gamma/(c-1)^3-1/(c-1).       (11d)

For k>=2 this exceeds2d_k: c^2/(c-1)^3 decreases for c>1,
and its value at39/16 is24336/12167=2+2/12167. The remaining
non-gamma terms are at least1/(c-1)^2, and gamma>0.

This calculation does NOT prove the lower load bound. Although
Z_k>=0 and W_k>=0, the difference W_k-W_(k-1) has not been signed.
Its summand cannot be treated as increasing just because birth-state
exit times increase. In fact it has a large decrease in the ORIGINAL
actual word: at the third step, i=2 and h_2=t=d_0,

 tau_2*omega_2=(2t+u)[gamma+rho(t+u)]/(c-1)>4,

whereas at the fourth step, the first d_2,

 tau_3*omega_3=(2t+u+d_2)*4gamma/(c-1)<1.

For the first comparison use 2t+u>47/10 and
rho(t+u)/(c-1)>(2/5)(339/100)/(3/2)>9/10.
For the second use 2t+u+d_2<17/2 and
4gamma/(c-1)<13/[128(7/5)]=65/896. These hold for every
actual m>=128. The expected difference in (11c) may still be
nonnegative, but a proof must use the original exit distribution;
pathwise monotonicity of this clock weight is false.

## 6. An exact reduction that distinguishes the original lower-level order

Put t=d_0, ell=c-1, gamma=ell-t, and

 Z(z)=c*z/(1+ell*z),
 A(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)=1+gamma*z-t*ell*z^2.

These are new scalar functions, unrelated to the adjacent entries A_i.
They are well-defined at every nonnegative reciprocal physical node.
The affine-geometric node relation gives

 (1-t*z)(1-d_(j+1)*z)=A(z)(1-d_j*Z(z)),
 Z(1/d_(j+1))=1/d_j.                                    (12)

Write the original infinite step word in consecutive pairs. The exact
pair at position r is (t,d_(nu_2(r+1)+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent
of2 in a positive integer. After removing every t and lowering the
remaining level by1, the compressed word is the same original ruler
word, with the SAME epsilon. For its first r steps write
P_r(z)=product_(i<r)(1-h_i*z) and s_r=sum_(i<r)h_i. Then

 P_(2r)(z)=A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),
 P_(2r+1)(z)=(1-t*z)A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),
 s_(2r)=c*s_r-gamma*r,
 s_(2r+1)=c*s_r-gamma*r+t.                               (13)

All four identities follow by multiplying or summing the individual
pairs in (12). This is a chronological identity, not an identity of
the full multiset alone. The foil that exchanges the third and fourth
steps destroys the displayed consecutive-pair description.

The Newton factors also transform exactly:

 phi_(k+1)(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)^k phi_k(Z(z)),              (14)

where phi_k(z)=product_(j<k)(1-d_j*z). At epsilon=0, interpreted only
as a coefficient limit, t=ell=sqrt(2), gamma=0, and A(z)=1-2z^2.
At every actual epsilon>0 the linear term gamma*z and the clock defect
-gamma*r in (13) must both be retained. In fact

 gamma=lambda*epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]>0.

Equations (12)-(14) do not yet establish a bound for E or the mixed
moments in (11). They give an exact original-order transformation in
which those estimates can be pursued without replacing an embedded
block's parameters by epsilon=1/j.

## 7. The decimation multiplier changes sign around one

It is false that the multiplier A at all required physical nodes can
be discarded by a uniform inequality A<=1, or by a uniform inequality
A>=1. Both failures occur at every actual m>=128, without using a
standalone-depth parameter in an embedded block.

For z=1/d_j,

             A(1/d_j)-1=(gamma*d_j-t*ell)/d_j^2.           (15)

We have 7/5<t<3/2, t<ell<3/2, and

 2epsilon<gamma
   <(lambda+2/lambda)epsilon
   =(3sqrt(2)-1)epsilon<(13/4)epsilon.                    (16)

At j=2, d_2<7/2, so
gamma*d_2<91/1024<49/25<t*ell. Therefore A(1/d_2)<1.
At the actual highest level j=m-1,

 gamma*d_(m-1)>(4/5)2^(m-1)/m>9/4>t*ell.

Here a>2/5 and c>2 give the first bound. For the second, 2^(m-1)/m
is increasing for m>=2, and even 2^10/128=8 already exceeds45/16.
This proves A(1/d_(m-1))>1. Both indices are in the original residual
coordinate set. These are counterexamples only to discarding the
decimation factor in one direction, not to the desired band bounds.

There is nevertheless a useful uniform estimate. For k>=8 define

 X_k=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.

Then, for every 0<=z<=1/d_k and every integer 0<=r<=2^(k-1),

 0<A(z),
 r|A(z)-1|<=X_k<1/80,
 |A(z)^r-1|<=(80/79)X_k<1/79,
 0<=gamma*r/d_k<=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k.                 (17)

For r=0 all expressions with r vanish. For positive r, (16),
t*ell<9/4, a>2/5, and d_k>a*c^k give

 r|A(z)-1|
 <=2^(k-1)[gamma/d_k+t*ell/d_k^2]
 <(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.

Also c>12/5, so 2/c<5/6 and 2/c^2<3/8. For k>=8, the exact rational
comparisons (5/6)^8<1/4 and (3/8)^8<1/2500 show

 X_k<65/8192+9/3200<1/80.

Positivity follows from z<=1/d_k<1/t. With delta=|A(z)-1|, if A(z)>=1
the binomial expansion gives

 A(z)^r-1<=sum_(j>=1)(r*delta)^j<=X_k/(1-X_k).

If A(z)<1, 1-A(z)^r<=r*delta<=X_k. These prove the third estimate in
(17); the last follows from the linear part of the same calculation.

Thus the multiplier and clock defects are uniformly small far up the
actual spectrum, despite the change of sign in (15). Their precise
rates include BOTH epsilon*(2/c)^k and (2/c^2)^k. This does not yet
control E: converting pointwise spectral estimates to a birth coefficient
requires retaining the signed divided differences, not taking a large
absolute bound on the entire inverse Newton matrix.

## 8. An early-time bound for the entire nearby band

This localization is in PHYSICAL TIME, in addition to the earlier
localization in matrix-column distance. It controls the part of a near
entry accumulated before the low reciprocal columns die.

Fix j>=2 and let E^early_kl be the contribution to (1) of the first
2^j steps, namely the prefix (H_j,d_j). If k,l>=j+6, then

                         |E^early_kl|<10^(-10).           (18)

Here is a uniform proof. Write T=sum(H_j,d_j). The exact word sum is

 sum H_j=a*c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^j-1.

Because 0<a/(c-2)<1 and 2<c, this sum is less than c^j-1.
Consequently T<(1+1/a)d_j<(7/2)d_j. All steps in the prefix are at
most d_j, and d_j>3.

For an integer r>=0 define

 p_r=min{1,(7/2)^r/[r!*2^(r(r-1)/2)]}.

At each query of this prefix, the probability of state j+r is at most
p_r. To prove this, condition on a birth path's first arrival at j.
Reaching j+r requires r subsequent births on increasing distinct
steps. Each specified set has probability at most the product of its
steps divided by d_j...d_(j+r-1); all stay factors are at most one.
The sum of those step products is at most T^r/r!, and (2) bounds the
denominator below by d_j^r*2^(r(r-1)/2). This proof applies at either
end of every step and for every arrival time.

The sequence p_r decreases for r>=3. Thus, for k>=j+6,

 0<=v_ik,v_(i+1),k<=p_6/d_k,
 |v_(i+1),k-v_ik|<=h_i[(5/2)p_5+p_6]/d_k^2.

For the second line, apply the birth recurrence and use
d_k/d_(k-1)<c<5/2. Define Q_6=(5/2)p_5+p_6. The exact fractions give

 p_5=16807/3932160<1/230,
 p_6=117649/1509949440<1/12500,
 Q_6<1/92+1/12500<11/1000.

The first and last terms of (1), polarized for a general k,l, are at
most [T^2+2d_j*T]p_6^2/(d_k*d_l) in absolute value. This uses
2sum_i s_(i+1)h_i=T^2+sum_i h_i^2 and
sum_i h_i^2<=d_j*T. The increment-square term is at most
T^2*d_j*Q_6^2/(d_k^2*d_l^2), since
sum_i s_(i+1)h_i^2<=T^2*d_j. These statements also apply when k=l.

Now d_k,d_l>2^6*d_j. The two contributions are respectively less than

 77/[4*2^12*12500^2] < 1/30000000000,
 49*121/[12*2^24*1000^2] < 1/30000000000.

Their sum is less than10^(-10), proving (18). Every estimate is
uniform in both the row indices and the enclosing m.

For a thirteen-entry row centered at i>=14, take j=i-12. Then both
indices of each entry E_(i,i+r), -6<=r<=6, are at least j+6, so (18)
controls its entire initial prefix through the first d_(i-12). This
does not remove any of the later repeated low steps; their physical
order and elapsed clock remain part of the late contribution.

## 9. Only eighteen reciprocal nodes remain in the late contribution

For this section fix an ACTUAL required row 24<=i<=m-1, put j=i-12,
and consider l=i+r with -6<=r<=6. Extended indices l>=m are only the
same-epsilon coefficient device, as in Section1. All contributing
physical steps are still through the first d_i and are in the original
queried word.

Define R_kb=(d_k/d_b)phi_k(1/d_b) for b>=k, and zero for b<k.
Its triangular inverse D has entries

 D_bk=product_(0<=r<=k,r!=b)(1-d_r/d_b)^(-1),  0<=b<=k,
 D_bk=0 for b>k.                                         (19)

One direct check uses Lagrange interpolation at the distinct nodes
1/d_0,...,1/d_k. Comparing highest-degree coefficients shows that the
weights reciprocal to the products of node differences annihilate
polynomials of degree below k and recover the leading coefficient
at degree k. Apply this identity to phi_r, whose leading coefficient
is (-1)^r product_(s<r)d_s, and rescale by d_b/d_k. The resulting
inverse of R is exactly (19).

Writing P_n(z)=product_(t<n)(1-h_t*z), the corresponding scalar
coefficient identity is

                  v_nk=sum_(b=0)^k D_bk*P_n(1/d_b)/d_b.  (20)

Indeed P_n(z)=sum_k B_nk phi_k(z), by the birth recurrence. Evaluation
at the first k+1 reciprocal nodes kills every phi_r with r>k, and
inversion by (19) gives (20).

For every query n>=2^j and every b<=j, P_n(1/d_b)=0 exactly; the same
is true at the following query. Thus all such nodes disappear from
the late part of (1). Define M^late_ab to be the quadratic coefficient
of the normalized modal columns P_n(1/d_a)/d_a and P_n(1/d_b)/d_b,
using only physical steps 2^j<=n<2^i, with their ORIGINAL elapsed
clocks s_n. Then (20) gives the exact identity

 E_il=E^early_il
          +sum_(a=j+1)^i sum_(b=j+1)^l D_ai*M^late_ab*D_bl. (21)

For l<i every term vanishes already by query2^l; for l>=i the first
column vanishes by query2^i. Consequently the displayed final cutoff
is correct in both cases and adds no physical step beyond the original
trajectory. Only the eighteen nodes i-11,...,i+6 can occur in (21).

Crucially, the remaining inverse columns have a uniform absolute sum.
For each k in [i-6,i+6],

                         sum_(b=j+1)^k |D_bk|<6.          (22)

To prove this, put q_c=1/c and P^q_s=product_(r=1)^s(1-q_c^r),
with P^q_0=1. Factoring each difference in (19) gives

 |D_bk|=q_c^((k-b)(k-b+1)/2)
             *(d_b/(d_b-1))^k/(P^q_b P^q_(k-b)).

Since q_c<5/12, the elementary inequality
product_r(1-x_r)>=1-sum_r x_r for 0<=x_r<=1 gives
P^q_s>=1-q_c-q_c^2>59/144>2/5 for s>=1.
Also P^q_1>7/12. For b>=i-11 and k<=i+6,

 k/(a*c^b)<(5/2)(i+6)/2^(i-11)<=75/8192<1/100.

The middle expression decreases with i>=24. Hence
(d_b/(d_b-1))^k<=exp(k/(a*c^b))<exp(1/100).
Summing with r=k-b and then extending the positive sum to all r>=0,

 sum_b |D_bk|
 <(5/2)exp(1/100)
       *[1+5/7+(5/2)*sum_(r>=2)(5/12)^(r(r+1)/2)]
 <=(5/2)exp(1/100)[1+5/7+625/3206]
 <5 exp(1/100)<500/99<6.

For the second line, ratios of successive terms starting at r=2
are at most(5/12)^3, so their sum is at most125/1603. For the last
line, exp(x)<=1/(1-x) for 0<=x<1 follows by comparing its power
series with the geometric series. This proves (22).

For example, if an analytic approximation changes every normalized
late modal coefficient in this eighteen-node window by at most eta,
then (21)-(22) change each reconstructed near entry by at most36eta.
Discarding the early part costs an additional10^(-10), by (18).
This is a genuine uniform coefficient-stability estimate; no small
fixed-index inverse coefficient is included in its norm.

The pointwise decimation bound (17) is only needed while a reciprocal
column is alive. For the node 1/d_b it is identically zero after
query2^b, so its even-query formula only requires r<=2^(b-1), exactly
the range of (17). Multiplying that bound by an unnecessarily longer
horizon would lose the extinction information.

This leaves a specific analytic step: control the signed late K/T
coefficients under (12)-(14), with the original clock, and bound the
resulting finite window uniformly. Neither (21) nor (22) asserts those
remaining estimates or the signs of b_i and u_i.

## 10. A uniform lower bound for the surviving mass at a killing step

Let pi_k=B_(2^k-1),k be the probability still in state k immediately
BEFORE the original first d_k. Then, at all actual m>=128 and every
same-epsilon coefficient index k>=0,

                              pi_k>17/100.                (23)

This is a quantitative probability bound, not the required quantitative
second-neighbor bound e_k>=1/15.

Here is a product proof. Put q_c=1/c and n_exp,k=2^k-1-k.
All smaller states have died before the first d_k. Evaluating
P_(H_k)(z)=sum_r B_(2^k-1),r phi_r(z) at z=1/d_k therefore gives

 pi_k=product_(r=1)^k (1-d_(k-r)/d_k)^(2^(r-1)-1)
     =(1-1/d_k)^(n_exp,k)
           product_(r=1)^k(1-q_c^r)^(2^(r-1)-1).           (24)

The second equality uses
1-d_(k-r)/d_k=(1-1/d_k)(1-q_c^r). In particular pi_0=pi_1=1,
pi_2=1-d_0/d_2>19/34, and
pi_3=(1-d_0/d_3)^3(1-d_1/d_3)>(3/4)^3(7/10)>17/100.
For the last comparison, d_3>34/5: the formula
d_3=1+lambda^2(1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3) exceeds
1+lambda^2=4+2sqrt(2)>34/5. The bounds on d_0,d_1 are in Sections2,5.

For k>=4 define the positive convergent product

 Phi(q)=product_(r>=1)(1-q^r)^(2^(r-1)-1),  0<q<1/2,
 S_1(q)=sum_(r>=1)(2^(r-1)-1)q^r
       =q/(1-2q)-q/(1-q).

Convergence, as well as the termwise differentiations below on the
closed parameter interval, follows by comparison with sum_r r(2q)^r.
Using log(1-x)>=-x/(1-x) and -log(1-x)>=x in (24) gives

 log(pi_k)-log Phi(q_c)
 >=-n_exp,k/(d_k-1)+sum_(r>k)(2^(r-1)-1)q_c^r
 =-[gamma/(a(c-2))](2q_c)^k
           +[(k+1)/a-1/(c-1)]q_c^k
 >-(325/16)epsilon*(5/6)^k.                              (25)

Here gamma=c-2-a, a>2/5, c-2>2/5, gamma<(13/4)epsilon,
and 2q_c<5/6. The second bracket is positive.

We compare Phi(q_c) with its critical parameter q_0=1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1.
For 0<=epsilon<=1/128, q_c=q_0/(1+epsilon), and

 d/d epsilon log Phi(q_c)
 =1/(1+epsilon) sum_(r>=1)
             (2^(r-1)-1)r q_c^r/(1-q_c^r)
 >=[q_c/(1+epsilon)]S_1'(q_c)>11.                         (26)

Indeed q_c>16/39>41/100 implies q_c/(1+epsilon)>2/5.
Also q_c<5/12 and

 S_1'(q_c)=1/(1-2q_c)^2-1/(1-q_c)^2
          >2500/81-144/49>55/2.

Integrating (26) from0 to epsilon and combining with (25) proves

 log(pi_k)>log Phi(q_0)
              +[11-(325/16)(5/6)^k]epsilon
          >=log Phi(q_0),

since (325/16)(5/6)^4=203125/20736<11.

Finally Phi(q_0)>17/100 follows from elementary bounds, with no
infinite-product evaluation. For 0<=x<1,
-log(1-x)<=x+x^2/[2(1-x)]. The r=1 exponent is zero, and
q_0^2<1/5. Thus

 -log Phi(q_0)<=S_1(q_0)+(5/8)S_1(q_0^2)
 =1+sqrt(2)/2+(5/8)(5-3sqrt(2))/14
 <12/7+11/320<7/4.

The last inequalities use sqrt(2)<10/7 and sqrt(2)>141/100.
The exponential series gives exp(1)<11/4: the sum through degree4
is65/24, and its remaining tail is at most1/100. It also gives
exp(3/4)<17/8: the sum through degree3 is269/128, and the tail
starting at degree4 is at most135/8704; their sum is18427/8704,
which is smaller than17/8. Hence

 exp(7/4)<(11/4)(17/8)=187/32<100/17,
 Phi(q_0)>exp(-7/4)>32/187>17/100.

This finishes (23), including the small indices handled separately.
The cancellation in (25) retains the additive1 in d_k and the actual
epsilon dependence. Bounding the two product factors in (24)
independently would lose that cancellation.

## 11. The constant-load inequalities hold uniformly in the bulk

The following is a new analytic conclusion, still inside this unverified
joint proof draft. For every original integer m>=128, with its SAME
epsilon=1/m, the required constant-load inequalities hold at every

                         17<=k<=m-1:

                    2d_k<ellhat_k<(11/4)d_k.               (27)

The proof below does not assume monotonicity of the oscillating clock
weight tau_i*omega_i. It bounds the actual exit moments on both sides.
Together with (11b), it leaves only rows3,...,16 of the load condition
unproved. It does not prove b_k or u_k.

### 11.1 Mass in the next state before a record step

Let chi_j=B_(2^j-1),(j+1), immediately before the first d_j. We prove

                         chi_j>9/20 for j>=8.             (28)

Write q_c=1/c and D=d_(j+1). The Newton expansion at z=1/D, where all
states below j have died and factors above j+1 vanish, gives

 U_j:=P_(H_j)(1/D)/phi_j(1/D)
     =pi_j+(1-d_j/D)chi_j
     =product_(r=2)^(j+1)(1-d_(j+1-r)/D)^(2^(r-2)-1).

Define the positive convergent product

 Psi(q)=product_(r>=2)(1-q^r)^(2^(r-2)-1),
 C(q)=sum_(r>=2)(2^(r-2)-1)q^r
     =q^2/(1-2q)-q^2/(1-q).

The same finite-tail cancellation as in (25), now with half the leading
exponent, can be checked directly as follows. The exponent sum in U_j is
2^j-1-j. Since
1-d_(j+1-r)/D=(1-1/D)(1-q_c^r), the elementary logarithm inequalities
used in Section10 yield

 log U_j-log Psi(q_c)
 >=-[gamma/(2a(c-2))](2q_c)^(j+1)
       +[(j+1)/a-1/(c-1)]q_c^(j+1)
 >-(325/32)epsilon*(5/6)^(j+1).                            (29)

For completeness, the first term is the sum of
-(2^j-1-j)/(a*c^(j+1)) and the tail
sum_(r>=j+2)(2^(r-2)-1)q_c^r. Summing its two geometric series gives
exactly the displayed first line. The second bracket is positive.

Along q_c=q_0/(1+epsilon), where q_0=sqrt(2)-1, differentiation gives

 (d/d epsilon)log Psi(q_c)
 >=[q_c/(1+epsilon)]C'(q_c)>5.

Here q_c/(1+epsilon)>2/5, and q_c>41/100, q_c<5/12 give

 C'(q_c)
 >41/9+1681/162-10/7-25/49>25/2.

Uniform convergence follows from sum r(2q_c)^r, as in Section10.
For j>=4, (325/32)(5/6)^(j+1)<5, so integrating the derivative and
using (29) proves U_j>Psi(q_0).

We now give an explicit rational lower bound on this last product.
The first nonzero exponent is at r=3, q_0^3<3/40, and q_0^2<7/40.
Consequently

 -log Psi(q_0)
 <=C(q_0)+[20/37]C(q_0^2)
 <sqrt(2)/2+1/185<713/1000.

Indeed C(q_0)=sqrt(2)/2 and
C(q_0^2)=q_0^6/[(1-2q_0^2)(1-q_0^2)]
 <343/34320<1/100. The penultimate rational comparison uses
sqrt(2)<99/70. To compare with log(100/49), integrate
2/(1-t^2)>2(1+t^2+t^4) from0 to51/149; this gives

 log(100/49)>2[51/149+(51/149)^3/3+(51/149)^5/5]
             >713/1000.

It follows that U_j>49/100 for j>=4.

For the other term, log(1-x)<=-x in the exact product for pi_j gives

 -log pi_j>=(S_j-L_(j-1))/d_j
 =1/[(c-2)(c-1)]
   -[j+1/(c-2)+gamma/(c-1)-gamma*2^j/(c-2)]/d_j.

For j>=8, use c<39/16, 1/(c-2)<5/2, gamma/(c-1)<1/50 and
d_j>(2/5)(12/5)^j. The expression (j+63/25)/d_j is less than1/40:
at j=8 it is bounded by (263/10)(5/12)^8<1/40, and this upper
bound decreases thereafter. Therefore

 -log pi_j>256/161-1/40>31/20.

The finite exponential sum through degree4 at31/20 exceeds50/11.
Thus pi_j<11/50. Since 1-d_j/d_(j+1)<1-1/c<23/39,

 chi_j>(49/100-11/50)/(23/39)=1053/2300>9/20.

This proves (28). All exponent counts are those of the original H_j.

### 11.2 Elementary scale and block-moment bounds

Let T_k=S_k+d_k denote the clock just after the first d_k, and retain
L_k,M_k,N_k,H2_k,Z_k,W_k from Section5.2. The following bounds will be
used only at the indicated indices:

 L_k<=2d_k for every k;
 L_k<(171/100)d_k, d_k>10000, L_k/d_k^2<1/1000  (k>=12);
 82/25<T_k/d_k<69/20                               (k>=12);
 16/39<d_(k-1)/d_k<5/12                            (k>=8). (30)

For the first assertion, L_0,L_1<2d_0,2d_1 respectively, and
d_2-d_0-d_1=epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]-lambda*epsilon^2>0.
Induction then uses d_(k+1)>2d_k for k>=2. For the second, the exact
formula L_k=c*d_k/(c-1)+k-1+gamma/(c-1) applies. For k>=12,
k/d_k<(5k/2)(5/12)^k<1/1000 and d_k>10000; also
c/(c-1)<=1+1/sqrt(2)<1+99/140. These imply the stated bounds.

The clock formula from (3a) is

 T_k/d_k=[(c-1)/(c-2)](1-1/d_k)
                  +gamma*2^k/[(c-2)d_k].

Its lower bound exceeds (23/7)(1-1/d_k)>82/25. For its upper bound,
use (c-1)/(c-2)<=2+sqrt(2)<683/200 and
gamma*2^k/[(c-2)d_k]<(325/2048)(5/6)^k<325/16384
for k>=12. Their sum is less than69/20.

Finally d_k<c*d_(k-1) gives the lower ratio. For the upper ratio,
d_7>198, c>241/100 and c-1<23/16 give
d_k/d_(k-1)>241/100-23/3168>12/5 for k>=8.

For k>=12, the sum of squared physical steps in H_(k-1) obeys

                  sum_(h in H_(k-1))h^2<d_k^2/20.         (31)

To verify it, expand the exact finite sum
sum_(r=0)^(k-2)2^(k-2-r)(1+a*c^r)^2. Its three terms, divided by
d_k^2, are bounded above by

 (25/8)(25/72)^k,
 (25/2)(5/12)^(k+1),
 625/13536,

respectively. The first two are each below1/1000 at k=12 and decrease;
their sum with625/13536 is below1/20. This proves (31) without
evaluating a kernel or an individual long word.

### 11.3 Two-sided clock-slack moments

We first prove the lower bound

                         W_k>d_k^2/8  (k>=13).            (32)

Consider the last copy of H_(k-1) in H_k, after the first d_(k-1).
It starts at clock T_(k-1) and ends at S_k. Throughout this block,
state k-1 is zero, so B_ik decreases to pi_k, and is at least17/100
by the explicit product proof in Section10. Also tau_i*omega_i>=0.
Therefore, replacing B_ik by17/100 in this block only decreases W_k.
Drop the positive term sum h_i*tau_i from omega_i=alpha_clock*tau_i+1-h_i.
The right endpoint rule for tau_i^2, together with (31), gives

 W_k >=(17/[100d_k])
   { [alpha_clock/3](S_k^3-T_(k-1)^3)
                                  -S_k*sum_(h in H_(k-1))h^2 }.

Here alpha_clock>29/100, S_k/d_k>57/25,
T_(k-1)/d_k<(69/20)(5/12)=23/16, and S_k/d_k<49/20.
Consequently

 W_k/d_k^2
 >(17/100){(29/300)[(57/25)^3-(23/16)^3]-49/400}
 =1/8+257488429/1920000000000>1/8.

The proof used the actual final H_(k-1) block, not monotonicity of
the clock weight across its record jumps.

The square-clock identity in Section5.2 gives

 H2_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r(2L_r-M_r).

The exit at the first d_r has probability pi_r>17/100, so
M_r>(17/100)d_r. For r>=12, (30) bounds its summand by
(13/4)d_r^2; for all smaller r it is bounded by(383/100)d_r^2.
Also sum_(r=0)^j d_r^2<(7/5)d_j^2 for j>=3: at j=2 the sum is
less than(8/5)d_2^2, and the ratios d_(r+1)>2d_r then prove the claim
inductively. Using the stronger ratios after index8, for k>=16 we get

 H2_k/d_k^2
 <(13/4)(144/119)+(383/100)(7/5)(5/12)^10<4.                (33)

Two particular ORIGINAL exit steps give quantitative lower bounds on
M_k and N_k. At the first d_k, the exit probability is pi_k. At the
first d_(k-1), the probability of being in state k just before that
step is chi_(k-1)>9/20. Thus, putting q_k=d_(k-1)/d_k,

 M_k/d_k>17/100+(9/20)q_k^2,
 N_k/d_k^2>(17/100)(T_k/d_k)
                   +(9/20)(T_(k-1)/d_(k-1))*q_k^3.       (34)

For k>=16, use W_k=alpha_clock*H2_k+L_k-N_k, (30), (33), (34), and
alpha_clock<7/23. This gives

 W_k/d_k^2
 <28/23+1/1000-(17/100)(82/25)
                     -(9/20)(82/25)(16/39)^3
 =9/16-5498383/1515930000<9/16.                           (35)

In particular, for k>=17, (32) and (35) imply an actual positive
signed difference, with no pointwise clock-monotonicity claim:

 W_k-W_(k-1)
 >[1/8-(9/16)(5/12)^2]d_k^2=(7/256)d_k^2>0.             (36)

### 11.4 Completion of the bulk load bounds

In (11c), Z_k>=0, and the explicit baseline (11d) exceeds2d_k at
k>=2. Combining this with (36) proves ellhat_k>2d_k for k>=17.

For the upper bound, use the ORIGINAL formula (11), rather than bounding
both signs in (11c) independently. Equations (32)-(33) and
N_(k-1)=alpha_clock*H2_(k-1)+L_(k-1)-W_(k-1) imply

 N_(k-1)/d_(k-1)^2<28/23+1/1000-1/8  (k>=17).

Put q_*=16/39 and q^*=5/12. Equations (30), (34) now give

 ellhat_k/d_k
 <342/100+1/1000
    -17/100-(9/20)q_*^2
    -(17/100)(82/25)-(9/20)(82/25)q_*^3
    +(28/23+1/1000-1/8)(q^*)^2
 =11/4-404145173/9095580000<11/4.                        (37)

The positive1/d_k in (11) was bounded by1/1000. Every index used here
is at most the required k; every contributing physical step occurs by
the first d_k, inside the original queried word for k<=m-1. This proves
(27) uniformly in BOTH k and the enclosing m.

The rational comparisons in this section were checked by nine elementary
Fraction operations. These compare the displayed proof constants only;
they do not evaluate any kernel, recurrence grid, or horizon sample.
No kernel case, optimization, installation, or fact_submit was used.

### 11.5 Sharpening the cutoff from17 to8

The preceding proof can be strengthened without a finite kernel evaluation.
In fact (27) holds for every required row

                              8<=k<=m-1.                 (38)

Here are the additional estimates, with all changed index thresholds given.

First, chi_j>9/20 already holds for j>=5. Set
X_j=(S_j-L_(j-1))/d_j and a_j^ratio=1-d_j/d_(j+1).
For each factor x=d_r/d_j in the products for pi_j and U_j,

 log(1-x)-log(1-(1-a_j^ratio)*x)
 <=-a_j^ratio*x.

Multiplying by the nonnegative exponents and summing gives
pi_j/U_j<=exp(-a_j^ratio*X_j). As in Section11.1, for j>=5,

 X_j>256/161-(94/5)(5/12)^5>27/20,
 0<a_j^ratio<23/39,
 U_j>49/100.

The function (1-exp(-aX))/a decreases as a increases and increases as
X increases; its first assertion follows by writing it as
integral_0^X exp(-at)dt. Therefore

 chi_j=U_j[1-pi_j/U_j]/a_j^ratio
 >(49/100)[1-exp(-(23/39)(27/20))]/(23/39)>9/20.            (39)

For the last comparison, put x=(23/39)(27/20) and use
exp(x)>1+x+x^2/2+x^3/6. The required comparison of these rational
quantities has positive difference40251633/5597956000 after multiplying
by 1-(9/20)(23/39)/(49/100). The bound on X_j uses the same decreasing
function (j+63/25)/d_j as before, now starting at j=5.

This earlier bound has a useful second-moment consequence. For r>=6,
the two actual record exits give M_r>(17/100)d_r+(9/20)d_(r-1)^2/d_r.
Since 2sum_(r<=k)d_r L_r=L_k^2+sum_(r<=k)d_r^2, it follows that

 H2_k <L_k^2+(83/100)d_k^2
          +(19/50)sum_(r<k)d_r^2+(9/20)sum_(r<=4)d_r^2.

For k>=7, L_k/d_k<869/500: use d_7>198 and
(k-1+gamma/(c-1))/d_k<(6+1/50)/198 together with
c/(c-1)<1+99/140. Also sum_(r<=j)d_r^2<(7/5)d_j^2 for j>=3,
d_(k-1)/d_k<1/2 and d_4/d_k<1/8. Hence

 H2_k/d_k^2
 <(869/500)^2+83/100+(19/50)(7/5)/4+(9/20)(7/5)/64
 =4-26049/4000000<4                         (k>=7).       (40)

We also need two refinements of (30). For every k>=3,

                         T_k/d_k<2+sqrt(2)<683/200.       (41)

Indeed, (c-1)/(c-2)=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon/[(lambda-2)(c-2)],
where the coefficient of epsilon exceeds13. The positive clock correction
is below(325/16)epsilon*(5/6)^k, whose coefficient is less than13 for
k>=3. Drop the negative term -(c-1)/[(c-2)d_k] to prove (41).
For the lower clock bound, c<487/200 and d_7>198 give

 T_k/d_k>(287/87)(197/198)>82/25               (k>=7).

For k=6 we only need T_6/d_6>13/4; this follows from d_6>83 and
(287/87)(82/83)>13/4. To check d_6>83, the function
(1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^5 increases on[0,1/128] and
1+lambda^5=42+29sqrt(2)>83.

The squared-block bound (31) holds already for k>=8. One fully rational
check uses a_0=81/200<a, c_0=1207/500<c and rho_0=207/500<c-2:

 sum_(h in H_(k-1))h^2/d_k^2
 <1/[c_0^2(c_0^2-2)]
       +2/[a_0*rho_0*c_0^(k+1)]
       +(2/c_0^2)^k/(2a_0^2)<1/20             (k>=8).       (42)

The last two terms decrease in k, and at k=8 their sum with the first
is less than1/20 by direct rational multiplication. At k=7 the earlier
coarser three-term bound is less than1/16. Also
d_(k-1)/d_k<3/7 for k>=6: use d_5>34 and
d_k/d_(k-1)>241/100-23/(16*34)>7/3.

Repeating the actual-last-block integral from (32), with (41)-(42), gives

 W_k/d_k^2
 >(17/100){(29/300)[(57/25)^3-(683/480)^3]-483/4000}
 >1/8                                                   (k>=8),

 W_7/d_7^2
 >(17/100){(29/300)[(57/25)^3-(3/2)^3]-483/3200}
 >1/10.                                                  (43)

For the second line, T_6/d_7<(683/200)(3/7)<3/2 and the squared-block
bound is1/16. The first rational margin over1/8 is
788900126789/414720000000000; the second margin over1/10 is
68258881/5000000000. Both are positive.

Simple consequences of the exact L_k formula and the lower d_k bound are

 L_k/d_k^2<9/1000, L_k/d_k<869/500              (k>=7);
 L_k/d_k^2<1/250, L_k/d_k<69/40, d_k>400       (k>=8);
 L_k/d_k<343/200, d_k>1000                    (k>=9).

For each statement, the correction (k-1+1/50)/[(2/5)(12/5)^k]
decreases in k; substitution of k=8 or9 proves the latter two lines.
Equations (34), (39)-(41), with these bounds, now imply

 W_k/d_k^2
 <28/23+1/250-(17/100)(82/25)
                    -(9/20)(82/25)(16/39)^3<9/16        (k>=8),

 W_7/d_7^2
 <28/23+9/1000-(17/100)(82/25)
                    -(9/20)(13/4)(16/39)^3<3/5.           (44)

Thus W_k-W_(k-1)>0 for every k>=8: at k=8 it exceeds
[1/8-(3/5)(5/12)^2]d_8^2=d_8^2/48, and for k>=9 it exceeds
(7/256)d_k^2. Formula (11c) and its positive baseline prove the lower
load bound at every k>=8.

For the upper bound at k>=9, repeat (37) with its first term343/100
and with the predecessor estimate28/23+1/250-1/8. The resulting bound is

 ellhat_k/d_k
 <343/100+1/1000-17/100-(9/20)(16/39)^2
   -(17/100)(82/25)-(9/20)(82/25)(16/39)^3
   +(28/23+1/250-1/8)(5/12)^2
 =11/4-1233808367/36382320000<11/4.                        (45)

At k=8, use L_8/d_8<69/40, 1/d_8<1/400 and
N_7/d_7^2<28/23+9/1000-1/10. The same calculation gives

 ellhat_8/d_8
 <69/20+1/400-17/100-(9/20)(16/39)^2
   -(17/100)(82/25)-(9/20)(82/25)(16/39)^3
   +(28/23+9/1000-1/10)(5/12)^2
 =11/4-262097237/36382320000<11/4.                         (46)

This proves (38). Only the five load rows3,4,5,6,7 are now outstanding.
All additional arithmetic checked only these displayed rational proof
constants; no chronological kernel was evaluated.

### 11.6 An explicit uniform proof for load row3

The first of those five rows can also be handled analytically:

                    2<ellhat_3/d_3<68/25<11/4.            (47)

Put t=d_0, u=d_1, d=d_2, e=d_3, A=1-t/e, B=1-u/e, and
C=2t+u+d. Before the first e, the nonzero state3 probabilities, starting
at query3, are respectively

 t/d, A, A^2, A^2 B, A^3 B.

The transition at step d gives B_4,3=1-t/e=A; after that transition
state2 is zero and the remaining physical steps are t,u,t,e. Thus the
probabilities of exiting state3 at these five consecutive steps are

 t/e, (t/e)A, (u/e)A^2, (t/e)A^2B, A^3B,

and their exit clocks are C,C+t,C+t+u,C+2t+u,C+2t+u+e.
This explicitly derives the following positive expression, without a
divided-difference subtraction:

 M_3/e+N_3/e^2=F(t,u,d,e,A,B;e),

 F(t,u,d,e,A,B;z)
 =[td(e+C)+t^2 A(e+C+t)+u^2 A^2(e+C+t+u)
                         +t^2 A^2 B(e+C+2t+u)]/z^3
      +A^3 B[1+(C+2t+u+e)/z],
 C=2t+u+d.                                                (48)

Every coefficient in F is nonnegative. The elementary parameter bounds
from Section5.1 and the preceding boundary proof give the following
UNIFORM rectangle for the entire interval0<epsilon<=1/128:

 1407/1000<t<283/200, 249/125<u<2,
 1707/500<d<683/200, 1707/250<e<55/8,
 99/125<A<199/250, 707/1000<B<711/1000.                    (49)

For d's sharper lower bound, use
d=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2>1707/500. For e use
e=1+lambda^2(1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3); this lies in the stated
interval by the same rational bounds on sqrt(2). The A,B bounds then
follow by division of the positive t,u,e endpoints.

Let F_minus be F with all six numerator variables at their lower
endpoints and z=55/8. Let F_plus use all upper numerator endpoints and
z=1707/250. Direct rational multiplication gives

 F_minus-37/20=5685525016957627/461578369140625000>0,
 2-F_plus=10672899727081973627/138165006750000000000>0.

Thus 37/20<M_3/e+N_3/e^2<2. At row2, writing rho_2=1-t/d<147/250,
the two possible exit steps give the exact formula

 N_2=t^2(2t+u)/d+d*rho_2*(2t+u+d).

Substitution of the same positive upper numerator and lower denominator
bounds gives N_2/e^2<21/50, with rational margin
5469997561/1326384064800. Also (49) gives

 4<2L_3/e+1/e<83/20:

the margins in these two inequalities are at least876/6875 and
101/34140, respectively. Finally use (11) in the grouped form

 ellhat_3/e=2L_3/e+1/e-(M_3/e+N_3/e^2)+N_2/e^2.

Its lower bound is greater than4-2=2, and its upper bound is less than
83/20-37/20+21/50=68/25. This proves (47) for every original m>=128.
Only the four load rows4,5,6,7 remain unproved. These are comparisons of
an explicitly written positive expression over the whole parameter
interval; no fixed-horizon kernel or parameter grid was evaluated.

## 12. Why the moment proof does not automatically prove the signed margins

The new argument settles linear constant loads, but a termwise positive
quadratic-storage proof is impossible even in the original word. For every
k>=3, consider only its actual first physical step h=d_k. State k-1 is
already zero, state k has probability pi_k>17/100, and it is killed by
this step. The coefficient of ||y_k||^2 in this SINGLE summand of (1) is
therefore exactly

 [T_k+d_k(1-d_k)]pi_k^2/d_k^2
     =pi_k^2[-1+(T_k/d_k+1)/d_k].                         (50)

By (41), T_k/d_k+1<883/200. Also d_k>=d_3>34/5. Since
883/1360<2/3, (50) is strictly smaller than

                   -pi_k^2/3<-289/30000.                 (51)

This is an exact original-word counterexample to requiring nonnegative
diagonal cost at EACH physical step. It does NOT contradict any proposed
inequality for the accumulated E, b, e, or u. It identifies the signed
cancellation still needed: positive contributions from preceding lower
steps must pay these negative killing-step diagonals. The successful
single-exit moments do not bound those pair products by themselves.

The two outstanding direct routes are consequently still distinct:
(a) record-state products and actual last-block evolution, now effective
for loads, need joint product estimates for b and u; (b) paired K/T
decimation with the retained clock needs a signed coefficient estimate
after the stable inverse. Neither route has supplied those estimates.
No counterexample to a REQUIRED accumulated band inequality was found.

## 13. Scope and remaining work

The new proved-in-draft part of the stated interface is e_i>=0 for
every required index. The stronger entrywise result (7), the identities
(9), (11), and (13), the uniform decimation estimates (17), and the
time localization and stable finite inverse (18)-(22), the row2 load
bound (11b), and the uniform surviving-mass bound (23) have explicit
proofs above.

For a fixed original m, the inherited-boundary identity only requires
e_i>=0, u_i>=0 and b_i>=2/15 at 0<=i<=m-1, the stronger e_i>=1/15
and u_i>=1/16 at the SINGLE index i=m-1, and the load bounds at
2<=i<=m-1. The early formulas already handle b_0,b_1,b_2 and
u_0,u_1,u_2; they are recorded in the preceding boundary draft and
are not replaced here by a positive margin independent of epsilon.
Uniform claims at all i>=127 with fixed epsilon would be stronger
than the quantitative terminal bounds actually used at i=m-1.

After (8), (11b), (38), and (47), the unresolved claims remain b_i>=2/15 and
u_i>=0 for 3<=i<=m-1, the stronger e_(m-1)>=1/15 and
u_(m-1)>=1/16, and the load bounds for 4<=i<=7.
This document does not prove the full
kernel positive semidefinite, and it is not a counterexample to those
remaining actual-word claims. Even their proof would only concern the
specified reciprocal-level temporal quotient, not arbitrary legal GD.

The existing same-multiset foil is still a useful warning: its first
few steps also satisfy h_i<s_i+2, and entrywise off-diagonal positivity
alone does not distinguish its negative diagonal-margin behavior.
Thus (7) must not be promoted into a substitute for the original-order
estimates. The exact pair identity (13), unlike that coarse envelope,
does distinguish the original word.

# A uniform terminal-charge reduction for the chronological birth kernel

Status: **unverified proof draft**, fifth launch, second and final allocated
round. This file gives exact horizon truncation identities, an exact formula
for the constant column, and a proved conditional terminal-charge estimate.
It does **not** prove the remaining coefficient hypotheses. No fact submission
is authorized, so none of the new assertions is a fact-graph building block.

The underlying problem is still the original exact-profile minimax problem.
Even positivity of this entire kernel would only exclude its specified
reciprocal-level temporal family, not resolve the original problem.

## 1. Definitions, with the same original chronological word

Fix m>=128, epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c, and d_k=1+a*c^k.
For this file extend the formula for d_k to every integer k>=0, keeping the
SAME epsilon. The extension is a device for writing coefficients; no new
steps are added to the original trajectory or interpolation constraints.

Let H_0 be empty and H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). The queried word is H_m with
its final d_0 removed, with q=2^m-2 steps h_i, elapsed times
s_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and S=s_q. For vectors v,w_0,...,w_(m-1), define

 p_ij=product_(r<i)(1-h_r/d_j),
 g_i=v+sum_j p_ij*w_j,  x_i=x_0-sum_(r<i)h_r*g_r.

All vectors use one arbitrary common real Gram matrix. The first physical
d_j kills its modal column, so g_q=v and
x_q=x_0-S*v-sum_j d_j*w_j.

For one common value array put
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, including the zero optimum jet.
Necessity of Q_ij<=0, with this orientation, follows from the accepted
fact1252b8823bb5a290. Define

 J=sum_(i<q){2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2}.

The exact identity is

 ||x_0||^2-2S f_q+2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)
          +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J.             (1)

To check it, the optimum terms sum to
2sum h_i f_i-||x_0||^2+||x_q||^2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2;
summation by parts in the chain terms gives
2S f_q-2sum h_i f_i plus the other two terms in J. This also shows that
all selected Q multipliers are actual nonnegative original constraints.

Define phi_k(z)=product_(r<k)(1-d_r*z),
V_k=v+sum_j phi_k(1/d_j)w_j, U_k=V_k-v, and y_k=d_k U_k.
For k=m put U_m=0. Set z=(S+1)v.
The triangular evaluation matrix has nonzero positive diagonal, so this
change of coordinates is invertible on the residual vectors.

There is a birth array B_ik, initially B_00=1, all other B_0k=0, with

 B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),          (2)

where the second summand is zero at k=0. It gives g_i=sum_k B_ik V_k.
This follows from
(1-hz)phi_k=(1-h/d_k)phi_k+(h/d_k)phi_(k+1).
On the infinite ruler word, an occupied state k always has d_k>=h_i:
at the first d_j all states below j have already vanished, and the
transition at d_j kills state j. Consequently all occupied transition
probabilities are nonnegative, sum_k B_ik=1, and

 B_ik=0 if k>i or i>=2^k.                                  (3)

The first assertion follows from advancing at most one state per step.
For the second, the first d_k is step2^k-1, with all smaller states already
zero, and its transition moves every remaining mass out of state k.

## 2. A row-finite infinite matrix and the exact constant-column identity

Run (2) formally on the infinite ruler word with the same d_k. In each
individual summand defining J put g_i=sum_k (B_ik/d_k)y_k, with no v.
Define E_kl to be its accumulated symmetric quadratic coefficient.
This is well-defined without summing an infinite numerical series: if a
term contains y_k, (3) forces its step index i<=2^k-1.
Also no state larger than i+1 can occur in that term. Therefore

 E_kl=0 when k<l and l>2^k.                                (4)

Every row has finite support. For 0<=k,l<m, E_kl is EXACTLY the residual
matrix entry for the original queried word, since all steps that can
contribute have already occurred before the last deleted d_0.

Write the actual form as

 J=sum_(k,l<m)E_kl<y_k,y_l>
       +2sum_(k<m)ell_k<y_k,z>+D||z||^2,
 D=S/(S+1).

Then its unnormalized constant load is

 ellhat_k:=(S+1)ell_k=sum_(l>=0)E_kl*d_l.                   (5)

In particular ellhat_k, like each E_kl, is independent of the enclosing
depth once the physical parameters epsilon,c,a have been fixed.

Proof of (5). In the extended birth system set every y_l=d_l*v. The
resulting g_i equals v because sum_l B_il=1. Polarizing against the single
coordinate y_k therefore gives the actual cross coefficient with the
constant gradient. Only finitely many steps and finitely many l can
contribute to that coefficient, by (3)-(4), so this substitution requires
no interchange of infinite sums. After restoring z=(S+1)v the identity
is precisely (5). Transient coefficients B_ik for k<m are unaffected by
the extension, and setting V_l=v at every l>=m recovers the original
absorbing state. Thus no artificial gradient or interpolation condition
has entered the actual data.

As direct checks, put t=d_0,u=d_1. The first two steps give

 E_00=(2-t)/t, E_01=(t-1)/u,
 E_11=[2t+u(2-u)]/u^2,
 E_12=(t+u)(u-1)/(u*d_2),
 ellhat_0=1, ellhat_1=t^2/u+t+1.                            (6)

For example, E_00*d_0+E_01*d_1=(2-t)+(t-1)=1, agreeing
with the first constant load. The second row likewise gives (6).

## 3. The exact effect of a right boundary

All definitions in this section use the infinite coefficient array (4).
Set an entry with a negative index to zero, and define

 A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),
 b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),
 tau_i^- =sum_(0<=l<=i-3)|E_il|,
 tau_i^+ =sum_(l>=i+3)|E_il|,
 t_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)-tau_i^-.                     (7)

The letter t_i in (7) is a diagonal margin, distinct from the isolated
scalar t=d_0 in (6). Both tail sums are finite by (4).

For the finite m-by-m residual matrix, extract second-neighbor triple
squares and adjacent pair squares using

 e_i^[m]=E_(i,i+2) (0<=i<=m-3),
 b_i^[m]=E_(i,i+1)-2(e_(i-1)^[m]+e_i^[m]),
 r_i^[m]=E_ii-E_(i-1,i)-E_(i,i+1)+e_(i-2)^[m]+e_i^[m]
                   -sum_(0<=l<m,|l-i|>=3)|E_il|.            (8)

Out-of-range entries in the FINITE definitions (8) are zero. Substitution
into (7) gives the exact formulas

 b_i^[m]=b_i                         (0<=i<=m-3),
 b_(m-2)^[m]=b_(m-2)+2e_(m-2),

 r_i^[m]=t_i+e_i-sum_(i+3<=l<m)|E_il| (0<=i<=m-3),
 r_(m-2)^[m]=t_(m-2),
 r_(m-1)^[m]=t_(m-1)+A_(m-1).                              (9)

Thus a proof of the infinite bulk remainder alone can miss the entire
e_(m-2) term at the penultimate row. Formula (9) retains that charge.

In particular, the following bounds imply all needed finite residual
square signs and uniform lower bounds for the denominators used below:

 e_i>=0, tau_i^+<=e_i, t_i>=0,
 b_i>=2/15, and A_i>=2/5 for i>=2.                          (10)

Under (10), e_i^[m]>=0, b_i^[m]>=2/15, r_i^[m]>=0, and
r_(m-1)^[m]>=2/5. These are direct consequences of (9).
No claim that (10) has been proved for the actual whole family is made.

## 4. A complete conditional bound on the terminal charge

Here is a sufficient condition using the actual known array, with no
pseudoinverse or eigenvalue hypothesis. In addition to (10), suppose

           2d_i<=ellhat_i<=(11/4)d_i for every i>=2.         (11)

Only the indices needed in (9) and below are necessary; uniform bounds on
the infinite arrays are a convenient stronger formulation.
Then the COMPLETE local-square constant remainder satisfies

                         Delta>1/100                       (12)

for every actual m>=128. This is a proved implication, not a proof of
its hypotheses (10)-(11).

First define alpha_(-1)=0, alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_(i-1), and

 C_charge=sum_(i<m-1)alpha_i^2/b_i^[m]
                    +alpha_(m-1)^2/r_(m-1)^[m],
 Delta=D-C_charge.                                         (13)

The exact full square identity is

 J=sum_(i<m-1)r_i^[m]||y_i||^2
   +r_(m-1)^[m]||y_(m-1)+(alpha_(m-1)/r_(m-1)^[m])z||^2
   +sum_(i<m-1)b_i^[m]||y_i+y_(i+1)+(alpha_i/b_i^[m])z||^2
   +sum_(i<m-2)e_i||y_i+2y_(i+1)+y_(i+2)||^2
   +sum_(k<l<m,l-k>=3)|E_kl| ||y_k+sgn(E_kl)y_l||^2
   +Delta||z||^2.                                         (14)

Indeed the triple squares supply the distance-two entries and twice their
weights to each adjacent entry. The pair squares then supply exactly
b_i^[m], and the long-range squares supply precisely their signed entries.
Their diagonal cost is the one subtracted in (8). Finally
ell_i=alpha_i+alpha_(i-1) proves the constant cross terms, and the completed
squares spend exactly C_charge. Define a zero-weight signed square as zero.

We now bound (13). The actual parameters satisfy

 2<c<39/16, a>2/5, 7/5<d_0<3/2,
 199/100<d_1<2, d_2>3, and d_i>2d_(i-1) for i>=3.          (15)

For c<39/16 use lambda<29/12, whence
c<(29/12)(129/128)=3741/1536<39/16.
For a>2/5 use a>(127/128)/(39/16)=127/312>2/5.
The remaining first-level inequalities follow directly. For doubling,

 c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2
       >1+6epsilon,

so (c-2)a*c^2=(1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.
Hence d_3>2d_2, and the inequality persists at every larger index.

Equations (6),(15) give 1=ellhat_0<ellhat_1<4, whereas
ellhat_2>=2d_2>6. For i>=3, (11),(15) give
ellhat_i>=2d_i>4d_(i-1)>(11/4)d_(i-1)>=ellhat_(i-1).
Thus the loads are positive and increasing. Also the upper bound
ellhat_i<=(11/4)d_i holds at i=0,1 by (6),(15).
Induction in alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_(i-1) now proves

                    0<=alpha_i<=ell_i.

Consequently (10) implies

 C_charge <= (121/16)/(S+1)^2
       *[(15/2)sum_(i=0)^(m-2)d_i^2+(5/2)d_(m-1)^2].       (16)

The actual, unreset elapsed clock obeys

 S+1=a*c^m/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^m-d_0
     >a*c^m/(c-2).                                       (17)

For the strict inequality, c(c-2)<2 and
c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)=[2lambda(lambda-1)+1]epsilon
                        +lambda^2 epsilon^2>7epsilon.
Thus 1-a/(c-2)>3epsilon, so its 2^m contribution exceeds d_0.

Expanding d_i^2=(1+a*c^i)^2, summing the geometric terms and using
c-2<1/2,a>2/5,c>2 gives

 sum_(i<m-1)d_i^2/(S+1)^2
    <=(c-2)^2/[c^2(c^2-1)]+2^(1-m),
 d_(m-1)^2/(S+1)^2 <=(c-2)^2/c^2+2^(-m).                 (18)

Here the cross term in the first line is at most
[2(c-2)^2/(a(c-1))]c^(-m-1)<2^(-m), and its constant term is
at most (25/16)m*2^(-2m)<2^(-m). The two lower-order terms in
the second line sum to less than 2^(-m), by the same bounds.

Therefore (16) is bounded above by F(c)+2^(-120), where

 F(c)=(121/16)(c-2)^2/c^2 *[15/(2(c^2-1))+5/2].           (19)

For c>2 this F is increasing: after removing positive factors the
derivative sign is that of
4(c^2+2)(c^2-1)-6c^2(c-2)
=2c^3(2c-3)+16c^2-8>0.
Exact rational arithmetic gives

 F(39/16)=1095787/1119456,
 49/50-F(39/16)=31997/27986400>1/1000.

It follows that C_charge<49/50. Meanwhile (17) gives
D=1-1/(S+1)>1-2^(1-m)>999/1000.
Thus Delta>19/1000>1/100, proving (12).

The additional original-space term in (1) is
||x_0-S*z/(S+1)-sum_j d_j*w_j||^2. Its coordinate change has
unit triangular determinant. Thus (14) together with this square retains
the full original x_0 coordinate and every constant-column range condition.

## 5. An exact inverse formula for attacking the remaining scalar bounds

Set xi_j=d_j*w_j. The triangular birth transformation is y=R*xi with
R_kj=(d_k/d_j)phi_k(1/d_j) for j>=k, and zero otherwise.
For 0<=j<=k its inverse has the closed formula

 (R^(-1))_jk = product_(0<=r<=k,r!=j)(1-d_r/d_j)^(-1).       (20)

One proof uses Newton interpolation at theta_j=1/d_j. The coefficients
c_j=[product_(r!=j)(theta_j-theta_r)]^(-1) annihilate every polynomial
of degree less than k and give its leading coefficient at degree k:
this follows by comparing the degree-k coefficient in the Lagrange
interpolation formula. Apply it to phi_l, 0<=l<=k, whose leading
coefficient at l=k is (-1)^k product_(r<k)d_r. This gives the inverse
of the evaluation matrix, and multiplying row j by d_j and column k
by1/d_k yields (20). The formula is also valid at k=0 by empty products.

For the ACTUAL affine-geometric levels, let q_c=1/c and
P_r=product_(s=1)^r(1-q_c^s), with P_0=1. Then (20) becomes

 (R^(-1))_jk
  =(-1)^(k-j) q_c^((k-j)(k-j+1)/2)
       *(d_j/(d_j-1))^k/(P_j P_(k-j)).                     (21)

This is exact, not a replacement of d_j by a*c^j. To see it, factor
1-d_r/d_j=[(d_j-1)/d_j](1-c^(r-j)) in each of the k factors
in (20), then split r<j and r>j. In particular, the alternating sign
and the fast decay with distance k-j have explicit formulas.

For later error estimates, P_r>1/4 uniformly. Indeed q_c<5/12, and
product_(s=2)^r(1-q_c^s)>=1-sum_(s=2)^r q_c^s
>=1-q_c^2/(1-q_c)>59/84. Multiplying by 1-q_c>7/12 gives
P_r>413/1008>1/4 for r>=1 (P_0=1). Therefore

 |(R^(-1))_jk| <16 q_c^((k-j)(k-j+1)/2)
                     *exp(k/(a*c^j)).                    (22)

The last step uses (1+1/(a*c^j))^k<=exp(k/(a*c^j)). The exponential
factor cannot be dropped uniformly at small j; it is the exact effect
of the additive1 in the physical levels. Formula (22) is a coefficient
estimate, not yet a bound on all entries of the transformed kernel.

## 6. What remains unproved, and failed transfers

For the factor allocation in Section4, the outstanding work is to prove
(10)-(11). In that allocation, the one-sided margin t_i>=0 is required
at the actual penultimate row; proving only the infinite interior remainder
t_i+e_i-tau_i^+>=0 does not establish it. Section7 below gives a different
allocation that removes this particular requirement.

The earlier exact boundary calculation already showed that the first
nontrivial such margin tends to zero with epsilon: t_1=r_1 is asymptotic
to (3sqrt(2)-1)epsilon/2. Thus there can be no positive uniform lower
bound for ALL t_i. This defeats a direct compactness argument based on
the smallest finite-horizon margin. It does not refute t_i>=0.

The new row-sum identity (5) does not imply (11) from positivity or total
nonnegativity alone: it contains the nonconstant weights d_l, including
future levels. Likewise the inverse formula (21) has alternating terms;
positive node factors do not turn its congruence into an entrywise bound.
The actual legal negative-swap example06fc37ec32b366f7 remains a regression
against assigning every local chronological swap a positive sign.

No additional direct kernel case was run in this round. Reading retained
matrix entries and one trivial exact rational evaluation of (19) did not
assemble or evaluate a new kernel. The public ledger remains at8/12 cases
and53.087538152998604/120 measured seconds. No optimization, installation,
fact submission, new launch, or renewed numerical allowance is inferred.

## 7. A better right-boundary allocation: keep the shortened triples

There is no need to force the t_i sign in (10). Instead define the full
interior remainder

 u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i-tau_i^--tau_i^+
    =t_i+e_i-tau_i^+.                                     (23)

Assume the following explicit bounds on the same extended actual array:

 e_i>=0, u_i>=0, b_i>=2/15 for all i>=0;
 u_i>=1/16 and e_i>=1/15 for i>=127;
 2d_i<=ellhat_i<=(11/4)d_i for i>=2.                        (24)

These bounds remain UNPROVED. Under (24), however, a different complete
square identity yields a uniform remainder

                         Delta_new>1/200.                 (25)

Here is the identity and its proof. For 0<=i<m set

 R_i=u_i+sum_(l>=m,l-i>=3)|E_il|
               +1_(i=m-1)(b_(m-1)+e_(m-1)).               (26)

Then the finite residual form has the exact decomposition

 sum_(k,l<m)E_kl<y_k,y_l>
  =sum_(i<m)R_i||y_i||^2
   +sum_(i<m-1)b_i||y_i+y_(i+1)||^2
   +sum_(i<m-2)e_i||y_i+2y_(i+1)+y_(i+2)||^2
   +e_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2
   +sum_(k<l<m,l-k>=3)|E_kl| ||y_k+sgn(E_kl)y_l||^2.        (27)

To prove it, first expand the infinite pair/triple/far decomposition with
diagonal remainder u_i from (23), and then set y_i=0 for i>=m. Every
row is finite, so only finitely many summands survive. The triple starting
at m-2 becomes the SHORTENED square displayed in (27); it is not removed.
The triple starting at m-1 contributes e_(m-1)||y_(m-1)||^2, and the
pair starting there contributes b_(m-1)||y_(m-1)||^2. Each far edge
crossing the boundary contributes its absolute weight to the lower-index
diagonal. These are precisely (26). The identity can also be checked
entry by entry, without positing any sign of u_i beforehand.

Thus (24) gives R_i>=0 and, since m-1>=127,

 R_(m-1)>=1/16+2/15+1/15=21/80.                            (28)

Keep the same exact alternating load alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_(i-1).
Complete the pair squares and the final diagonal in (27), leaving the
shortened triple unloaded. The resulting FULL J identity is (27) with

 b_i||y_i+y_(i+1)||^2 replaced by
 b_i||y_i+y_(i+1)+(alpha_i/b_i)z||^2,

 R_(m-1)||y_(m-1)||^2 replaced by
 R_(m-1)||y_(m-1)+(alpha_(m-1)/R_(m-1))z||^2,

 and the additional term Delta_new||z||^2, where

 C_new=sum_(i<m-1)alpha_i^2/b_i+alpha_(m-1)^2/R_(m-1),
 Delta_new=D-C_new.                                       (29)

As in (14), ell_i=alpha_i+alpha_(i-1) checks every constant cross term.
The original x_0 square from (1) is retained. This provides the complete
constant-column condition; no range information is suppressed.

A sharper estimate on alpha pays the smaller final diagonal (28).
First, d_j/d_(j-1)>12/5 for every j>=8. In fact
d_7=1+(1-epsilon)c^6>=1+lambda^6>198:
the function (1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^6 increases on [0,1/128].
Using lambda>241/100 and c-1<23/16 gives

 d_j/d_(j-1)=c-(c-1)/d_(j-1)
       >241/100-23/3168>12/5.

The positivity and monotonicity argument before (16) uses only the load
bounds, so 0<=alpha_i<=ell_i still holds. For i>=9,

 (S+1)alpha_i
  <=ellhat_i-ellhat_(i-1)+ellhat_(i-2)
  <=(11/4)d_i-2d_(i-1)+(11/4)d_(i-2)
  <=(11/4)d_i-(41/48)d_(i-1)
  <[11/4-41/(48c)]d_i
  <(1123/468)d_i<(12/5)d_i.                               (30)

The first inequality uses alpha_(i-2)<=ell_(i-2). The fourth uses
d_i<c*d_(i-1). The last uses c<39/16 and
12/5-1123/468=1/2340>0. This gain explicitly uses the alternating load;
replacing alpha_i by ell_i would fail to obtain the following bound.

Equations (28)-(30) imply

 C_new <=(144/25)/(S+1)^2
       *[(15/2)sum_(i<m-1)d_i^2+(80/21)d_(m-1)^2]
       +2^(26-2m).                                       (31)

The correction covers all i<9: their entire charge is at most
(121/16)(15/2)*9*d_8^2/(S+1)^2<576*2^(16-2m)<2^(26-2m),
because d_8<2a*c^8 and (17) give d_8/(S+1)<2^(8-m).
It is harmless to add their full charge even though the first term in
(31) already gives them some budget.

Apply (18) to (31). The result is at most G(c)+2^(-119), where

 G(c)=(144/25)(c-2)^2/c^2 *[15/(2(c^2-1))+80/21].          (32)

This G increases on 2<c<5/2. For a function of the form
(c-2)^2/c^2 [A/(c^2-1)+B], with A=15/2 and B=80/21,
the derivative sign reduces to
4B(c^2-1)^2+2A[4c^2-c^3-2]>0 on this interval.
The latter bracket equals c^2(4-c)-2>4, proving the sign.
Exact rational values are

 G(39/16)=3170048/3206775,
 99/100-G(39/16)=18637/12827100>1/1000.

Therefore C_new<99/100. Since D>999/1000 by (17),
Delta_new>9/1000>1/200, as claimed.

This alternative is the preferred next interface: establish the bulk
inequalities (24), using the exact inverse and chronology if helpful.
It does NOT require the stronger one-sided penultimate margin, or any
new sign for local swaps. All hypothesized scalar bounds concern known
explicit coefficients, and are still the unsolved part of this route.

## 8. Unconditional uniform tail bounds from the birth paths

The infinitely extended rows in (24) can be reduced to a fixed width with
explicit error, without a numerical kernel evaluation or an extra sign
assumption. The following bounds hold for the ACTUAL parameters above.

For an integer r>=0 define

 p_r=min{1, (7/2)^r/[r! 2^(r(r-1)/2)]}.

For r>=1 put

 H_r=(77/4)p_r+(343/24)2^(-r)[(5/2)p_(r-1)+p_r].           (33)

Then for every k>=2 and r>=1,

 |E_(k,k+r)| <=(d_k/d_(k+r))H_r <=2^(-r)H_r.              (34)

Here is a direct path proof. All steps that contribute to row k occur
by the first physical d_k. Their total elapsed time is
T=S_(H_k)+d_k. The explicit word sum and a/(c-2)<1 give

 S_(H_k)=a*c^k/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^k-1<=c^k-1,
 T<(1+1/a)d_k<(7/2)d_k.                                  (35)

Every one of these steps is at most d_k. Once a birth path first reaches
state k, to reach state k+r by this time it must make r further births
on distinct increasing steps. Condition on its first arrival at k.
For any specified r birth steps the probability is at most their step
product divided by d_k*d_(k+1)*...*d_(k+r-1); the intervening stay
probabilities and any continuation probability are at most one.
The sum of these products is at most T^r/r!, because the expansion of
T^r contains every product of r distinct steps r! times and all other
terms are nonnegative. By (15), the denominator is at least
d_k^r*2^(r(r-1)/2). The resulting bound holds for every possible
arrival time, hence also without conditioning. It proves

       B_(i,k+r)<=p_r for every i<=2^k.                    (36)

This estimate applies to both ends of every step contributing to row k.

To bound the actual quadratic coefficient, write v_ij=B_ij/d_j for the
coefficient of y_j in g_i; this symbol v_ij is unrelated to the constant
gradient vector v. Equation (2) implies

 |v_(i+1),k-v_ik| <=h_i(c+1)/d_k^2,
 |v_(i+1),l-v_il| <=h_i(c*p_(r-1)+p_r)/d_l^2,
                    l=k+r,                               (37)

because every adjacent ratio d_j/d_(j-1) is less than c.
Also |v_ik|,|v_(i+1),k|<=1/d_k and
|v_il|,|v_(i+1),l|<=p_r/d_l.

Let M=7/2. The first and third terms of J therefore contribute in
absolute value at most

 [2sum_i s_(i+1)h_i+sum_i h_i^2]p_r/(d_k*d_l)
 <=[T^2+2d_k*T]p_r/(d_k*d_l)
 <=(M^2+2M)(d_k/d_l)p_r.                                 (38)

The middle term of J, using (37), contributes at most

 M^2(c+1)(d_k/d_l^2)[c*p_(r-1)+p_r],                       (39)

since sum_i s_(i+1)h_i^2<=T*sum_i h_i^2<=d_k*T^2.
Now d_l>=2^r*d_k>3*2^r, c+1<7/2, and c<5/2.
Combining (38)-(39) yields (33)-(34). This proof estimates absolute
values, retaining all signed cross terms; it assigns no sign to E itself.

The constants are small after r=6. For r>=7, each summand in H_(r+1)
is at most 1/256 of its corresponding summand in H_r. For the terms
p_r this follows from
p_(r+1)/p_r=(7/2)/[(r+1)2^r]<=7/2048<1/256;
for 2^(-r)p_(r-1) the ratio is (7/2)/(r*2^r)<=1/256.
All p involved here are below one, so the displayed exact ratios apply.
Consequently exact rational arithmetic gives

 sum_(r>=7)H_r <=(256/255)H_7
   =6636933037/197138998886400 <1/25000.                   (40)

Symmetry and (34) now show, for EVERY k>=2,

 sum_(|l-k|>=7)|E_kl| <1/1000000,                         (41)

 sum_(|l-k|>=7)|E_kl|*(d_l/d_k) <1/20000.                 (42)

For a right entry, (34) supplies respectively 2^(-r)H_r and H_r.
For a left entry l=k-r>=2, use (34) with base l; the corresponding
bounds are 2^(-r)H_r and 2^(-2r)H_r. Any distant entry with l=0 or1
is exactly zero by (4). Thus the two tails in (41) total at most
2^(-6)sum_(r>=7)H_r<1/1600000<1/1000000. The tails in (42)
total at most (1+2^(-14))sum_(r>=7)H_r<1/20000.

For a concrete finite-width reformulation, define

 u_i^[6]=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i
                         -sum_(3<=|l-i|<=6)|E_il|,
 L_i^[6]=sum_(|l-i|<=6)E_il*d_l/d_i,

where all indices l are nonnegative. Then for i>=2,

 u_i^[6]-1/1000000 < u_i <=u_i^[6],
 |ellhat_i/d_i-L_i^[6]|<1/20000.                           (43)

The first two rows have exact finite support by (4). Thus proving the
remaining sufficient conditions (24) does not require an uncontrolled
infinite tail or any exponentially long trajectory enumeration: a fixed
thirteen-entry band, together with (43), contains the missing estimates.
The row index and epsilon still vary, and uniform estimates for those
band entries are NOT established here. Equations (34),(41)-(43) are actual
unconditional mathematical claims with proofs in this draft; they remain
unverified because this launch has no fact_submit allowance.

## 9. Why the tail theorem does not decide the near-diagonal signs

The older same-multiset foil also satisfies the hypotheses used in the
tail proof. This gives a concrete failure test for promoting locality to
positive semidefiniteness. Keep the actual d_k and exchange only the third
and fourth steps, changing (t,u,t,d_2,...) to (t,u,d_2,t,...).
For k=2 its killing step and elapsed time move earlier; for every k>=3
the prefix through the first d_k has unchanged total time and maximum
step. The k=0,1 killing times are unchanged. Thus extinction still occurs
by2^k and the entire path proof (35)-(42) continues to apply.

Nevertheless choose v=0, V_0=0,V_1=1,V_2=-1, and V_j=0 for j>=3.
The first three transitions in this permuted word are pure births,
so its scalar gradients are exactly (0,1,-1,0,0,...).
This is a genuine rank-one formal Gram in the same invertible birth
coordinates, with y_1=u,y_2=-d_2 and all other y zero. Its quadratic is

 J=t+[4(t+u)-2u(t+u)+u(1-u)]+[t+u+2d_2-d_2^2]
  =6t+6u-3u^2-2tu+2d_2-d_2^2.

Writing tau=sqrt(2), the actual values are
t=tau-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], u=2-epsilon,
d_2=tau+2-lambda epsilon^2. The coefficient of t is6-2u>0.
Replacing t by its upper bound tau and expanding the remaining expression
therefore gives

 J<=-2+(6+2tau)epsilon-3epsilon^2
                    +2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^4
   <-2+9epsilon+12epsilon^2<-19/10.

The last inequality uses 9/128+12/128^2<1/10.
This rederives, for the present tail comparison, the formal obstruction
previously recorded as501aba74c8acfcdf. It is NOT a negative direction
of the ORIGINAL chronological kernel. It is also NOT legal interpolation
data: g_0=0 forces x_1=x_0, while g_1=1, so
Q_01+Q_10=||g_0-g_1||^2=1 for every value array.

Hence the new unconditional tail inequalities leave a real mathematical
obligation at the near-diagonal band; they cannot settle its sign merely
by retaining the level multiset, birth support and clock upper bounds.
The original lower-step repeats must enter those remaining estimates.

## 10. Source check and exact finite cancellation

The main-agent pointer research/SOURCE_Q_PRODUCTS_20260927.md and the
primary NIST DLMF17.2 and17.5 pages were read. DLMF17.2.35 gives the finite
q-binomial identity, while17.2.37 is an infinite series with |z|<1.
These are optional algebraic pointers, not premises of any proof above;
no external theorem has been imported as a fact. The targeted Matlas
search for the birth-tail bound returned other Markov-process statements,
not an applicable theorem. Section8 supplies its own finite path proof.

One exact cancellation that already follows from (20), without an external
identity, is sum_(j=0)^k(R^(-1))_jk=1. In fact
(R^(-1))_jk=d_j^k/product_(r<=k,r!=j)(d_j-d_r), and Lagrange
interpolation of X^k shows that these leading coefficients sum to1.
Thus sum_j xi_j=sum_k y_k exactly, so the original final-position square
can also be written ||x_0-S*v-sum_k y_k||^2. This retains the additive1
in every node and all alternating signs of the inverse; an absolute-value
estimate of (21) would lose this cancellation.

No unproved q-series positivity or infinite limit is used in this file.
The outstanding estimate is still uniform control of the band entries,
in both the row index and actual enclosing-horizon parameter.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002572 -->
# Complex polynomial transcripts and interpolation cycles

Status: exploratory derivation, not a verifier-accepted fact. This does not resolve the exact Zhang--Jiang profile comparison. Normalize L=1, x_*=0, f_*=0 and ||x_0||=1.

## 1. Counterexample to local curvature lifting

Take h_0=1 and x_0=(1,0), g_0=(1/2,1/2), x_1=(1/2,-1/2), g_1=(1/2,0), x_*=g_*=0.
The GD equation holds. Every pair satisfies <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j>=||g_i-g_j||^2.
Also g_0=P_0 x_0 and g_1=P_1 x_1, where P_0 projects onto span((1,1)) and P_1 projects onto the first axis. Both are symmetric PSD contractions.
Nevertheless, smooth-convex interpolation against the optimum forces f_0=1/4 and f_1=1/8. The inequality from point 1 to point 0 requires f_0-f_1>=3/8, a contradiction. The three-cycle * -> 1 -> 0 -> * has positive total lower-increment weight 1/4.

## 2. The schedule-specific algebraic object

Fix n>=1 and any real nonnegative schedule h_0,...,h_{n-1}. Define

    P_0(z)=1, P_t(z)=product_{s=0}^{t-1}(1-h_s z), 1<=t<=n.

For z=a+ib identify C with R^2 and set x_t=P_t(z), g_t=z P_t(z), x_*=g_*=0.
Multiplication by z is the real matrix A_z=[[a,-b],[b,a]]. It need not be a gradient map globally. Only the finite sampled transcript is proposed for interpolation. GD holds exactly, with the original ordered real schedule. Put c=a-a^2-b^2.

For every ordered pair in I={*,0,...,n}, define the edge weight from j to i by

    q_ij = <g_j,x_i-x_j> + (1/2)||g_i-g_j||^2
         = (a/2)(|x_i|^2-|x_j|^2)
           + b Im(conj(x_j) x_i) - (c/2)|x_i-x_j|^2.

For a directed simple cycle C=(i_0,...,i_{r-1},i_r=i_0), put

    A_C = sum_{k=0}^{r-1} Im(conj(x_{i_k}) x_{i_{k+1}}),
    E_C = sum_{k=0}^{r-1} |x_{i_{k+1}}-x_{i_k}|^2.

The sum of q weights on C is b A_C-c E_C/2. Both orientations occur.
The finite data admit smooth-convex interpolating values if and only if

    c E_C >= 2 |b A_C| for every simple cycle C.                     (C)

Write D_h for this set of complex parameters. It is a closed semialgebraic set whose defining polynomials are obtained from all prefix polynomials. The two-cycle between * and 0 forces c>=0, so D_h is in the compact disk (a-1/2)^2+b^2<=1/4. No restriction to rational or algebraic schedules occurs.

Here is the value-selection argument. Give the complete directed graph on I weights q_ij on j->i. Under (C), every cycle has nonpositive weight. Let ell_i be the maximum weight of a path i->*, permitting the empty path at *. Removing repeated nonpositive cycles cannot decrease weight, so the maximum is over finitely many simple paths; ell_*=0. Set F_i=-ell_i. Appending a maximum path i->* to edge j->i, then removing cycles, gives ell_j>=q_ij+ell_i. Hence F_i-F_j>=q_ij.
Conversely, summing feasible differences along each path i->* yields F_i<=-ell_i. Thus these values simultaneously maximize every F_i for the fixed transcript.

A constructive interpolant is f=H^*, where

    H(g)=max_i { <g_i,x_i>-F_i + <x_i,g-g_i>
                 +(1/2)||g-g_i||^2 }.

The pair inequalities imply that the i-th quadratic is active at g_i, that H(g_i)=<g_i,x_i>-F_i, and that x_i belongs to the subdifferential of H at g_i. The finite function H is 1-strongly convex. Its conjugate is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth. Conjugacy gives f(x_i)=F_i and grad f(x_i)=g_i. The sample at * gives f(0)=0 and grad f(0)=0, so 0 is a global minimizer. This is the constructive argument of Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 4, Corollary 1 and Remark 2; the exact downloaded statement and proof were read.

Thus each z in D_h yields a legal witness

    B_h(z)=2 F_n=-2 ell_n <= W_n(h).                                   (B)

The optimum-pair inequalities give

    |z|^2 |P_n(z)|^2 <= B_h(z)
                    <= (2a-|z|^2)|P_n(z)|^2.                        (V)

All prefix information enters D_h and ell_n; the terminal polynomial alone cannot determine these cycle constraints.

## 3. A universal legal lens

Put N=n+2, theta=pi/N. The schedule-independent set

    K_n={a+ib : a-a^2-b^2 >= |b| cot(theta)}

lies in D_h for every schedule. For any complex r-gon v_j,

    |sum_j Im(conj(v_j) v_{j+1})|
      <= (1/2) cot(pi/r) sum_j |v_{j+1}-v_j|^2.                     (A)

Proof without simplicity or convexity of the polygon: write
v_j=sum_{k=0}^{r-1} d_k exp(2 pi i k j/r). Orthogonality makes the area expression r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin(2 pi k/r), and the energy 4r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin^2(pi k/r). The zero mode contributes nothing. For each other mode the absolute ratio is (1/2)|cot(pi k/r)|, at most (1/2)cot(pi/r). Since r<=N, (C) follows.

The finite cyclic monotonicity viewpoint is also discussed by Bartz, Bauschke, Borwein, Reich and Wang, Fitzpatrick functions, cyclic monotonicity and Rockafellar's antiderivative (2007), Example 4.6. That exact paper and its Fourier/circulant proof were downloaded and read. Inequality (A) is derived here instead of invoked as a black box.

## 4. Quantitative obstruction: the universal lens is too weak

K_n supplies legal nonquadratic witnesses but cannot prove the silver exponent on its own. An explicit Chebyshev schedule defeats this mechanism.

Let m=2n+1. The polynomial

    p(z)=(-1)^n T_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z))

has degree n, p(0)=1 and roots

    lambda_k=cos^2((2k-1) pi/(2m)), k=1,...,n.

It equals product_k(1-h_k z) for positive h_k=1/lambda_k, in any order. Here T_m(cos u)=cos(mu). Oddness makes the quotient a polynomial with a well-defined value at zero.

For n>=2 set t=tan(pi/(n+2)) and u=asinh(t/2). For z=a+ib in K_n, 0<=a<=1 and |b|<=t a(1-a). With s=a(1-a),

    (2a-1)^2/cosh^2(u) + (2b)^2/sinh^2(u)
      <= 1-4s+16s^2 <= 1.

Thus K_n is in the filled ellipse with foci 0,1 parametrized by z=(1+cos(w))/2 with |Im w|<=u. A square root is cos(w/2), so

    |z| |p(z)|^2 = |T_m(sqrt(z))|^2/m^2
                 <= cosh^2(m u/2)/m^2.

Since u<=t/2 and tan(theta)<=4 theta/pi on [0,pi/4],
m u/2<=m/(n+2)<2. From (V), every legal interpolating function for every z in K_n has terminal gap

    2 F_n <= 2 cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.                                  (O)

This is an upper bound only for the restricted witness family. It is not a full-class upper bound on the Chebyshev schedule.

Adding the canonical flat-region Huber witness does not repair this rate loss.
Differentiating the Chebyshev differential equation
(1-x^2)T_m''-x T_m'+m^2 T_m=0 at zero gives
T_m'''(0)=-(m^2-1)T_m'(0), with T_m'(0)=(-1)^n m. Consequently

    sum_k h_k=-p'(0)=(m^2-1)/6=2n(n+1)/3.

For S=sum h, the canonical Huber function is f_delta(x)=x^2/2 if |x|<=delta and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise, with delta=1/(2S+1).
Starting at x_0=1, GD stays in its positive affine-gradient region: all prefixes have x_t>=x_n=(S+1)/(2S+1)>=delta. Its normalized terminal gap is exactly 1/(2S+1), also O(n^-2).

The target benchmark has order n^-rho with rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2))<2.
Thus the guaranteed-lens-plus-canonical-Huber mechanism loses a factor tending to zero relative to the target on these schedules. This kills the universal-lens simplification, not D_h and not the original minimax conjecture.

## 5. Remaining branch decision

The full D_h construction gives a concrete legal candidate mechanism. No uniform estimate of sup_{z in D_h} B_h(z) matching S_bullet(n) is proved. Merely postulating that estimate is a theorem-strength blocked comparison.
A reopening needs a distinct structural estimate on prefix-polynomial point clouds or a stronger moment class; replacing D_h by K_n is quantitatively ruled out by (O).

## DOC-002573 — project/research/max2_earlier_reset_obstruction.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002573 -->
# Earlier reset weights: a quantitative bound and an exact obstruction to naive propagation

Status: unverified supplement to the full-certificate descent search. All claims below are proved directly; no earlier unverified finding is used as an assumed theorem.

## 1. A reset criterion for any earlier star-outgoing multiplier

Normalize L=1, f_*=0. Consider a full feasible certificate at level q for a length-n schedule h. Let nu_i=lambda_{*,i}>0 for some i<n. Define

    delta_i=f(x_i)-||g_i||^2/2 >=0,
    A_i=prod_{t=i+1}^{n-1} max{1,(h_t-1)^2}.

The full PSD certificate, with the term nu_i Q_{*,i}=-nu_i delta_i isolated, gives for every legal function

    2f(x_n)+nu_i delta_i <= q||x_0-x_*||^2.                 (1)

Replace h_i by one, leaving every other step in place. The changed trajectory agrees with the old one through x_i and reaches y=x_i-g_i at the next iteration. Unit-step descent gives f(y)<=delta_i.

For any nonnegative step a and any current point z, smoothness gives

    f(z-a grad f(z)) <= f(z)+(a^2/2-a)||grad f(z)||^2.

When 0<=a<=2, this is at most f(z). When a>2, use ||grad f(z)||^2<=2f(z) to obtain an upper bound (a-1)^2 f(z). Thus the unchanged suffix multiplies the objective by at most A_i, and

    W_n(h with h_i replaced by 1) <= (2A_i/nu_i) q.          (2)

In particular nu_i>2A_i gives a genuine fixed-length strict improvement. Every tight certificate at a globally minimizing schedule must therefore obey

    nu_i<=2A_i, 0<=i<n.                                    (3)

At i=n-1, direct endpoint convexity gives the much stronger conclusion nu_{n-1}=0 by the explicit last-step descent already recorded separately. Bound (3) is useful for earlier indices but does NOT assert that their multipliers vanish.

The product A_i is a proved worst-case growth bound, not a guess about conditional history. It can be large and does not itself yield the exact Zhang--Jiang profile.

## 2. Exact failure of carrying final-step convexity through even one later unit step

A tempting extension is to move an earlier step toward one and then use convexity to average the two resulting terminal objective values after the unchanged suffix. This fails for a single unchanged unit step.

Define an everywhere finite convex 1-smooth function on the real line by

    f(x)=x^2/2,                       x<=1;
         x-1/2,                       1<=x<=2;
         (x-1)^2/2+1,                 2<=x<=3;
         2x-3,                        x>=3.

Its derivative is, respectively, x, 1, x-1, and 2. It is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The unique minimum is f(0)=0.

Write T(x)=x-f'(x), the unit GD update, and F(x)=f(T(x)). The following exact values hold:

    F(3/2)=1/8,
    F(5/2)=1/2,
    F(2)=1/2 > (F(3/2)+F(5/2))/2 =5/16.                   (4)

Thus f composed with a unit GD step is not convex, even though f itself is convex and 1-smooth.

The points in (4) arise from exactly the proposed schedule interpolation. Start at x_0=9/2, where g_0=2. The first step b=3/2 gives x_1=3/2. Replacing it by the unit step gives x_1=5/2. Taking their midpoint step b=5/4 gives x_1=2. Follow all three by the same final unit step. Their final objective values are respectively 1/8, 1/2, and 1/2. Hence the middle schedule's value exceeds the convex average of the endpoint schedules by 3/16, with a common function and common initial point.

This refutes the exact propagation step needed to extend the penultimate convex-blend proof unchanged to earlier times. It does not refute a different simultaneous schedule/certificate perturbation, and the example is not claimed to be a matching worst-case stationary tuple.

## 3. Consequence for the active program

The final-step descent uses literal convexity of f along the final update line. Earlier reset changes enter a nonlinear suffix, so that proof cannot simply be repeated backward. The quantitative fallback (2) survives, but requires sufficiently large multiplier weight relative to the proved suffix-growth factor.

Likewise the terminal-pair Schur-range descent controls a perturbation at the final point directly; an earlier gradient relation cannot be treated as if the rest of the GD trajectory were affine. Any propagation mechanism must retain or bound the actual suffix response. This is the concrete obstruction to the attempted backward extension, not a conclusion that all full-certificate approaches fail.
