<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002574 -->
# Quantitative constraints on the full PEP certificate matrix

Status: new algebraic derivation under the updated assignment; not verifier-submitted. The initial selected submission was already used for fact dfde2fa326c908bf.

## 1. The actual certificate, including PSD

Normalize L=1, x_*=0 and f_*=0. Fix n>=1 and h in [0,infinity)^n. Put S=s(h), P_i=sum_{t<i}h_t for 0<=i<=n, P_*=0, and extend h by h_n=0. The P_i are prefix sums, not the polynomial notation Poly_i.

Use the signed interpolation inequalities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0,

with g_*=x_*=0. Let lambda_ij>=0 for all unequal indices i,j in {*,0,...,n}; put lambda_ii=0. A standard full PEP upper certificate at level q consists of

    out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n}, 0<=i<=n,                                 (F)
    M_cert(q,lambda,h) >= 0,                                       (PSD)

where out_i=sum_j lambda_ij, in_i=sum_j lambda_ji, and M_cert is the matrix of the quadratic expression

    q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_{i,j}lambda_ij Q_ij

after substitution x_i=x_0-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t. Condition (F) cancels every f_i. The matrix is evaluated on (x_0,g_0,...,g_n). Because every Q_ij<=0, (PSD) proves W_n(h)<=q. This statement is only the sufficient certificate direction; it does not assume strong duality.

Set mu_i=lambda_{i,*}, nu_i=lambda_{*,i}, Nu=sum_i nu_i and U=sum_i mu_i. Summing (F) gives U=2+Nu. The matrix has block form

    M_cert=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]].                                (M)

Here A is the full gradient block; it is not replaced by its diagonal or row sums. Positive semidefiniteness forces q>0, because q=0 would force mu=0, contradicting U>=2. Equivalently,

    Z_cert=A-mu mu^T/(4q) >=0.                                     (SC)

The equivalence follows by completing the square in the x_0 coordinate.

## 2. Exact chronological entries

For 0<=i<n define Forw_i=sum_{j=i+1}^n lambda_ij, and Forw_n=0. Direct expansion gives

    A_ii=out_i-1_{i=n}-h_i Forw_i.                                (D)

For 0<=i<j<=n,

    2A_ij=-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji)
          -h_j sum_{k=j+1}^n lambda_{ik}
          +h_i(mu_j+sum_{k=0}^i lambda_{jk}).                      (O)

Explanation: the squared-gradient differences supply minus the two directed edge weights off the diagonal, and half of out_i+in_i on the diagonal. In <g_i,x_j-x_i>, the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 is -h_i exactly when j>i. For the cross term <g_i,g_j>, i<j, a term based at i contributes -h_j if its destination is after j, while a term based at j contributes h_i if its destination is at most i or is *. Using (F) simplifies the diagonal.

Thus every feasible certificate satisfies, with z_i=A_ii-mu_i^2/(4q),

    z_i>=0,
    [A_ij-mu_i mu_j/(4q)]^2<=z_i z_j for i<j.                    (P2)

These are necessary consequences of the full Schur-complement PSD condition. They are not asserted to replace higher principal minors.

## 3. A flow-weighted quantitative Huber inequality

Let e denote the all-ones vector in R^{n+1}. Then the exact scalar identity is

    e^T A e=2S+1+sum_i nu_i(P_i+1).                               (E)

One way to check it is to substitute all gradients equal to a scalar t and x_0=1. Formally take f_i=t-(P_i+1/2)t^2. Then every Q_ij with finite i,j and every Q_{i,*} vanishes, while Q_{*,i}=-t+(P_i+1)t^2. The resulting polynomial identity in t is

    (1,t e)^T M_cert (1,t e)
      =q-(2+Nu)t+[2S+1+sum_i nu_i(P_i+1)]t^2.                    (T)

This is an identity in formal data; no assertion of interpolation for every t is needed to evaluate a PSD quadratic form. Its minimum over real t is nonnegative, so

    q >= (2+Nu)^2/[4(2S+1+sum_i nu_i(P_i+1))]
      >= (2+Nu)^2/[4(2S+1+(S+1)Nu)]
      >= 1/(2S+1).                                              (H)

The last inequality follows on multiplying through: with w=2S+1, the numerator difference is
w(2+Nu)^2-4[w+(S+1)Nu]=4S Nu+w Nu^2>=0.
All steps are exact for arbitrary nonnegative real h, including zero entries.

A more local version retains chronological residuals. Define

    drift_i=sum_{j finite or *}lambda_ij(P_i-P_j),
    tailnu_i=sum_{j=i+1}^n nu_j,
    R_i=2h_i+nu_i+drift_i+h_i tailnu_i-2S mu_i.                    (R)

The exact row identity is

    2Ae=2h+mu+nu+drift+h .* tailnu.                               (Rows)

The entrywise product term h .* tailnu is necessary when multipliers leave the optimum. It must not be discarded.
For a derivation, write c_i=-sum_{t<i}h_t e_t and c_*=0. The gradient part of each interpolation term is sym(e_i(c_j-c_i)^T), for finite i; the i=* terms contribute only squared gradients. Its product with e has first part drift/2. The second part is half of
sum_{i finite,j}lambda_ij(c_j-c_i)
 = -2c_n-sum_j nu_j c_j
 = 2h+h .* tailnu,
by the flow equations. The squared-gradient terms supply (mu+nu)/2. This proves (Rows).

Put tau=1/(2S+1), and define the nonnegative PSD evaluation

    eta=(1,tau e)^T M_cert (1,tau e)
       =q-tau-tau sum_i nu_i[1-tau(P_i+1)] >=0.                   (eta)

For S>0, P_i<=S implies

    Nu <= (q-tau)/(tau^2 S).                                     (Leak)

Furthermore, the i-th gradient coordinate of M_cert(1,tau e) is tau R_i/2. Cauchy--Schwarz for a PSD quadratic form, applied to this vector and the i-th coordinate vector, yields

    R_i^2 <= 4 A_ii eta/tau^2.                                   (Residual)

This is a quantitative restriction on the actual multipliers and PSD diagonal, not merely a recurrence for a scalar schedule statistic.

## 4. The exact Huber face

Assume S>0 and q=tau. The positivity of 1-tau(P_i+1)>=tau S implies nu=0 from (eta). The PSD quadratic form then vanishes on (1,tau e), so its entire matrix product with that vector vanishes. Consequently

    sum_i mu_i=2,
    2S mu_i=2h_i+sum_j lambda_ij(P_i-P_j),                        (B)
    Ae=(2S+1)mu/2,
    Z_cert e=0,    Z_cert>=0.                                    (Face)

The last PSD constraint is essential. The balance equations (B) alone are not a valid certificate.

If S=0, all steps vanish and W_n(h)=1, witnessed by f(x)=||x||^2/2. The division by S in (Leak) and the assertion nu=0 are not used in this degenerate case. Equations (F), (M), (D), (O), (E), (T), (Rows) and (eta) remain valid.

## 5. An exact pseudo-certificate test, and a feasible check

Take one step h_0=a>0 and demand q=1/(2a+1). If a full certificate exists, the exact-face formulas force nu=0. Put t=mu_0. Flow and (B) then give

    mu_1=2-t,
    lambda_01=2(1-t),
    lambda_10=2-t,
    0<=t<=1.

The two-dimensional Schur complement has zero row sums. Its first diagonal is

    Z_00=(2-2a)+(2a-1)t-(2a+1)t^2/4.                            (Z1)

For a>=3/2 this is increasing on 0<=t<=1: its derivative is
2a-1-(2a+1)t/2 >= a-3/2. Its maximum is therefore

    Z_00|_{t=1}=(3-2a)/4.

For a>3/2 it is negative, proving that no full PSD certificate at the Huber level exists, despite the nonnegative flow and row equations admitting t=1 for every a.

For 0<a<=3/2, t=1 gives

    mu=(1,1), lambda_10=1, lambda_01=0, nu=0,
    Z_cert=((3-2a)/4)[[1,-1],[-1,1]] >=0.

Thus the full matrix really is feasible and proves W_1(a)<=1/(2a+1); the canonical Huber witness makes this equality. At a=3/2 the Schur complement is exactly zero, reproducing the exact 1/4 objective bound.

For a=2, the same reduced data t=1 pass flow, all nonnegativity conditions and the Huber row equations, yet Z_00=-1/4. They would falsely claim W_1(2)<=1/5 if PSD were discarded; the legal quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 from x_0=1 has normalized terminal error 1. This is an exact hostile test of the scalar-only reduction.

## 6. Scope and next obstruction

The inequalities (H), (Residual) and (P2) are quantitative necessary conditions for every feasible full interpolation certificate. They do not rely on a global minimizer of the stepsize problem, differentiability of W, or stationarity, and therefore do not exclude unbounded or zero steps.

The all-schedule exact-profile problem is still open. Inequality (H) alone is only a refined sum-based Huber test. The mechanism worth pursuing is the interaction of the residual equations with the complete PSD matrix (SC), including the chronological entries (O). No claim is made that its row equations or its 2-by-2 minors suffice. Declaring the existence of an additional separating inequality of target strength would not close the problem.

# A globally minimizing stationary schedule with an unavoidable forward edge

Status: UNVERIFIED exact counterexample package. No additional verifier call
is allocated. The two-step optimum is used here as a fully certified calibration
example for a FACE-LEVEL support claim, not as a solution to the asymptotic target.

## 1. The precise property being refuted

The proposed property is:

    At a globally minimizing positive schedule, one can reselect a tight
    full interpolation certificate with lambda_(i,j)=0 for every
    finite chronological forward pair i<j.

This property is false. The example below is BOTH globally minimizing
among all nonnegative two-step schedules AND a full matching stationary
primal-dual tuple. Its ENTIRE optimal certificate set is a singleton,
and its multiplier lambda_01 is strictly positive.

The forced edge0->1 is ADJACENT. This does NOT refute the narrower
unproved property that only nonadjacent remote forward edges can be removed.

## 2. Exact constants and a complete full certificate

Define

    u=sqrt(2), a=u-1,
    b=(-1+sqrt(9+8u))/4,
    r=a b, q=r^2,
    h=(u,1+b).                                      (1)

The useful exact identities are

    a^2+2a=1,  u=1+a,  1/a=a+2,
    2b^2+b=a+2,
    r(2b+1)=1,
    q=1/[1+2(u+1+b)].                               (2)

For example, r(2b+1)=a(2b^2+b)=a(a+2)=1, and
1+2(u+1+b)=2a+2b+5=(2b+1)^2.
Also 2/5<a<1/2, 3/4<b<1, 0<r<1/2.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Use the complete inequalities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.

Set all unlisted multipliers to zero and take

    mu_0=lambda_(0,star)=2u r^2,
    mu_1=lambda_(1,star)=2u b r,
    mu_2=lambda_(2,star)=2r,

    lambda_01=2r^2,
    lambda_10=2r^2/a,
    lambda_21=2(1-r).                               (3)

All are positive. There are no star-outgoing multipliers.
The finite flow equations are

    mu_0+lambda_01=lambda_10,
    mu_1+lambda_10=lambda_01+lambda_21,
    mu_2+lambda_21=2.

They follow from (2); for example 2r^2/a+r=1 and
u r+u b+1=2b+1=1/r.

After x_1=x_0-u g_0 and x_2=x_1-(1+b)g_1, the FULL quadratic identity is

    q||x_0||^2-2f_2+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
         =||r x_0-u r g_0-u b g_1-g_2||^2.          (4)

One way to check all coefficients is to write its gradient block as

    A=(u r,u b,1)^T(u r,u b,1),

whose diagonals are (2r^2,2b^2,1) and off-diagonal entries are
A_01=2rb, A_02=u r, A_12=u b.
Its x_0 cross coefficients are minus the three mu_i from (3).
Thus every multiplier sign, flow, and full PSD entry is explicit.

This proves W_2(h)<=q.

## 3. Three exact matching legal functions

Each scalar function starts at x_0=1 and is minimized at zero.

H: the even Huber function of threshold q, with derivative
max(-q,min(q,x)). Its vector (x_0,g_0,g_1,g_2) is

    v_H=(1,q,q,q),

and its final value is f_2=q/2. At every sampled point its
star-outgoing slack is strictly positive:

    f_i-||g_i||^2/2=q[1-q(P_i+1)]>0,               (5)

where P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, including P_2=u+1+b.
The strict inequality follows from P_i<=P_2 and
q=1/(2P_2+1), with P_2>0.

Q: the quadratic function x^2/2. Its transcript vector is

    v_Q=(1,1,-a,r),

and f_2=r^2/2=q/2.

C: the one-sided capped quadratic

    f_C(x)=x^2/2 for x<=a,
           a x-a^2/2 for x>=a.

Its derivative min(x,a) is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz.
The trajectory is x_0=1, x_1=a, x_2=-r, with

    v_C=(1,a,a,-r),  f_2=q/2.                       (6)

All are actual globally legal smooth-convex functions. They establish
W_2(h)>=q and hence equality with the full certificate (4).

## 4. Global optimality among ALL two-step schedules

The only verified predecessor used here is fact b98bb807d514e80d:
its exact Huber and both single-cut lower bounds apply to arbitrary
nonnegative schedules. The elementary quadratic witness adds its own
explicit lower bound. Thus for every X,Y>=0,

    W_2(X,Y)>=max{
       1/(1+2X+2Y),
       (X-1)_+^2/(1+2Y),
       (Y-1)_+^2/(1+X)^2,
       (X-1)^2(Y-1)^2
    }.                                             (7)

The last term is the error on x^2/2; the other three are the verified
single-cut/Huber bounds. Suppose W_2(X,Y)<=q=r^2. The Huber term gives

    X+Y>=u+1+b=2+a+b>3.                            (8)

If X<=1, then Y>1 and the third term in (7) gives
Y-1<=r(1+X), whence X+Y<=2+2r<3, contradicting (8).
If Y<=1, then X>1 and the second term gives
X-1<=r sqrt(1+2Y)<=r sqrt(3), whence X+Y<3, again impossible.

We may therefore set U=X-1>0 and V=Y-1>0. Equation (7) implies

    U V<=r,
    V<=r(U+2),
    U^2<=r^2(2V+3).                                (9)

For U<=a, use the second inequality:

    U+V<=U+r(U+2)<=a+r(a+2)=a+b,

with equality only at U=a,V=b.
For U>=a, the first and third inequalities imply

    U^3-3r^2 U-2r^3<=0,
    (U-2r)(U+r)^2<=0,

so a<=U<=2r. Also V<=r/U. The strictly convex function
U+r/U attains its maximum on [a,2r] at an endpoint. Its values are

    a+r/a=a+b,
    2r+1/2.

The first is strictly greater, since

    (a+b)-(2r+1/2)=a^2(b-1/2)>0.

Thus U+V<=a+b, again with equality only at U=a,V=b.
Combining with (8), every schedule with W_2(X,Y)<=q is exactly
(X,Y)=(u,1+b). In particular (1) is the UNIQUE global minimizer, and

    W_2^*=q.                                       (10)

All nonnegative schedules, including zero steps and arbitrary large
steps, were included. This is an exact two-step argument, not an
asymptotic profile claim.

## 5. The ENTIRE optimal certificate set is a singleton

Take ANY full nonnegative-flow PSD certificate at this fixed h,q.
Every actual instance in Section3 attains q. Therefore PSD and the
interpolation signs force the certificate matrix M to annihilate
v_H,v_Q,v_C, and every multiplier to vanish on a strict interpolation
slack of any one of these instances.

The three transcript vectors are independent. Indeed, v_H-v_C has
equal, nonzero g_0 and g_1 coordinates, whereas v_Q-v_C has those
coordinates 1-a and -2a, which are unequal. Both differences have
x_0 coordinate zero, while v_C has x_0 coordinate one. The nonzero
claim uses q<a, which follows from a b^2=(1-r)/2<1/2.

Their common orthogonal complement is generated by

    N=(r,-u r,-u b,-1).

It annihilates v_Q and v_C by (2), and N dot v_H=0 follows from
u r+u b+1=1/r. Since M is PSD and its x_0,x_0 coefficient is q=r^2,

    M=N N^T.                                       (11)

This is the WHOLE matrix for every optimal certificate. No assumption
about a numerical maximal rank or a chosen observed kernel is used;
three explicit legal equality instances force it.

Equation (5) forces every star-outgoing multiplier nu_i to be zero.
The x_0 row of (11) fixes mu uniquely at the values in (3).
The final gradient diagonal satisfies

    A_22=out_2-1=1+in_2

by the full flow equation. In (11), A_22=1, so every incoming
terminal multiplier is zero: lambda_02=lambda_12=nu_2=0.

For function C a direct substitution gives

    Q_20=(a-1)(a+r)<0.

Therefore lambda_20=0 for every optimal certificate as well.
The first gradient diagonal now reads

    A_00=mu_0-(u-1)lambda_01
         =2u r^2-a lambda_01.

In (11) it equals 2r^2, hence

    lambda_01=2r^2=2q>0.                            (12)

Flow then forces lambda_10=2r^2/a and lambda_21=2(1-r), leaving
exactly (3). All directed pairs have now been accounted for.

Thus the optimal certificate set is a singleton, and re-selection,
relative-interior selection, and maximal-rank selection all leave
the positive adjacent forward edge unchanged.

## 6. An ACTUAL matching stationary primal, with no assumed regularity

To check stationarity directly, set

    omega_H=1/(1+a^2 b^3),
    omega_C=omega_H*a^2 b^2*(b-a)/(1+a^2),
    omega_Q=omega_H*a^3 b^2*(1+a b)/(1+a^2).          (13)

They are positive and sum to one:
omega_C+omega_Q=omega_H a^2 b^3.
Construct the actual three-dimensional function

    F(z_H,z_Q,z_C)
       =sum_(J in {H,Q,C}) omega_J f_J(z_J/sqrt(omega_J))

and start at (sqrt(omega_H),sqrt(omega_Q),sqrt(omega_C)).
It is convex and 1-smooth, the initial squared distance is one, and
its full trajectory Gram matrix is
G=sum_J omega_J v_J v_J^T. Its final normalized value is q.

For the certificate (3), the two formal matrix derivatives evaluated
on any scalar/vector realization are

    D_0=2b^2<g_0,g_1>+2r<g_0,g_2>-2r^2||g_0||^2,
    D_1=2<g_1,g_2>.                                (14)

Their values on the three equality instances are

              H              Q                C
    D_0       2r^4           -2a b^2           2a r^2
    D_1       2r^4           -2a r             -2a r.

These values are obtained from (14), not by differentiating a
nonsmooth family of extremal functions. In particular the fixed
capped function C has a gradient kink at x_1=a, so that shortcut
would be unsafe.

The identities

    omega_Q-a^2 omega_C=omega_H a^3 b^2,
    omega_Q+omega_C=omega_H a^2 b^3

make both weighted derivatives zero. Therefore

    <G,partial M/partial h_0>=<G,partial M/partial h_1>=0.

All primal interpolation inequalities, PSD, normalization, full
matrix complementarity, multiplier complementarity, and both
stepsize stationarity equations hold for this actual instance.
Moreover rank(G)=3, rank(M)=1, and the formal matrix size is4.
This example even has strict complementarity; a hidden nonphysical
common kernel is not responsible for the obstruction.

## 7. Consequences and precise limits

This is a complete counterexample to removing ALL forward finite
multipliers by re-selection, even at a positive globally minimizing
schedule with a full stationary matching instance and no extra
common kernel. It closes a possible attempt to turn the triangular
sufficient hypothesis in the earlier suffix-deformation candidate
into a universal property of optimal schedules.

It does not refute removal of ONLY nonadjacent remote forward
edges: lambda_02=0 in the example. It also has q equal to the
Huber value, every nu_i=0, and Z=0; consequently it is not a
counterexample to global Huber tightness. The asymptotic exact-profile
question and the mass-cap bridge remain unresolved.

No literature result beyond the verified predecessor b98bb807d514e80d
was used as a proof brick. The targeted Matlas query returned no
applicable statement. The global two-step calculation is included
solely to make the counterexample's global and stationary status
reviewable without relying on a numerical optimum.

## DOC-002576 — project/research/max2_interval_descent_compatibility.md

# Compatibility screen: interval matrices and full joint descent

Status: UNVERIFIED algebraic screen requested by master_guidance31b8d52469caa452.
It uses max4's cd0f93f08a50828f as a lead, not as a verified building block.
All identities needed below are derived explicitly. No numerical or verifier
allocation is used.

## 1. Exact dictionary, including the star edges

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Use
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.
A full certificate has lambda>=0, finite flow out-in=2 e_n, and
M(q,lambda,h)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij>=0.

Let C be an (n+1)-square real matrix with C1=0; its off-diagonal
entries may have either sign. Put b=C^T1. Define T(h) by T_ii=1,
T_ij=h_j when j<i, and T_ij=0 when j>i, and set
H(C,h)=CT(h)+T(h)^T C^T.

The exact sign dictionary is

2 sum_(i!=j) C_ij Q_ij
  =-2 sum_i b_i f_i - sum_(i,j)H_ij<g_i,g_j>
    +sum_i b_i||g_i||^2.                             (1)

For verification, the function terms are -2b^Tf because row sums
vanish. The position term is 2sum C_ij<g_i,x_j-x_i>
=2sum C_ij<g_i,x_j>, since C1=0. Substituting
x_j=x_0+g_j-(Tg)_j and again using C1=0 gives
2sum C_ij<g_i,g_j>-2sum(CT)_ij<g_i,g_j>.
The squared-gradient difference contributes
b^T(||g_i||^2)-2sum C_ij<g_i,g_j>. Combining proves (1).

Thus the following simultaneous finite/star multiplier change preserves
all flow equations and keeps mu_i=lambda_(i,star) fixed:

lambda'_ij=lambda_ij+2C_ij   (finite distinct i,j),
nu'_i=nu_i-2b_i,             nu_i=lambda_(star,i).     (2)

Indeed finite out-in changes by -2b_i, which is canceled by the
change -2b_i in incoming star flow. Also sum b_i=0.

At a FIXED schedule, the exact complete matrix change is simply

M(q,lambda',h)-M(q,lambda,h)=-H(C,h),                  (3)

embedded in the gradient block, with the x_0 row unchanged. The
function terms and b_i||g_i||^2 terms in (1) cancel against
sum(-2b_i)Q_(star,i). This is why the star adjustment must not be
omitted.

The ACTUAL sign constraints are

lambda_ij+2C_ij>=0  (i!=j),   nu_i-2b_i>=0.           (4)

They are neither positivity of interval coordinates nor positivity of
every C entry. Negative C entries are allowed exactly to the extent
that the old finite multipliers supply them.

## 2. Simultaneously changing the schedule

Let h'=h+a>=0. Define the full cut vector

z_t(lambda)=sum_(i,j)lambda_ij
 (1_(t<i)-1_(t<j))g_i,

where either indicator is zero at the star. Define the quadratic form

K(a;lambda)=sum_(t<n)a_t<g_t,z_t(lambda)>.

Then (1)--(3), now applied at h', give the exact identity

M(q',lambda',h') =
M(q,lambda,h)+(q'-q)||x_0||^2+K(a;lambda)-H(C,h').    (5)

This includes all remote forward-cut terms. For i<j, the matrix entries
of K in the gradient block are

2K_ij=a_i[mu_j+sum_(l<=i)lambda_(j,l)]
       -a_j sum_(l>j)lambda_(i,l),
K_ii=-a_i sum_(j>i)lambda_(i,j).                     (6)

The expression in (6) follows directly by collecting the two cut
contributions to <g_i,g_j>.

Consequently a diagonal H(C,h') does NOT diagonalize the full
joint perturbation: K still has the mixed entries (6). The exact
zero-mixed-entry target is H_ij=K_ij for i!=j, not H_ij=0.

For example, if every finite forward edge is zero, (6) becomes
2K_ij=a_i c_(i,j), where
c_(i,j)=mu_j+sum_(l<=i)lambda_(j,l).
Summing flow over the suffix shows sum_(j>i)c_(i,j)
=2+sum_(j>i)nu_j>0. Thus if H is diagonal AND K-H is
required to have no mixed entries, each a_i must be zero.
This is a precise incompatibility of those two simultaneous
requirements. It does not rule out allowing a non-diagonal PSD
remainder in (5).

## 3. Inactive-constraint costs survive this coordinate change

Suppose the old full certificate matches one legal stationary primal:
||x_0||=1, 2f_n=q, M vanishes on its data, lambda_ij Q_ij=0, and
<g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0. Put sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0 on these SAME data.

If (4) holds, evaluating the fixed-h change (3) on the old data gives

sum_(i,j)H(C,h)_ij<g_i,g_j>
 =sum_(i,j)lambda'_ij sigma_ij>=0.                  (7)

The sum on the right includes the star edges. At the new schedule,
PSD of (5) necessarily gives

q'-q >= sum_(i,j)H(C,h')_ij<g_i,g_j>.                (8)

Writing DeltaT=T(h')-T(h), the right side is exactly the nonnegative
cost in (7) plus

2 sum_(i,j)(C DeltaT)_ij<g_i,g_j>.

Thus a descent q'<q must pay every inactive-interpolation cost through
this bilinear term. Diagonalization does not erase the earlier
joint-descent obstruction.

## 4. The triangular elimination can be made inhomogeneous

Assume h'_i>1 for i<n. Specify the strict upper entries U_ij=C_ij.
For j>i define L_(j,i)=sum_(l<=i)C_(j,l), L_(j,-1)=0.
The desired off-diagonal matrix H has the exact equation

(h'_i-1)L_(j,i)+L_(j,i-1)
 =U_ij+h'_j sum_(l>j)U_(i,l)-H_ij.                 (9)

At j=n the displayed sum is empty and the auxiliary h'_n is irrelevant.
Indeed (CT)_ij=U_ij+h'_j sum_(l>j)U_(i,l), while
(CT)_ji=-(h'_i-1)L_(j,i)-L_(j,i-1), proving (9).

Taking H_ij=K_ij makes (9) a scalar elimination for the mixed
cancellation in (5). After it, one STILL has to check every sign in
(4), all diagonal entries or the full remaining PSD matrix, and
strict output improvement. Nothing in the elimination proves those
conditions automatically.

## 5. Compatibility with the actual new deformation

The independently derived candidate8c998d29efd3da99 uses
C_(j,k)=eta p_j/2 for j>k and C_(j,j)=-eta p_j/2, with all other
entries zero. It has b_k=eta/2 and b_j=-eta p_j/2.
These are exactly the star changes in that candidate.

Its H(C,h') is generally NOT diagonal. The specific suffix step
rescalings make its mixed entries equal to K_ij, and its remaining
diagonal difference is eta diag(p). This is a concrete solution of
(5), whereas max4's homogeneous H_ij=0 modes do not by themselves
supply that cancellation.

Conclusion of the bounded screen: the interval coordinates give a
useful exact dictionary and an inhomogeneous elimination, but the
published zero-remainder interval subclass is not a ready-made repair
for the new deformation's cut-profile mismatch. It retains the same
aggregate-sign and inactive-cost obligations. No all-schedule descent,
Huber saturation, or exact-profile lower bound follows.

# Exact cost of a joint certificate perturbation above the Huber level

Status: unverified quantitative derivation, following the updated q>tau assignment. The historical GS interface is not re-proved. The initial verification allowance is already used.

## 1. Definitions and the exact finite-perturbation inequality

Normalize L=1, x_*=g_*=f_*=0. For a fixed positive length-n schedule h, let (q,lambda,G,f) be a full stationary primal-dual tuple. Explicitly:

* lambda_ij>=0 for distinct i,j in I={*,0,...,n}, with out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n};
* M(q,lambda,h)>=0 is the matrix of q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij after GD substitution;
* G>=0 is the Gram matrix of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), G_00=1, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0;
* M G=0 and lambda_ij Q_ij=0;
* <g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0 for t<n, where
  z_t(b)=sum_{i,j} b_ij(1_{t<i}-1_{t<j})g_i.
  The indicator is zero for the star, and g_*=0.

All objects in these conditions refer to the SAME full legal transcript. No subset of these conditions is used as a replacement for the full system.

Set sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0. Consider ANY second feasible upper certificate (q',lambda',h'), where h'>=0 and lambda' has the same flow. The new certificate need not be optimal. Put a=h'-h and b=lambda'-lambda. Define the explicit bilinear form

    B_G(a,b)=sum_{t<n} a_t <g_t,z_t(b)>.

Because M is affine separately in h and lambda, its exact finite expansion, paired with the original G, is

    <M(q',lambda',h'),G>
      = (q'-q)-sum_{i,j}lambda'_ij sigma_ij+B_G(a,b).           (1)

To verify the formula, the first-order h term vanishes by stationarity. The lambda term is sum b_ij T_ij(G), with T_ij=Q_ij-(f_i-f_j). Flow equality cancels the f differences, while sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0 changes sum b_ij Q_ij to -sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij. The only remaining mixed term is B_G(a,b). The q coefficient is G_00=1. There are no higher-order terms.

Both matrices in the left side are PSD. Therefore EVERY new feasible certificate satisfies

    q'-q >= sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij - B_G(h'-h,lambda'-lambda). (2)

This is a concrete quantitative obstruction to a proposed joint descent. A descent by Delta=q-q'>0 must obey

    B_G(h'-h,lambda'-lambda)
       >= Delta+sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij.                       (3)

In particular:

* Holding h fixed cannot lower q, as expected from matching primal feasibility.
* Holding lambda fixed cannot lower q for ANY new h, not just for a small change: then both terms on the right of (2) vanish.
* The gain from simultaneous motion has to overcome the exact cost of placing multiplier mass on interpolation inequalities that are strict for the old stationary primal.

Let R_g=max_i ||g_i||. Direct Cauchy--Schwarz bounds give

    |B_G(a,b)|<=R_g^2 ||a||_1 ||b||_1,
    Delta+sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij
       <=R_g^2 ||h'-h||_1 ||lambda'-lambda||_1                 (4)

for every feasible descent. Thus joint movement, not a single-block update, is essential. This is a necessary inequality, not a proof that a descent exists when q exceeds tau.

For a differentiable path through a stationary tuple, any first-order newly positive multiplier on an edge with sigma_ij>0 costs first order, while the useful bilinear term starts at second order. Hence a path with q'(0)=0 must have zero first derivative on every such inactive edge. This conclusion also follows directly from (2) by dividing by the path parameter.

## 2. One-step q>tau certificates with unavoidable star-outgoing flow

The full one-step system provides a useful hostile test of attempts to remove lambda_{*,i} without moving the schedule.

Take h_0=1+a with a>=1. The true worst-case value is q=a^2, certified by

    lambda_{0,*}=2a^2,
    lambda_{*,0}=2a^2-2,
    lambda_{1,0}=2,

and all other multipliers zero. The flow equations hold. Its entire PSD identity is

    q||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
      =a^2||x_0-g_0||^2+||a g_0+g_1||^2.                    (5)

The quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 from x_0=1 gives g_0=1, g_1=-a, f_1=a^2/2 and makes the right side zero. Thus primal and dual match and W_1(1+a)=a^2. This value is strictly above tau=1/(2a+3) for a>=1.

For a>1, every tight certificate at this very same schedule needs nonzero star-outgoing flow. To prove this, suppose nu_i=lambda_{*,i}=0. Then mu_0+mu_1=2, where mu_i=lambda_{i,*}. Pairing the optimal PSD slack with the matching quadratic vector (1,1,-a) gives M(1,1,-a)^T=0. Its x_0 row yields

    mu_0-a mu_1=2a^2.

Combining with mu_0+mu_1=2 gives

    mu_1=2(1-a)<0,

a contradiction. Thus a general-q proof cannot simply remove all star-outgoing multipliers while holding h and q fixed.

There IS an actual full feasible descent in this example. For any 0<epsilon<a-1, replace a by a-epsilon in all displayed variables. The nonnegative multipliers, both squares in (5), the chronological GD equation and the matching quadratic primal all remain valid. The objective drops by

    a^2-(a-epsilon)^2=2a epsilon-epsilon^2>0.

This verifies a schedule-and-flow motion with the full cone and the same legal function, rather than just a formal linearized descent. These tuples are not stationary, so the example does not decide the hypothetical global-minimizer question.

For completeness, the path continues below a=1. For 1/2<=a<=1 take

    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a,

all other multipliers zero, and q=a^2. The gradient block is [[2a,1],[1,1]], while mu=(2a,2(1-a)). Its Schur complement is

    Z=(2a-1) [[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]] >=0.

The quadratic again annihilates the full slack and attains q. Decreasing a keeps all constraints feasible until a=1/2, at which point q=tau=1/4. Thus the full one-step non-Huber region has an explicit piecewise-algebraic descent to the Huber-tight optimum. The change of formula at a=1 is necessary to preserve nonnegative multipliers.

## 3. Why the general-q residual alone has not supplied the descent

For an arbitrary full feasible certificate, the earlier exact Huber evaluation is

    eta=(1,tau e)^T M(1,tau e)
       =q-tau-tau sum_i nu_i[1-tau(P_i+1)]>=0,
    tau=1/(2S+1), S=sum h_t, P_i=sum_{t<i}h_t.

Its residual estimates control M applied to this one explicit Huber vector. A feasible descent from a stationary certificate also has to control the ENTIRE nullspace of M and the cost of activating strict primal interpolation inequalities. Formula (2) makes the missing interaction concrete: a positive value of q-tau by itself neither gives an allowed first-order multiplier support nor a positive value of B_G large enough to pay the required costs.

Two attempted shortcuts therefore fail for explicit reasons:

1. Freeze the optimal multipliers and use the Huber slack to move only h: equation (2) proves that q cannot decrease for any h'.
2. Remove star-outgoing flow at fixed h and q: the exact a>1 example proves such a tight certificate need not exist.

These are restrictions on the indicated operations. They do not refute a general simultaneous feasible descent theorem at global minimizers. The old full stationary tuple must remain attached to (2); using source/cut/energy identities with a nonlegal Gram would invalidate the inequality.

The current open task is to build a simultaneous variation with a favorable explicit B_G and PSD feasibility from the specific GD structure, or obtain a different quantitative consequence from the complete matrix. No Huber-tightness or asymptotic profile comparison has been proved here.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002578 -->
# Joint suffix deformation with exact PSD preservation

Status: complete conditional derivation, UNVERIFIED. It is a new J1 candidate,
not a claim that all global minimizers have the hypotheses. No further
verification allocation has been made.

## Full certificate and the additional triangular hypothesis

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be
nonnegative and finite. For distinct indices in I={star,0,...,n}, set

Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Assume a complete upper certificate (q,lambda,h): q>0, lambda_ij>=0,
out_i-in_i=2 1_(i=n) at finite indices, and the quadratic form

M=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij

after x_j=x_0-sum_(t<j)h_t g_t is positive semidefinite. It is a
quadratic form in the independent formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n);
flow cancels every function-value coefficient. Thus 2f_n<=q||x_0||^2
on every legal trajectory.

For this candidate ONLY, additionally assume

lambda_ij=0 whenever 0<=i<j<=n.                         (T)

All finite-index multiplier flow goes backward. Star edges may go either
way. Write mu_j=lambda_(j,star), nu_j=lambda_(star,j), and, for t<j,

c_(t,j)=mu_j+sum_(l=0)^t lambda_(j,l).

Every c is nonnegative. The complete matrix entries are

M_(x_0,x_0)=q,   M_(x_0,g_j)=-mu_j/2,
M_(g_j,g_j)=out_j-1_(j=n),
2 M_(g_t,g_j)=h_t c_(t,j)-lambda_(j,t),   t<j.          (1)

To check (1), the squared-gradient terms contribute
(out_j+in_j)/2=out_j-1_(j=n) on the diagonal. A backward
edge j->t contributes -lambda_(j,t)<g_j,g_t> from the squared
difference, and h_t times the total flow from j to star or an index at
most t contributes h_t c_(t,j)<g_j,g_t>. The star pair contributes
-mu_j<x_0,g_j>. This accounts for every term; no principal submatrix
replaces M.

## Deformation

Fix k<n with nu_k>0. Define C=sum_(j>k)c_(k,j). By summing flow over
{k+1,...,n}, hypothesis (T) gives C=2+sum_(j>k)nu_j>0.
Put p_j=c_(k,j)/C for j>k, so p_j>=0 and sum p_j=1.

Assume the following explicit proportionality condition on later cuts:

for every k<t<n there is r_t>=0 such that
p_j=r_t c_(t,j) for all j>t.                           (P)

Zero p entries are allowed. If all p_j vanish beyond t, one may take
r_t=0. Set r_k=1/C.

Choose any 0<eta<=nu_k. Change only these multipliers:

nu'_k=nu_k-eta,
nu'_j=nu_j+eta p_j             (j>k),
lambda'_(j,k)=lambda_(j,k)+eta p_j   (j>k).             (2)

Keep every mu_j and every other finite edge unchanged. Change the schedule by

h'_t=h_t                                      (t<k),
h'_k=(h_k+eta r_k)/(1+eta r_k),
h'_t=h_t/(1+eta r_t)                           (k<t<n). (3)

All new steps and multipliers are nonnegative. At k the increased
incoming backward flow eta cancels the removed incoming star flow eta;
at j>k the new outgoing edge eta p_j cancels the new incoming star
edge eta p_j. Thus every flow equation stays exact.

The NEW full matrix, on the same formal vector coordinates, satisfies

M(q,lambda',h') =
M(q,lambda,h)+eta sum_(j>k)p_j ||g_j||^2.               (4)

Proof: diagonal j>k increases by eta p_j, while all x_0 entries and
all other diagonals stay fixed. For t<k, c_(t,j), h_t and lambda_(j,t)
are unchanged. At t=k,

h'_k(c_(k,j)+eta p_j)-(lambda_(j,k)+eta p_j)
=h_k c_(k,j)-lambda_(j,k),

using p_j=r_k c_(k,j). At t>k the edge lambda_(j,t) is unchanged and

h'_t(c_(t,j)+eta p_j)=h_t c_(t,j),

using (P). Therefore EVERY mixed entry is unchanged, proving (4).
In particular the whole new matrix remains PSD, including every old
kernel direction. No forward edge is created and no preexisting edge
is decreased except the explicitly available star multiplier nu_k.

## Strict worst-case improvement

Identity (4) gives, on every trajectory of the NEW schedule,

q R^2-2f_n >= eta sum_(j>k)p_j ||g_j||^2,               (5)

where R=||x_0||>0. Here the old M on the right of (4) is PSD on arbitrary
formal vectors; the new transcript need not be a trajectory of the old
schedule.

Set B=product_(t=0)^(n-1)(1+h'_t) and
D_j=product_(t=j)^(n-1)(1+h'_t), with D_n=1.
These finite positive quantities are deliberately crude.

Since g_star=0 and the gradient is 1-Lipschitz, ||g_t||<=||x_t||, hence
||x_n||<=B R. Convexity gives f_n<=<g_n,x_n><=B R||g_n||.
The same Lipschitz property gives
||g_(t+1)||<=||g_t||+||x_(t+1)-x_t||=(1+h'_t)||g_t||,
so ||g_n||<=D_j||g_j||.

Writing E=2f_n/R^2, these inequalities and (5) yield

q-E >= gamma E^2,
gamma=eta/(4B^2) sum_(j>k) p_j/D_j^2 >0.              (6)

Consequently the actual fixed-length new schedule obeys

W_n(h') <= 2q/[1+sqrt(1+4 gamma q)] < q.               (7)

This is a uniform bound over every dimension and every legal function,
not a calculation on the old active instance. Neither stationarity nor
q=1/(2sum h+1) was used. In particular, if the old certificate is tight
at a globally minimizing schedule, hypotheses (T),(P),nu_k>0 cannot
hold simultaneously.

## Two remaining steps: condition (P) is automatic

Take k=n-2. Define

c_1=mu_(n-1)+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n-1,l),
c_2=mu_n+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n,l),
c_3=mu_n+sum_(l<=n-1)lambda_(n,l)=2+nu_n>0.

Then C=c_1+c_2, p_(n-1)=c_1/C, p_n=c_2/C, and
r_(n-1)=p_n/c_3 satisfies (P), including c_2=0. Formula (3) changes
BOTH of the final two steps:

h'_(n-2)=(C h_(n-2)+eta)/(C+eta),
h'_(n-1)=h_(n-1)c_3/(c_3+eta p_n).

The complete slack gains exactly
eta p_(n-1)||g_(n-1)||^2+eta p_n||g_n||^2.
Thus for a triangular tight certificate at a global minimizer,
nu_(n-2) must vanish. At horizon n=2 this excludes nu_0>0 under (T).
This is a genuine earlier-step joint deformation, not the already
recorded final-step convex blend.

## Precise limitation

For k<t<n, if positive ratios p_j/c_(t,j) differ as j varies, no single
h'_t can satisfy h'_t(c_(t,j)+eta p_j)=h_t c_(t,j) for all j>t when
h_t>0. Thus this one-column transport cannot simply be declared to
work on arbitrary longer suffixes. Additional multiplier changes,
a non-diagonal PSD residual, or a different mechanism would be needed.

Nor is (T) established for arbitrary optimal certificates. Forward
edges produce additional mixed terms when suffix steps change.
The candidate does NOT establish global Huber tightness or the
Zhang--Jiang exact-profile lower bound.

## Hostile checks within the derivation

- No division by h_t, h_t-1, p_j, or nu_j for j>k is used.
  Zero steps, zero weights, and steps on either side of1 are covered.
- If h_k=1, that coordinate stays fixed; positive later r_t can still
  change the suffix. If every affected coordinate were unchanged,
  (5)--(7) would show that q was not a tight certificate.
- Even p_n=0 leaves gamma>0, since some earlier p_j is positive and
  D_j is finite.
- The old matching primal is never reused as if it satisfied new GD
  recursions. Uniform legality enters only in (5)--(7).

Retrieval: fact_search found no matching result. Targeted Matlas search
returned unrelated statements; no external theorem is used in this proof.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002579 -->
# Selected complex-polynomial bridge and lens obstruction

Status: verifier accepted as fact dfde2fa326c908bf. The fact graph is authoritative; this is the submitted proof artifact.

## Statement

Complex-polynomial transcripts, an explicit legal spectral lens, and a quantitative limit of that lens.

For each integer n>=1 and each real nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), define Poly_0(z)=1 and Poly_i(z)=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1-h_t z) for 1<=i<=n. Identify the complex plane with the real Euclidean plane, with inner product <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). For z=alpha+i beta set xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z) for 0<=i<=n, and xi_*=gamma_*=0. Put kappa=alpha-|z|^2 and I_n={*,0,...,n}. For each ordered pair define
q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2,
regarded as the weight on the directed edge j->i. For each directed simple cycle C in I_n define
Area_C=sum_{j->i in C} Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i),
Energy_C=sum_{j->i in C}|xi_i-xi_j|^2.
Define D_h to be the set of z satisfying kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C| for every such cycle.

(a) The data (xi_i,gamma_i), including the optimum sample *, are interpolable by a finite everywhere-defined convex differentiable function f:R^2->R with 1-Lipschitz gradient and grad f(0)=0 if and only if z belongs to D_h. For z in D_h let ell_i be the largest q-weight of a directed path i->*, allowing the empty path at *, and put v_i=-ell_i. The maxima exist over simple paths and ell_*=0. There is such an f with f(xi_i)=v_i for every i. These values are simultaneously maximal among all interpolating f satisfying f(0)=0. Its exact GD recursion for h starts at xi_0=1, and its normalized terminal error is
Gap_h(z):=-2ell_n.
In particular
|z|^2|Poly_n(z)|^2 <= Gap_h(z) <= (2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.

(b) The parameter lens
Lens_n={alpha+i beta: alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}
is contained in D_h for every h.

(c) Let Cheb_j be the polynomials defined by Cheb_0(x)=1, Cheb_1(x)=x and Cheb_{j+1}(x)=2xCheb_j(x)-Cheb_{j-1}(x). For every n>=2 define the positive schedule Chi_n, in any permutation, by the n entries
sec^2((2k-1)pi/(2(2n+1))), k=1,...,n.
Its terminal polynomial is (-1)^n Cheb_{2n+1}(sqrt(z))/((2n+1)sqrt(z)), interpreted as a polynomial including z=0, and its sum is s(Chi_n)=2n(n+1)/3. For all z in Lens_n,
Gap_{Chi_n}(z) <= 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.

For an arbitrary schedule of length n>=1 define
V_lens(h)=max{sup_{z in Lens_n} Gap_h(z), 1/(2s(h)+1)}.
Let W_n(h) be the project worst-case normalized objective error, namely the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite dimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and s(h)=sum h_t. Then V_lens(h)<=W_n(h). For the exact benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1) defined by the recursions in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, and p=log_2 3 from that predecessor, the explicit schedules satisfy
0<=V_lens(Chi_n)/U_n^ZJ
 <= 2cosh^2(2)(1+4n^p)/n^2 ->0.
Thus the universal-lens witnesses, even augmented by the canonical sum-based Huber witness, cannot prove the required uniform matching lower bound. This is a limitation of that witness family only, not an upper bound on W_n(Chi_n) and not a decision of the unrestricted minimax target.

## Proof

1. Necessary smooth-convex inequality.
For any convex differentiable 1-smooth f and any x,y, put k(u)=f(u)-<grad f(y),u>. Convexity and grad k(y)=0 imply k(y)<=k(u). Integrating the Lipschitz gradient along a segment gives k(u+d)<=k(u)+<grad k(u),d>+||d||^2/2. Take d=-grad k(u) and combine these inequalities to obtain
f(x)>=f(y)+<grad f(y),x-y>+||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2/2. (1)
Applied to any proposed interpolant, (1) says its values obey f_i-f_j>=q_ij.

2. Cycle identity and necessity.
For complex u,v, <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). Expand
<z xi_j,xi_i-xi_j>
=alpha Re(conj(xi_j)(xi_i-xi_j))+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).
The real part is (|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2-|xi_i-xi_j|^2)/2. Also
||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2=|z|^2|xi_i-xi_j|^2.
Therefore
q_ij=(alpha/2)(|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2)+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i)
      -(kappa/2)|xi_i-xi_j|^2. (2)
The norm-difference terms telescope on a cycle, whose weight is beta Area_C-kappa Energy_C/2. Both orientations are available, and their areas have opposite signs but equal energies. Consequently nonpositivity of every cycle is equivalent to the definition of D_h. Summing (1) on cycles proves the necessary implication in (a). The cycle *->0->* also forces kappa>=0.

3. Values from paths.
Assume z is in D_h. Every closed walk is a sum of simple directed cycles, after splitting at repeated vertices; thus every closed walk has nonpositive weight. Any path to * can have repeated vertices deleted, and deleting the intervening nonpositive cycle cannot reduce the weight. There are finitely many simple paths on I_n, so ell_i is finite and is a maximum over them. At * the empty path has weight zero and every closed walk has weight at most zero, so ell_*=0.
Concatenate edge j->i with a maximum path i->*, then delete cycles if needed. It follows that ell_j>=q_ij+ell_i. Hence v_i=-ell_i satisfies v_i-v_j>=q_ij. For any other feasible values F_i with F_*=0, summing the inequalities along a path i->* yields -F_i at least the weight of that path. Thus F_i<=-ell_i=v_i for every i.

4. A finite-dimensional global interpolant.
Define on the real plane
H(g)=max_{i in I_n}{<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i+<xi_i,g-gamma_i>+||g-gamma_i||^2/2}.
This is the maximum of finitely many quadratics with Hessian equal to the identity. It is finite, continuous and 1-strongly convex. The inequality v_j-v_i>=<gamma_i,xi_j-xi_i>+||gamma_j-gamma_i||^2/2 is exactly the assertion that at g=gamma_i the j-th quadratic is at most the i-th quadratic. Thus
H(gamma_i)=<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i.
The i-th quadratic is active and has derivative xi_i at gamma_i. Its supporting affine function is below H, so xi_i belongs to the subdifferential of H at gamma_i.

Define f(x)=sup_g{<x,g>-H(g)}. For each fixed x, the maximizer g(x) exists and is unique: H(g)-<x,g> is continuous, strongly convex, and coercive because even any one term of the defining maximum has leading term ||g||^2/2. Optimality says x belongs to the subdifferential of H at g(x). Strong convexity implies that for all x,y,
<x-y,g(x)-g(y)>>=||g(x)-g(y)||^2.
To justify this implication, strong convexity and x in partial H(u) imply H(v)>=H(u)+<x,v-u>+||v-u||^2/2: combine H((1-t)u+tv)>=H(u)+t<x,v-u> with the strong-convexity upper bound along the segment, divide by t>0, and let t decrease to zero. Write this inequality for u=g(x),v=g(y) and in the reverse direction, then add. Cauchy--Schwarz gives ||g(x)-g(y)||<=||x-y||.
The definition of f yields, for every displacement d,
<g(x),d><=f(x+d)-f(x)<=<g(x+d),d>.
It follows that the difference between f(x+d)-f(x) and <g(x),d> lies in [0,||d||^2]. Therefore f is differentiable with gradient g(x), and that gradient is 1-Lipschitz. It is finite by existence of the maximizers and convex as a supremum of affine functions. The subgradient condition xi_i in partial H(gamma_i) gives g(xi_i)=gamma_i and f(xi_i)=v_i. At the optimum sample this gives f(0)=0 and grad f(0)=0, so convexity makes 0 a minimizer.

The prefix-polynomial identity Poly_{i+1}=(1-h_i z)Poly_i gives xi_{i+1}=xi_i-h_i gamma_i. Thus the interpolation is a legal GD trajectory, with ||xi_0||=1. Apply (1) in both directions between n and *:
v_n>=||gamma_n||^2/2,
v_n<=<gamma_n,xi_n>-||gamma_n||^2/2.
Since <gamma_n,xi_n>=alpha|Poly_n(z)|^2, these are the two bounds in (a).

5. A discrete area-energy inequality.
Consider any r>=2 complex numbers w_0,...,w_{r-1}, with w_r=w_0. Put
d_k=(1/r)sum_{j=0}^{r-1}w_j exp(-2pi i k j/r), 0<=k<r.
The finite geometric-sum identity reconstructs w_j=sum_k d_k exp(2pi i k j/r). The same identity, on expanding the quadratic sums, gives
sum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})=r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin(2pi k/r),
sum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2=4r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin^2(pi k/r).
The k=0 terms vanish. For 1<=k<r, the ratio of the absolute sine coefficient in the first sum to the coefficient in the second is (1/2)|cot(pi k/r)|<=cot(pi/r)/2. The triangle inequality proves
|sum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})| <= (cot(pi/r)/2)sum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2. (3)
This also covers r=2, where the left side and cot(pi/2) are zero.
Every simple cycle on I_n has length r<=n+2. The function cot(pi/r) increases as the integer r>=2 increases. From (3) and the defining inequality for Lens_n one gets kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C|. This proves (b).

6. Explicit real positive Chebyshev steps and their sum.
The recurrence for Cheb_j, the cosine addition formula, and induction give Cheb_j(cos t)=cos(jt). Its parity is the parity of j and its degree is j. Put m=2n+1. The odd polynomial Cheb_m(x) is divisible by x; hence (-1)^n Cheb_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is a real polynomial of degree n. Differentiating Cheb_m(cos t)=cos(mt) at t=pi/2 gives Cheb_m'(0)=(-1)^n m, so its value at z=0 is 1.
The positive roots of Cheb_m are cos((2k-1)pi/(2m)), k=1,...,n. Squaring them gives exactly the distinct roots lambda_k=cos^2((2k-1)pi/(2m)) in (0,1) of the degree-n polynomial. It follows from the value at zero that the polynomial equals product_k(1-z/lambda_k). Thus its reciprocal roots are precisely the stated positive schedule, regardless of their order.

Twice differentiating the cosine identity and eliminating the angle gives the polynomial identity
(1-x^2)Cheb_m''(x)-xCheb_m'(x)+m^2 Cheb_m(x)=0.
Differentiation at x=0 gives Cheb_m'''(0)=-(m^2-1)Cheb_m'(0). The coefficient of z in (-1)^n Cheb_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is consequently -(m^2-1)/6. The coefficient of z in the product of schedule factors is minus their sum. Hence s(Chi_n)=(m^2-1)/6=2n(n+1)/3.

7. The lens lies in an ellipse of small width.
Let n>=2, N=n+2, theta=pi/N, t=tan(theta)>0, and u=asinh(t/2)>0. For z=alpha+i beta in Lens_n,
0<=alpha<=1 and |beta|<=t alpha(1-alpha),
because alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=0 and dropping beta^2 only weakens the defining inequality. Put s=alpha(1-alpha), so 0<=s<=1/4. Since sinh(u)=t/2 and cosh(u)>=1,
(2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(u)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(u)
 <=1-4s+16s^2<=1. (4)

For completeness (4) implies there is a complex w with |Im w|<=u and z=(1+cos w)/2. If beta=0, take a real w with cos w=2alpha-1. If beta!=0, the continuous function
v -> (2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(v)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(v)
decreases from infinity as v increases from zero and is at most 1 at v=u. Choose v in (0,u] where its value is 1, and choose a real angle a with
cos a=(2alpha-1)/cosh(v), sin a=-2beta/sinh(v).
Then w=a+i v has cos w=2alpha-1+2i beta.
A square root of z is cos(w/2). The cosine identity for Cheb_m holds for complex arguments as well, because it follows from polynomial recurrence and the complex cosine addition formula. Thus
|z| |Poly_n(z)|^2
 =|Cheb_m(sqrt(z))|^2/m^2
 =|cos(mw/2)|^2/m^2
 <=cosh^2(m u/2)/m^2. (5)
The polynomial interpretation covers z=0 as well; both sides of the equality before the bound are zero there.

The inequality asinh(t/2)<=t/2 follows by integrating its derivative, which is at most 1 on the nonnegative line. The function tan is convex on [0,pi/4], so it lies below its chord and tan(theta)<=4theta/pi=4/N. Therefore m u/2<=m t/4<=m/N<2. Combining (5) with (a) and alpha<=|z| proves
Gap_{Chi_n}(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2
 <=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2<=2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.

8. Canonical Huber witness and the exact-profile comparison.
For any h>=0 let S=s(h) and delta=1/(2S+1). In dimension one define
f_delta(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=delta, and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise.
The matching derivative is x clipped to [-delta,delta], hence is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The function is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth and minimized at 0. Starting from x_0=1, every partial sum of h lies in [0,S]. Induction gives x_i=1-delta sum_{t<i}h_t>=1-delta S=(S+1)/(2S+1)>=delta. Thus every gradient equals delta and
2f_delta(x_n)=2delta(1-delta S)-delta^2=1/(2S+1).
Multiplying either this function or the interpolant from part 4 by L produces the same normalized error for the specified arbitrary L>0. Consequently each term defining V_lens(h) is a legal lower-bound witness for W_n(h), proving V_lens(h)<=W_n(h).

For Chi_n the Huber term is
1/(1+4n(n+1)/3)<=3/(4n^2).
Part 7 bounds its lens term by 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2. Since 2cosh^2(2)>=2>3/4, both terms are at most 2cosh^2(2)/n^2.
Verified predecessor b98bb807d514e80d states, for exactly the project phi/psi recursions and the project normalization, that B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1<=1+4n^p with p=log_2 3<2. Multiply the preceding bound on V_lens by B_n to obtain the displayed ratio estimate. It tends to zero because n^{-2}->0 and n^{p-2}->0. This uses no assumed limit for S_bullet(n)/n^rho and no numerical estimate of the profile constant.

Only the explicitly restricted witness family was upper bounded. None of the calculations gives an upper bound for the unrestricted W_n on the displayed schedules.

## DOC-002580 — project/research/max2_order_sensitivity.md

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002580 -->
# Exact order sensitivity beyond the terminal schedule polynomial

Status: complete exploratory derivation, not submitted to the verifier. This is a counterexample to a proposed algebraic reduction, not to the project's asymptotic conjecture.

For every H>2,

    W_2(H,1)=(H-1)^2/3
    > W_2(1,H), since W_2(1,H)<=1/3+(H^2-2H)/4.

In particular W_2(3,1)=4/3 while W_2(1,3)<=13/12.
The two schedules have identical terminal polynomial (1-z)(1-Hz) and identical total step sum. Thus neither the full terminal polynomial, nor that polynomial together with the sum, determines the full smooth-convex worst-case objective.

All proofs below apply in arbitrary finite dimension. Normalize L=1, x_*=0, f_*=0, ||x_0||=1 by translating and rescaling the function and coordinates; the ratio defining W is unchanged.

## Elementary inequalities

For convex differentiable 1-smooth f,

    f(u)>=f(v)+<grad f(v),u-v>+(1/2)||grad f(u)-grad f(v)||^2.       (I)

For a self-contained proof, put k(x)=f(x)-<grad f(v),x>. Convexity and grad k(v)=0 show v minimizes k. Integrating the 1-Lipschitz gradient on a segment gives k(x-grad k(x))<=k(x)-||grad k(x)||^2/2. Combine with k(v)<=k(x-grad k(x)) and rearrange.

Adding both orientations of (I) gives
<grad f(u)-grad f(v),u-v>>=||grad f(u)-grad f(v)||^2.
Taking v=0 shows <grad f(u),u>>=||grad f(u)||^2, and Cauchy--Schwarz yields ||grad f(u)||<=||u||.

## A unit GD step

Start at an arbitrary vector x, put u=grad f(x), y=x-u and v=grad f(y). Inequality (I) for (x,y) gives

    f(x)-f(y)>=(||u||^2+||v||^2)/2.

The optimum pairs give f(x)<=<u,x>-||u||^2/2 and
f(y)<=<v,x-u>-||v||^2/2. Therefore

    f(y)<=<u,x>-||u||^2-||v||^2/2,
    f(y)<=<v,x>-<v,u>-||v||^2/2.

Average these two upper bounds. Writing w=(u+v)/2 gives

    f(y)<=<w,x>-(3/2)||w||^2-(1/8)||u-v||^2
         <=||x||^2/6.                                             (U)

Also f(y)>=||v||^2/2. Combining this with the first upper bound above yields

    ||v||^2<=<u,x>-||u||^2
             =||x||^2/4-||u-x/2||^2<=||x||^2/4.                   (G)

## Upper bound for (1,H)

After its first step, (U) and (G) give f(x_1)<=1/6 and ||grad f(x_1)||^2<=1/4.
The integrated Lipschitz-gradient inequality gives

    f(x_1-H grad f(x_1))
      <=f(x_1)+(H^2/2-H)||grad f(x_1)||^2.

For H>=2 the coefficient is nonnegative, so

    W_2(1,H)<=1/3+(H^2-2H)/4.                                    (F)

## Exact value for (H,1)

For H>=2, put u=grad f(x_0). Cocoercivity against the minimizer gives

    ||x_0-Hu||^2
      =1-2H<u,x_0>+H^2||u||^2
      <=1+H(H-2)||u||^2
      <=1+H(H-2)=(H-1)^2.

Apply (U) to the following unit step:

    W_2(H,1)<=(H-1)^2/3.                                         (R)

Equality is realized in dimension one. Set delta=(H-1)/3>0 and define

    f(x)=x^2/2                       if x>=-delta,
         -delta*x-delta^2/2          if x<=-delta.

The pieces and their derivatives match. Its derivative is max(x,-delta), which is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz; therefore f is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth. It has minimizer 0 and minimum zero.

Starting from x_0=1, the H-step gives x_1=1-H=-3delta. The unit step gives x_2=-2delta. Since x_2<=-delta,

    f(x_2)=2delta^2-delta^2/2=3delta^2/2.

The normalized terminal error is 2 f(x_2)=3delta^2=(H-1)^2/3. This proves equality in (R).

Finally the difference between (R)'s exact value and (F)'s upper bound equals H(H-2)/12, strictly positive for H>2. The result permits arbitrary real H>2 and does not use numerical certificates or computational evidence.

## Scope

This rules out an exact order-blind description of W using only the terminal scalar polynomial. It does not rule out a weaker permutation-invariant lower bound, and it does not by itself decide the asymptotic minimax target.

## DOC-002581 — project/research/max2_penultimate_flow_descent.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002581 -->
# A concrete final-step descent from a penultimate star-outgoing multiplier

Status: new self-contained candidate, unverified. No new verification allocation is assumed. This addresses one real component of the general-q descent problem; it does not prove that every q>tau tuple has such a component.

## 1. Input and exact schedule change

Normalize L=1 and f_*=0. Let h be any nonnegative schedule of length n>=1, with final step b=h_{n-1}. Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0, and the standard full upper certificate

    q||x_0-x_*||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij = M >=0,

where M is a PSD quadratic form after the exact GD substitutions, lambda_ij>=0, and the function-value coefficients cancel. The index * has x_*=g_*=0 and f_*=0. The result applies to every full feasible certificate, regardless of stationarity or Huber tightness.

Suppose

    nu=lambda_{*,n-1}>0.

For any 0<epsilon<=nu/(nu+2), put c=1-epsilon and replace ONLY the last step by

    b_new=c b+epsilon.

Then the new predetermined GD schedule has the rigorous uniform bound

    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new) <= c q < q.                    (D)

At the largest allowed epsilon this reads

    b_new=(2b+nu)/(nu+2),       q_new=2q/(nu+2).

The strict inequality is meaningful whenever q>0; every valid finite-horizon objective upper bound has q>0 by the canonical Huber witness.

## 2. Direct proof for all smooth convex functions

Fix any legal function and initial point, using the old and new schedules with the same prefix. Write

    x=x_{n-1}, g=grad f(x),
    z=x-b g                    (old endpoint),
    y=x-g                      (unit-step point),
    z_new=c z+epsilon y         (new endpoint),
    delta=f(x)-||g||^2/2 >=0.

Because Q_{*,n-1}=-delta, isolating this term in the original full PSD certificate yields

    2f(z)+nu delta <= q||x_0-x_*||^2.                        (A)

The remaining terms have the correct signs: the PSD quadratic form is nonnegative and every other -lambda_ij Q_ij is nonnegative.

The unit-step descent inequality gives f(y)<=delta. Convexity then yields

    2f(z_new) <=2c f(z)+2epsilon f(y)
                <=2c f(z)+2epsilon delta
                <=c[2f(z)+nu delta]
                <=c q||x_0-x_*||^2,

where 2epsilon<=c nu was the only restriction on epsilon. This proves (D) without selecting any hard function, differentiating W, or assuming a stationary worst-case primal.

The unit-step inequality can itself be derived from a single smooth-convex interpolation constraint:

    Q_{y,x}=f(y)-f(x)+<grad f(y),g>
              +||grad f(y)-g||^2/2
           =f(y)-delta+||grad f(y)||^2/2 <=0.

Thus the proof uses precisely the full smooth-convex class and is dimension-independent.

## 3. Explicit nonnegative-multiplier, PSD certificate

The construction is stronger than a sign argument: it gives an explicit enlarged interpolation certificate for the changed schedule. The two auxiliary points are the old endpoint z and the unit-step point y. The new algorithm still uses n steps and no auxiliary oracle calls; auxiliary points appear only in the proof and are defined from the same penultimate point and gradient.

Let g_z,g_y,g_new denote their actual gradients, and put

    d=c nu-2epsilon >=0.

Keep c times every old multiplier except lambda_{*,n-1}; assign d to Q_{*,n-1}. Add the new nonnegative multipliers

    2c       on Q_{z_new,z},
    2epsilon on Q_{z_new,y},
    2epsilon on Q_{y,x}.

All function-value terms cancel against the new objective -2f(z_new). After substituting z_new=c z+epsilon y and y=x-g, the full polynomial identity is

    c q||x_0-x_*||^2-2f(z_new)
      +c sum_{(i,j)!=(star,n-1)}lambda_ij Q_ij
      +d Q_{*,n-1}
      +2c Q_{z_new,z}+2epsilon Q_{z_new,y}
      +2epsilon Q_{y,x}

    = c M
      +c||g_new-g_z||^2
      +epsilon||g_new-g_y||^2
      +epsilon||g_y||^2.                                    (PSD)

The right side is an explicit PSD quadratic form, including the original full matrix M, not only its row sums or energy trace. The left side uses nonnegative multipliers throughout. All interpolation data concern the same globally legal function; no incompatible local transcript is glued in.

To check the identity, combine the first two new inequalities with weights c,epsilon. Their gradient-position terms cancel because z_new=c z+epsilon y. Then combine Q_{y,x}=f(y)-delta+||g_y||^2/2. The coefficient d is exactly what balances the old nu delta term.

The displayed certificate uses auxiliary analysis points. It is not asserted to be an explicit parameter-by-parameter map into the minimal n+2-point dual. Its full PSD identity proves the actual uniform upper bound directly, so it is already sufficient for a global-minimizer contradiction. When the new steps are positive, exact finite interpolation duality also gives a minimal-format certificate at its true worst-case value, but that existential statement is not needed for (D).

## 4. Consequence at global minimizers

Suppose h is a globally minimizing length-n schedule and its full certificate is tight: q=W_n(h)=W_n^*. If lambda_{*,n-1}>0, formula (D) constructs a length-n nonnegative predetermined schedule with strictly smaller true worst-case value. This contradicts global minimality.

Consequently EVERY tight full certificate at a global minimizer must satisfy

    lambda_{*,n-1}=0.                                      (V)

The contradiction uses an actual globally valid changed algorithm, so it is not merely a stationarity-implies-tightness shortcut. It does not need q=tau or a bound on the total step mass. It also handles a final step already equal to one: then the schedule does not change and (D) would improve the allegedly tight q itself.

This conclusion leaves lambda_{*,i} for i<n-1, lambda_{*,n}, and the PSD Schur-complement defect completely open. In particular it does not establish Huber tightness of a global minimizer.

## 5. Exact checks and the remaining q>tau case

For the one-step certificate at h_0=1+a, a>1, take

    q=a^2, lambda_{0,*}=2a^2,
    lambda_{*,0}=2a^2-2, lambda_{1,0}=2,

all other multipliers zero. Its exact slack is

    a^2||x_0-g_0||^2+||a g_0+g_1||^2,

and f(x)=x^2/2 attains q. The new formula gives c=1/a^2, h_new=1+1/a and the certified bound q_new=1. This is strictly smaller than a^2. The formula preserves nonnegativity and PSD through the explicit identity above; smaller epsilon gives a continuous descent.

The hypothesis nu>0 cannot be silently replaced by q>tau. For 1/2<a<1, the tight one-step certificate at h_0=1+a has q=a^2>tau=1/(2a+3), but no star-outgoing multipliers:

    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a.

Its gradient block is [[2a,1],[1,1]], its star-directed vector is mu=(2a,2(1-a)), and its full Schur complement is

    Z=(2a-1)[[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]] >=0.

The quadratic matches q. Thus the remaining non-Huber defect can be carried entirely by nonzero PSD slack even when all star-outgoing weights vanish. This example is not globally optimal; it is a hostile test of extending (V) into an unsupported general tightness claim.

## 6. Related terminal extension

If a certificate instead has lambda_{*,n}=nu>0, the same argument applied to an appended step gives, for 0<=epsilon<=nu/(nu+2),

    W_{n+1}(h,epsilon) <= (1-epsilon)q.

Here use the convex combination of x_n and x_n-grad f(x_n), and isolate the terminal residual delta_n=f_n-||g_n||^2/2 in the old certificate. This is a length-(n+1) improvement and must not be counted as a length-n contradiction. It may be useful for upper-bound composition, but no asymptotic comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang profile follows automatically.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002582 -->
# General polynomial Gram data and the failure of spectral moments

Status: exploratory algebraic formulation, not a verified fact and not a target proof. Normalize L=1, x_*=g_*=0 and ||x_0||=1.

## Lossless formal-polynomial representation

First remove zero steps from a nonnegative schedule: they repeat the same point, value and gradient and do not change the final iterate. Let m be the number of remaining positive steps. Write them as h_0,...,h_{m-1}; define

    P_0(z)=1, P_i(z)=product_{t<i}(1-h_t z), 1<=i<=m.

Let V=R[z]_{<=m+1}. The list

    1, z P_0(z), z P_1(z), ..., z P_m(z)

is a basis of V: its degrees are 0,1,...,m+1 and its leading coefficients are nonzero because each retained h_t is positive.

For any actual legal GD transcript, the unique linear map T from V into its ambient Euclidean space satisfying

    T(1)=x_0, T(z P_i)=g_i

also satisfies T(P_i)=x_i. Indeed the polynomial telescoping identity is

    P_i=1-sum_{t<i} h_t z P_t,

and its image under T is precisely the GD recursion. Thus the PSD bilinear form

    K(U,V)=<T(U),T(V)>

contains all transcript Gram data, with K(1,1)=1.

Conversely, for any PSD symmetric bilinear form K on V, its quotient by the null space is a Euclidean space of dimension at most m+2. Denote polynomial equivalence classes by brackets and take x_i=[P_i], g_i=[z P_i]. The same polynomial identity gives the exact GD recursion. The optimum sample is x_*=g_*=0. Define

    Q_ij=K(z P_j,P_i-P_j)
          +(1/2)K(z P_i-z P_j,z P_i-z P_j),

with P_*=0 and z P_*=0. These samples admit convex 1-smooth interpolating values if and only if every directed cycle on {*,0,...,m} has nonpositive total Q weight, with Q_ij attached to j->i.

The sufficiency can be seen explicitly. Let ell_i be the maximum Q weight of a path i->*, with the empty path allowed at *. Every such maximum is over finitely many simple paths, since all cycles are nonpositive. Put F_i=-ell_i. Then F_i-F_j>=Q_ij, and these are the smooth-convex interpolation inequalities. The conjugate of

    H(g)=max_i { <g_i,x_i>-F_i+<x_i,g-g_i>+(1/2)||g-g_i||^2 }

is a globally finite convex 1-smooth interpolant. Its gradient is zero at the optimum sample. All candidate function values are at most F_i, by summing their inequalities along paths to *.

Hence the original W for this fixed schedule equals

    2 sup_K (-ell_m),

where K ranges over the PSD forms with K(1,1)=1 and all the displayed cycle inequalities. Zero steps can now be restored exactly, without a density argument or any restriction on real steps. This is an algebraic rewriting of the full interpolation problem, not a simplification of its minimax strength.

The matrix of K in the monomial basis has entries M_rs=K(z^r,z^s), 0<=r,s<=m+1. These form a two-index PSD array. There is no requirement that they depend only on r+s.

## A legal Huber transcript already needs non-Hankel entries

Take one step h_0=1 and the usual Huber function with threshold 1/3:

    f(x)=x^2/2                           for |x|<=1/3,
         |x|/3-1/18                     for |x|>=1/3.

From x_0=1, the transcript is x_1=2/3 and g_0=g_1=1/3. The function is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with minimizer 0. Its terminal value is f(x_1)=1/6, so normalized error is 1/3.

The representing map satisfies T(1)=1, T(z)=1/3 and
T(z-z^2)=1/3; hence T(z^2)=0. In the basis (1,z,z^2),

    M=[[1,1/3,0],[1/3,1/9,0],[0,0,0]].

It is a rank-one PSD matrix. It is not Hankel:
M_02=0 while M_11=1/9.

For any scalar spectral measure mu on R, a form of the type
K(U,V)=integral U(lambda)V(lambda) dmu(lambda) would instead have
M_02=integral lambda^2 dmu=M_11. Thus even this ordinary one-dimensional legal Huber transcript cannot be represented by scalar quadratic spectral moments.

There is a further issue beyond self-adjointness. Multiplication by z does not descend to the Euclidean quotient for this example. The polynomial z-1/3 is null because T(z-1/3)=0, but

    T(z(z-1/3))=T(z^2-z/3)=-1/9,

which is not zero. Thus the null space of K is not stable under multiplication by z. No single fixed linear operator, normal or nonnormal, represents this exact transcript on its one-dimensional realized space.

A scalar moment or fixed-matrix ansatz can therefore silently exclude legal smooth-convex behavior in two different ways: imposing Hankel symmetry, and requiring shift-invariance of null relations. The general bilinear polynomial form avoids both restrictions, at the price of retaining the full interpolation problem.

## What remains unresolved

The representation identifies the extra algebraic data exactly, but no lower-bound selection of K has been proved that matches the exact S_bullet profile uniformly over schedules. Choosing K by optimizing the full displayed interpolation problem is equivalent to the original fixed-schedule performance problem, so it is not an independent mechanism for the target comparison.


<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002582 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002583 -->
# Complete-certificate obstruction to the unchanged remote-edge extension

Status: UNVERIFIED exact counterexample. This refutes one specified extension
of the suffix transport, not the existence of some descent and not Huber
tightness at global minimizers. It directly addresses TASK.md's unrestricted
two-step-suffix test.

## 1. A complete tight certificate

Take n=3, L=1, h=(2,9/4,3), q=25/4, and zeta=sqrt(78)/5.
Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, with
x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All unspecified multipliers below are zero:

mu_0=lambda_(0,star)=25/2+5sqrt(78)/8,
mu_1=lambda_(1,star)=5sqrt(78)/8,
nu_0=lambda_(star,0)=5sqrt(78)/4+1,
nu_1=lambda_(star,1)=9/2,
nu_2=lambda_(star,2)=5,

lambda_10=25/2-5sqrt(78)/8,
lambda_21=8, lambda_32=2,
lambda_02=5, lambda_20=4.                            (1)

All are nonnegative; lambda_10>0 because sqrt(78)<9.
The finite flow equations can be checked explicitly:
at0, out=in=35/2+5sqrt(78)/8;
at1, out=in=25/2;
at2, out=in=12;
at3, out-in=2.

For independent formal vectors (x_0,g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3), define

R0=x_0-g_0,
U=g_0+g_1,
V=g_2+(5/4)g_1,
Z=g_3+2g_2.

The FULL matrix has the exact sum-of-squares representation

M=q||x_0||^2-2f_3+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
 =(25/4)||R0-(sqrt(78)/20)U||^2
   +(1/32)||U-16V||^2+||Z||^2.                     (2)

Here GD substitution and the displayed flows cancel every f coefficient.
Thus this is a full PSD certificate, with no omitted prefix or remote entry.

A short derivation of (2) also checks every coefficient. At the first step2,
the multiplier set
mu_0=2+zeta/2, mu_1=zeta/2, nu_0=zeta,
lambda_10=2-zeta/2
gives the identity
||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda Q
 =||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2.
Multiply it by25/4. For a step of length1+r, r>=1, direct expansion gives

2r^2 f_i-2f_(i+1)+2Q_(i+1,i)+2(r^2-1)Q_(star,i)
 =||r g_i+g_(i+1)||^2.

Add four times this identity with i=1,r=5/4 and once with i=2,r=2.
This gives a base certificate with the same q and the same multipliers
as (1), except lambda_02=lambda_20=0, nu_0=5sqrt(78)/4,
and nu_2=6. Its slack is

M_base=(25/4)[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]
        +4||V||^2+||Z||^2.                         (3)

Finally add the exact zero-flow multiplier change
5Q_02+4Q_20+Q_(star,0)-Q_(star,2).
Its quadratic form is
-5||U||^2-<U,V>+4||V||^2.
Combining with (3), and using
(25/4)(1-zeta^2/16)=161/32, gives (2).

The certificate is TIGHT on the globally legal one-dimensional function
f(x)=x^2/2, with x_0=1. Its GD data are

(x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),
(g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),
(f_0,f_1,f_2,f_3)=(1/2,1/2,25/32,25/8).

Every Q_ij, including star pairs, is zero on these data, (2) vanishes,
and 2f_3=q. Thus W_3(h)=25/4. This is a complete legal matching
primal, not merely a PSD principal block.

The selected star multiplier is nu_(n-2)=nu_1=9/2>0.
There is a genuine remote prefix-to-suffix edge lambda_02=5.

## 2. The precise extension being refuted

Apply the previously valid c_2=0 two-step transport to k=1 while
leaving the remote edge unchanged. In this certificate,

c_1=mu_2+lambda_20+lambda_21=12,
c_2=mu_3+lambda_30+lambda_31=0,
c_3=lambda_32=2.

For any 0<eta<=9/2, the proposed extension changes

h'_1=(27+eta)/(12+eta),    h'_0=2, h'_2=3,
lambda'_21=8+eta,
nu'_1=9/2-eta, nu'_2=5+eta,                          (4)

and leaves every other multiplier and q unchanged. All steps and
multipliers remain nonnegative, and every flow equation remains exact.
Thus the only issue is the full PSD condition.

Direct expansion gives

M_new-M
 =eta||g_2||^2
   +[25 eta/(4(12+eta))]<g_0,g_1>.                  (5)

The second term is the previously absent remote cut contribution
(h_1-h'_1)lambda_02<g_0,g_1>. It cannot be discarded.

## 3. Exact negative direction in the OLD full kernel

Use one-dimensional formal vectors with

g_0=47/40,  g_1=-7/40,  g_2=9/32,  g_3=-9/16,
x_0=47/40+sqrt(78)/20.                              (6)

These need not be a legal new primal: they are a legitimate test of
PSD, which is required on ALL formal vectors.

Here U=1, V=1/16, Z=0, and R0=sqrt(78)/20.
Consequently the OLD FULL slack (2) is exactly zero.
On (6), equation(5) is exactly

M_new=eta(81 eta-344)/[1024(12+eta)].                (7)

It is negative for every 0<eta<344/81. In particular eta=1 is allowed
by (4), all actual multipliers remain nonnegative, but
M_new=-263/13312<0. There is therefore no sufficiently small positive
move of this unchanged transport that preserves PSD.

On the actual matching quadratic data from Section1, in contrast,
(5)/eta is 25/16-25/[4(12+eta)]>0. Thus checking only the
matching primal would falsely suggest a gain. The second full-kernel
direction is essential.

## 4. Scope and a different successful action on this example

This is NOT a stationary tuple and NOT a globally optimal schedule.
It does not refute some other simultaneous descent. Indeed one exists
with a different certificate re-selection.

First continuously undo the last zero-flow adjustment in Section1,
moving from (1) to its base certificate (3) at the SAME h,q.
Every intermediate actual multiplier is nonnegative, and every
intermediate slack is a convex combination of the PSD matrices (2)
and (3). The same legal quadratic remains matching throughout.
The base slack (3) has no extra kernel beyond that quadratic direction,
because zeta^2/16=39/200<1.

Now use the schedule h'=(2,28/13,3), corresponding to eta=1 in (4).
An explicit full certificate with value q'=900/169<25/4 is obtained
by keeping the first-step multiplier pattern
(mu_0,mu_1,nu_0,lambda_10)
=(2+zeta/2,zeta/2,zeta,2-zeta/2),
multiplying it by q'=4(15/13)^2, and then adding the two
function-growth identities from Section1:
four times the i=1 identity with r=15/13, and once the i=2
identity with r=2.

Its slack is

q'[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]
 +4||(15/13)g_1+g_2||^2+||2g_2+g_3||^2 >=0,

all multiplier signs hold since15/13>1 and zeta<4, and its
function-value flow is exactly2e_3. The quadratic again attains q'.
This is a real fixed-length improvement for this example.

The obstruction therefore distinguishes two mechanisms:
the same sparse transport can fail at an unnecessarily degenerate
optimal dual certificate, whereas reselecting the dual certificate
inside the fixed-h optimum face can make a different descent available.
No theorem is claimed that such re-selection removes remote edges
for every schedule, nor that the maximal-rank dual face always has
the needed kernel structure.

Next action: analyze certificate re-selection together with the
inactive-interpolation costs before attempting a universal descent.
Do not add a remote-edge-absence hypothesis and call the original
extension repaired. The eventual exact-profile mass-cap issue remains
separate even if global Huber tightness were established.

## 5. A continuous repair that RETAINS the remote edges

The same obstruction can be repaired by a different, explicit simultaneous
reweighting, without first setting the remote multipliers to zero.
This supplies a concrete alternative mechanism on this example only.

For 1<=r<=5/4, take h(r)=(2,1+r,3), q(r)=4r^2, retain
zeta=sqrt(78)/5, and set z(r)=64r^2/25. Choose

mu_0=4r^2(2+zeta/2),  mu_1=2r^2 zeta,
nu_0=4r^2 zeta+z(r)(r-1),
nu_1=8(r^2-1),        nu_2=6-z(r)(r-1),

lambda_10=4r^2(2-zeta/2),  lambda_21=8, lambda_32=2,
lambda_02=r z(r),          lambda_20=z(r).            (8)

All unlisted multipliers are zero. They are nonnegative throughout the
interval: z<=4, r-1<=1/4, so nu_2>=5; and zeta<4.
Direct substitution gives out-in=0 at0,1,2 and2 at3. At r=5/4
these are exactly (1), and both remote edges stay positive for
every r in the interval.

Use R0=x_0-g_0, U=g_0+g_1, V_r=g_2+r g_1, Z=g_3+2g_2.
The full slack is

M(r)=4r^2||R0-(zeta/4)U||^2+||Z||^2
     +A(r)||U||^2-(r-1)z(r)<U,V_r>+(4+z(r))||V_r||^2,

A(r)=r^2(161-128r)/50.                              (9)

To derive (9), scale the first-step identity by4r^2, use the two
function-growth identities with factors r and2, and add the
finite/star flow change corresponding to
C_02=r z/2, C_20=z/2.
Its quadratic contribution is
-z[r||U||^2+(r-1)<U,V_r>-||V_r||^2].
Completing the same first square yields (9).

The remaining two-by-two matrix in (U,V_r) has positive A(r), and
its determinant is exactly

r^2(5-4r)(512r^3+1664r^2+16r+3220)/1250 >=0.          (10)

Thus the FULL M(r) is PSD. It has a positive determinant in this
remaining block when r<5/4, so the extraneous kernel responsible
for (7) is removed. The legal quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 follows
(1,-1,r,-2r), satisfies every active interpolation equality, and
attains q(r)=4r^2. Consequently (8)--(10) give the exact identity

W_3(2,1+r,3)=4r^2  for 1<=r<=5/4.

In particular moving r downward from5/4 is a continuous strict
same-length descent starting from the original degenerate certificate,
with nonnegative flow and both remote finite edges retained.
The failed sparse update and this successful update start from the
SAME full certificate. Their difference is the simultaneous scaling
of the prefix-source coefficients and the remote weights (quadratic
and cubic in r), which changes the full kernel correctly.

This does not assert that a comparable homogeneous reweighting exists
for an arbitrary near-optimal schedule. It identifies a genuinely
different concrete mechanism from keeping prefix multipliers fixed.

## DOC-002584 — project/research/max2_star_flow_transfer.md
