<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002584 -->
# Explicit forward transfer of optimum-outgoing multipliers

Status: unverified new certificate operation. It preserves the standard full matrix and nonnegative interpolation multipliers under the stated capacity condition.

## 1. One adjacent transfer

Use the signed smooth-convex quantities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,

with the optimum sample * equal to zero. Fix a positive step h_i=a>0, so x_{i+1}=x_i-a g_i. A direct expansion gives the polynomial identity

    Q_{*,i+1}+(1/a)Q_{i+1,i}+((1-a)/a)Q_{i,i+1}
       -Q_{*,i}
       =(1/a)||g_{i+1}-(1-a)g_i||^2.                       (I)

For clarity, write delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2. Then
Q_{i+1,i}=delta_{i+1}-delta_i+||g_{i+1}||^2+(a-1)<g_i,g_{i+1}>,
and
Q_{i,i+1}+Q_{i+1,i}=(1-a)||g_i||^2+||g_{i+1}||^2+(a-2)<g_i,g_{i+1}>.
Substitution yields (I), including all coefficients and signs.

Take any full feasible upper certificate with multipliers lambda and PSD slack M. Choose t>0 satisfying

    t<=lambda_{*,i},
    t<=a lambda_{i,i+1}/(a-1)       when a>1.                (C)

When 0<a<=1 the second condition is absent. Change only these four multipliers:

    lambda'_{*,i}=lambda_{*,i}-t,
    lambda'_{*,i+1}=lambda_{*,i+1}+t,
    lambda'_{i+1,i}=lambda_{i+1,i}+t/a,
    lambda'_{i,i+1}=lambda_{i,i+1}+t(1-a)/a.                (T)

All multipliers remain nonnegative by (C), and each node's flow divergence is unchanged. The schedule h and certificate level q are unchanged. The FULL slack changes by

    M'=M+(t/a)||g_{i+1}-(1-a)g_i||^2 >=0.                  (P)

Thus (T) is an explicit feasible motion in the original certificate variables, not merely a reduced row-sum operation. It neither assumes stationarity nor removes any PSD constraint.

For a<=1, the transfer adds or preserves the forward-edge multiplier. For a>1 it consumes exactly t(a-1)/a of the existing forward edge lambda_{i,i+1}. At a=1 the added square is t||g_{i+1}||^2 and no forward capacity is consumed.

## 2. Transport across a whole interval

Suppose lambda_{*,i}>0 for some i<n-1. Assume that for every j=i,...,n-2, either

    0<h_j<=1,

or

    h_j>1 and lambda_{j,j+1}>0.                             (A)

Choose t>0 at most lambda_{*,i} and at most every finite capacity

    h_j lambda_{j,j+1}/(h_j-1),  j in [i,n-2] with h_j>1.

There are finitely many strictly positive capacities, so such a t exists. Apply (T) successively at j=i,...,n-2, always transferring the same amount t. Intermediate optimum-outgoing weights receive t and then pass it on, while the consumed adjacent forward edges are distinct. The resulting standard-format certificate has

    lambda'_{*,i}=lambda_{*,i}-t,
    lambda'_{*,n-1}=lambda_{*,n-1}+t>0,

and unchanged other optimum-outgoing weights, schedule, objective level and flow divergence. Its full matrix is

    M'=M+sum_{j=i}^{n-2}(t/h_j)
             ||g_{j+1}-(1-h_j)g_j||^2 >=0.                 (B)

No arbitrary chronological crossing edge has been discarded; the operation modifies only the stated adjacent multipliers and retains the rest of the original matrix exactly.

## 3. A resulting actual descent of the algorithm

Write nu=lambda'_{*,n-1}>0 and b=h_{n-1}. Isolating Q_{*,n-1}=-(f_{n-1}-||g_{n-1}||^2/2) in the transferred certificate gives

    2f_n+nu delta_{n-1}<=q||x_0-x_*||^2.

At x=x_{n-1}, the unit-step point y=x-g_{n-1} satisfies f(y)<=delta_{n-1}. The old endpoint is z=x-b g_{n-1}. For epsilon=nu/(nu+2), c=1-epsilon, the new final step b_new=c b+epsilon gives z_new=c z+epsilon y. Convexity yields

    2f(z_new)<=2c f(z)+2epsilon delta_{n-1}
                =c[2f(z)+nu delta_{n-1}]
                <=c q||x_0-x_*||^2.

Thus the concrete predetermined schedule with final step

    b_new=(2b+nu)/(2+nu)

has the strict uniform bound

    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new)<=2q/(2+nu)<q.

This is a genuine fixed-length improvement. The transfer phase stayed within the minimal full interpolation certificate, and the final convexity argument is valid for the same function in every instance. If desired, its explicit enlarged PSD certificate is obtained by adding the old endpoint and unit-step point as auxiliary analysis samples; its identity is given in research/max2_penultimate_flow_descent.md.

## 4. A forced blocking pattern at global minimizers

For a globally minimizing positive schedule and ANY tight full certificate, lambda_{*,n-1}=0. Moreover, if lambda_{*,i}>0 with i<n-1, then there must exist at least one j in [i,n-2] such that

    h_j>1 and lambda_{j,j+1}=0.                             (Block)

Otherwise (A) would transport positive weight to the penultimate point and the preceding explicit schedule change would strictly improve the global value.

This is a necessary pattern in the actual full certificate. It does not say that every star-outgoing weight vanishes: an overrelaxed step with a zero forward edge can block this particular transfer. The final weight lambda_{*,n} is also outside the fixed-length transport argument.

## 5. Tightness and matching primal data

If the original q is tight at the fixed h, every matching legal primal remains matching after any feasible transfer: h and q have not changed and the new certificate is globally valid. Because M' is the sum of M and the nonnegative squares in (B), each transferred square must vanish on that matching primal:

    g_{j+1}=(1-h_j)g_j

at every transfer edge. The new positive star weights also require delta_j=0 wherever they are placed. These are consequences of the full certificate and primal complementarity, not assumptions used to discard other inequalities.

This explains the restrictive nature of the transport. In particular, the earlier nonlinear-suffix counterexample does not invalidate (I): its relevant tight-certificate capacity conditions are not supplied. Conversely, it remains invalid to bypass a zero forward edge merely by claiming that the composed objective is convex.

## 6. Scope of the new mechanism

The operation supplies a concrete way to exploit some earlier optimum-outgoing weights while preserving PSD and every multiplier sign. It isolates exact barriers h_j>1, lambda_{j,j+1}=0 rather than an unspecified missing separator.

It still does not show that a q>tau global minimizer must have a transferable weight, or that the full Schur defect can always remove a blocking edge. Those are distinct open structural questions. No implication to the exact profile U_n^ZJ is asserted.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002585 -->
# Global minimizers and a stationary energy identity for full PEP certificates

Status: exploratory, unverified proof package. No extra verifier submission is authorized. The selected initial submission was used for fact dfde2fa326c908bf. This document does not establish the exact-profile target.

## 1. Claim under investigation

For each fixed horizon n>=1, the project minimization over h in [0,infinity)^n has a global minimizer, and every global minimizer has strictly positive steps. At any such minimizer there exist a full interpolation upper certificate (q,lambda), a legal primal Gram matrix G and values f_i, attaining q=W_n^*, for which

    <g_t,z_t>=0, 0<=t<n,                                      (S)
    q=(1/2) sum_{i,j} lambda_ij ||g_i-g_j||^2.                 (E)

Here the Euclidean vectors realize G, x_*=g_*=0, ||x_0||=1, and

    z_t=sum_{i>t}(mu_i+sum_{j<=t}lambda_ij)g_i
        -sum_{i<=t}(sum_{j>t}lambda_ij)g_i,                    (Z)
    mu_i=lambda_{i,*}.

Every unstarred sum ranges over 0,...,n; directed pairs are distinct. The assertion retains the full certificate PSD constraint and the full primal interpolation constraints. It does not claim that these displayed identities alone characterize global minimizers.

The proof below supplies the compactness, zero-step exclusion, strong-duality and multiplier-regularity arguments rather than assuming interiority or nonsingular multipliers.

## 2. Fixed-horizon compactness and positive optimal steps

Normalize L=1, x_*=0 and f_*=0. For any fixed finite h, the estimates ||g_i||<=||x_i|| and ||x_{i+1}||<= (1+h_i)||x_i|| imply W_n(h)<=prod_i(1+h_i)^2. Every fixed legal function and initial point has an error continuous in h, by continuity of its gradient. A supremum of continuous functions is lower semicontinuous, so W_n is lower semicontinuous.

Write Poly_n(z)=prod_{t<n}(1-h_t z). For n+1 distinct nodes a_k=k/(n+1), 1<=k<=n+1, the quadratic f(x)=a_k x^2/2 from x_0=1 gives

    a_k Poly_n(a_k)^2 <= W_n(h).

Define the Lagrange polynomials L_k(z)=prod_{l!=k}(z-a_l)/(a_k-a_l). On the sublevel W_n(h)<=1, interpolation of a polynomial of degree at most n gives

    sum_t h_t = -Poly_n'(0)
      <= sum_k |L_k'(0)|/sqrt(a_k) =: C_n < infinity.

Nonnegativity of the steps therefore bounds each h_t by C_n. The sublevel is closed, bounded, and nonempty because h=0 has W_n=1. Lower semicontinuity on this compact set proves attainment of the global infimum.

For positivity, first note the explicit Huber lower bound. Put S=sum h_t and delta=1/(2S+1). The function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=delta, and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise, is convex and 1-smooth. From x_0=1, every iterate is x_i=1-delta sum_{t<i}h_t>=delta; its terminal error is 2f(x_n)=1/(2S+1)>0.

Suppose a globally minimizing length-n schedule contains a zero. Delete that zero to obtain h' of length n-1 with exactly the same terminal point and worst-case value q>0. Set B=prod_{t<n-1}(1+h'_t), so every normalized legal instance has terminal radius at most B. If its terminal function gap is F, convexity gives F<=B||g||, and a final unit step gives

    F_new <= F-||g||^2/2 <= F-F^2/(2B^2).

We have 0<=F<=q/2<=B^2/2. The displayed scalar function is increasing on this interval. Thus appending a unit step to h' produces another length-n schedule satisfying

    W_n(h',1) <= q-q^2/(4B^2) < q,

contradicting global minimality. Therefore every minimizing step is positive. This argument also handles the all-zero proposed minimizer and uses no differentiability of W_n.

## 3. Exact finite-dimensional primal formulation

Let I={*,0,...,n}. For i!=j define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The primal variables are the Gram matrix G of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with G>=0 and G_00<=1, together with the real values f_0,...,f_n. The remaining positions are defined by x_i=x_0-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t, and the optimum sample is zero. Impose Q_ij<=0 for every ordered pair. Maximize 2f_n. Necessity of the interpolation inequalities follows by applying a unit gradient step to the convex 1-smooth function f(x)-<g_i,x>, whose minimum is attained at x_i: its decrease bound from x_j gives f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i>>=||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

For completeness, the interpolation sufficiency has the following explicit proof. Given such finite vector/value data, put a_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i and

    H(v)=max_i {a_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2}.

At v=g_i, the i-th expression is a_i and every j-th expression is at most a_i, because that comparison is exactly -Q_ij>=0. Hence H(g_i)=a_i and x_i belongs to the convex subdifferential of H at g_i. The function H is finite and 1-strongly convex, with quadratic growth. Its conjugate f=H^* is finite everywhere, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, and satisfies f(x_i)=f_i and grad f(x_i)=g_i. Indeed, strong convexity makes the maximizing vector in the conjugate unique; the strong monotonicity of the subgradient relation bounds the distance between two such maximizing vectors by the distance between their arguments. This gives the claimed gradient Lipschitz constant. The zero sample makes zero a minimizer. Thus the primal optimum is exactly W_n(h), with dimension at most n+2.

The feasible primal set is compact: the radius/gradient estimates above bound all Gram entries; the two optimum interpolation inequalities imply 0<=f_i<=||x_i||^2/2. It is closed and nonempty, so its optimum is attained.

For positive h, the primal admits strict interpolation inequalities and G positive definite. Take n+2 distinct numbers theta_k in (0,1), the quadratic with diagonal Hessian diag(theta_k), and an initial vector having every coordinate nonzero and norm less than one. The columns x_0,g_0,...,g_n are evaluations of 1,zPoly_0(z),...,zPoly_n(z) at these nodes, times nonzero coordinate factors. Their distinct degrees and nonzero leading coefficients make this matrix invertible. No two sampled positions agree, because their different prefix polynomials cannot agree at n+2 distinct nodes. None equals zero for the same degree reason. Direct substitution gives

    Q_ij=-(1/2)sum_k theta_k(1-theta_k)(x_{i,k}-x_{j,k})^2 <0

for every distinct pair, including *. The norm inequality is also strict.

Source context: Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 4 and Theorem 6 treat smooth-convex interpolation and strong duality for fixed-step methods using the latest gradient with nonzero coefficient. Their proof uses a full-rank quadratic transcript. The preceding Vandermonde construction and the separation argument below provide the needed hypotheses and conclusion directly here; no general-step extension is presumed.

## 4. Full dual attainment by finite-dimensional separation

Let T_ij(G) denote the gradient/position part of Q_ij, so Q_ij=f_i-f_j+T_ij(G). Consider the open convex set of triples (a,b,c) for which some G>=0 and some f satisfy

    a_ij>Q_ij(f,G), b>G_00-1, c<2f_n.

The point (0,0,q), where q is the finite primal optimum, is outside this set. A separating hyperplane, using the independent upward variations of a,b and downward variation of c, has nonnegative coefficients lambda_ij, q_0, alpha, not all zero, and yields

    sum lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G)+q_0(G_00-1)-alpha*2f_n >= -alpha*q

for all G>=0 and all f. The strict primal point constructed above rules out alpha=0: substituting it would make the left side strictly negative whenever lambda or q_0 is nonzero. Thus normalize alpha=1.

The arbitrary f coordinates force

    out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n}.

The arbitrary PSD matrix G then forces

    M(q_0,lambda,h)=q_0 E_00+sum lambda_ij T_ij >=0.

Taking G=f=0 gives q_0<=q. Conversely the flow and PSD constraints give an upper certificate W_n(h)<=q_0. Therefore q_0=q and the full dual is attained.

This argument establishes the direction needed here: at a minimizing positive schedule there is a certificate with q exactly equal to its true worst-case value. A feasible upper certificate by itself is never interpreted as a lower bound.

## 5. A strictly feasible certificate at every finite h

A regularity direction is needed because the optimal certificate can have many zero multipliers and singular PSD slack. We now construct a certificate with all directed multipliers positive and M positive definite at the same fixed h.

For each finite i, adding equal weight to lambda_{i,*} and lambda_{*,i} preserves the flow. Their quadratic sum is

    Q_{i,*}+Q_{*,i}=||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>.

Let H_max=max_t h_t, r=1/(2H_max+3), and w_i=r^(2i)>0. The gradient block A_w of the weighted sum of these cycles has diagonal w_i and, for i>t, off-diagonal w_i h_t/2. With D=diag(w_i), the matrix D^(-1/2) A_w D^(-1/2) has diagonal one and off-diagonal h_t r^(i-t)/2. Each row's absolute off-diagonal sum is at most H_max*r/(1-r)<1/2. Hence A_w is positive definite, as follows also by bounding each cross term with the two corresponding squared coordinates.

Start with lambda_{n,*}=2, and add weight one to every directed finite-to-finite pair. Those paired edges are flow-balanced. Next add K w_i to each of lambda_{i,*} and lambda_{*,i}. All directed multipliers are now positive and the required flow holds. For sufficiently large finite K, their gradient block A is positive definite, because K A_w dominates the fixed remaining symmetric matrix. The full matrix has form

    M=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]],  mu_i=lambda_{i,*}.

Choosing q>mu^T A^(-1)mu/4 makes M positive definite. This constructs strict feasibility with no assumptions on which multipliers of the optimum vanish.

## 6. KKT without assumed nondegeneracy

Take a global minimizing positive schedule h and an attained certificate (q,lambda). It is also a global minimizer of q over the joint variables (q,lambda,h), subject to flow, lambda>=0 and M>=0: every such certificate upper-bounds a true W_n(h)>=W_n^*.

Here is the finite-dimensional separation argument giving the needed multipliers. Restrict directions d to the linear flow-null space. Let J be the set of zero lambda entries, and let K_0=ker M. If a direction satisfies

    dq<0, d lambda_e>0 for e in J,
    (DM[d]) restricted to K_0 is positive definite,

then a sufficiently small positive step along it is feasible and improves q. To justify the matrix assertion, decompose into K_0 and its orthogonal complement. The complementary block of M is positive definite, the kernel block has a positive first-order term, and cross blocks are first order. The Schur complement subtracts only a second-order term. The dependence on h and lambda is polynomial, so its Taylor remainder is also second order. Positivity of h persists for sufficiently small steps. Such a descent direction is therefore impossible.

Separating the image of the direction space from the open cone defined by these three strict inequalities gives an objective multiplier alpha>=0, numbers r_e>=0 supported on J, and a PSD matrix G supported on K_0, not all zero, annihilating every flow-null direction:

    alpha*dq - sum r_e*d lambda_e - <G,DM[d]> =0.

Move from the optimal certificate toward the strictly feasible certificate from section 5 while holding h fixed. That direction has every d lambda_e>0 on J and has positive compression of DM to K_0. Substituting it rules out alpha=0, unless r=G=0, which would contradict nontrivial separation. Normalize alpha=1.

Extending the annihilator by flow multipliers written as -f_i gives the coefficient identities

    G_00=1,
    f_i-f_j+T_ij(G)=-r_ij<=0,
    lambda_ij r_ij=0,
    M G=0,
    <G,partial M/partial h_t>=0 for every t.

The PSD matrix G is consequently a legal normalized primal Gram matrix by section 3. The interpolation inequalities and lambda complementarity imply sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0. Taking the trace of M G then gives q=2f_n. Thus G realizes an actual worst-case transcript. Degenerate lambda and singular M were allowed throughout.

If either J or K_0 is empty, omit its corresponding strict-cone factor and multiplier. The same argument applies in these zero-dimensional cases.

## 7. The resulting quantitative identities

Let x_0,g_0,...,g_n be Euclidean vectors with Gram matrix G. Expanding the derivative with respect to h_t gives exactly

    partial/partial h_t [sum lambda_ij T_ij]
      = <g_t,
          sum_{i>t}(mu_i+sum_{j<=t}lambda_ij)g_i
          -sum_{i<=t}(sum_{j>t}lambda_ij)g_i >.

The stationary equations are therefore (S) with z_t defined in (Z), including every edge that crosses the chronological cut t.

The x_0 row of M G=0 and G_00=1 give

    sum_i mu_i <x_0,g_i>=2q.                                  (X)

The full quadratic trace can be expanded as

    0=<M,G>
     =q-sum_i mu_i<x_0,g_i>
       +sum_{t<n}h_t<g_t,z_t>
       +(1/2)sum_{i,j}lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2.

Using (S) and (X) yields the announced energy identity (E). The energy is nonnegative, and all chronological cut terms have been justified individually rather than discarded.

These equations constrain both a full feasible dual certificate and a full feasible primal transcript at an actual global minimizer. They do not supply a uniform numerical comparison with the Zhang--Jiang recurrence yet.

## 8. Exact one-step primal-dual check

For n=1, h_0=3/2, q=1/4, take

    lambda_{0,*}=lambda_{1,*}=lambda_{1,0}=1,

with all other multipliers zero. Direct expansion yields

    M = v v^T, v=(1/2,-1,-1).

A matching stationary primal Gram matrix is

    G=[[1,1/3,1/6],
       [1/3,1/6,0],
       [1/6,0,1/12]],
    (f_0,f_1)=(1/4,1/8).

It is the convex combination with weights 8/9 and 1/9 of two legal one-dimensional transcripts, each starting at x_0=1:

* Huber threshold 1/4: (g_0,g_1)=(1/4,1/4), (f_0,f_1)=(7/32,1/8).
* Quadratic f(x)=x^2/2: (g_0,g_1)=(1,-1/2), (f_0,f_1)=(1/2,1/8).

The direct sum after coordinate scaling by the square roots of the weights is itself one legal two-dimensional instance, so the combination is not merely a formal averaging device. In detail, for weights a,b and scalar functions H,Q, use F(u,v)=a H(u/sqrt(a))+b Q(v/sqrt(b)) and initial point (sqrt(a),sqrt(b)); this preserves smoothness one and gives exactly the displayed Gram matrix and values.

The full matrix equality M G=0 holds. The cut vector is z_0=2g_1 and <g_0,g_1>=0, verifying stationarity. The directed energy is

    (1/2)(||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2+||g_1-g_0||^2)
      =1/6+1/12=1/4=q.

Global one-step optimality follows directly: the Huber lower bound is at least 1/4 for h_0<=3/2, and the quadratic lower bound (h_0-1)^2 is at least 1/4 for h_0>=3/2. The displayed certificate attains 1/4.

A hostile check is useful: the pure Huber transcript is worst-case for this very same optimal schedule and dual certificate, but has <g_0,g_1>=1/16 and fails stationarity. Thus it is invalid to assume that an arbitrary active worst-case function supplies the stationary primal matrix. The multiplier-separation construction selects a suitable primal matrix, and an admissible direct sum may be essential.

## 9. Exact remaining obstruction

The current identities do not force q to equal the sum-based Huber level, do not show lambda_{*,i}=0 at a global minimizer, and do not imply that all temporal gradients are orthogonal. Each such additional simplification would require its own proof or counterexample test.

A route to the target would still need a quantitative estimate combining the full PSD slack, all interpolation inequalities, the cut equations (S), and the energy (E), uniformly in horizon, whose constant matches the exact recurrence. Merely defining that missing estimate is not a result. The package above establishes a candidate rigorous interface at genuine global minimizers, with exact feasible-data checks, and records the current obstruction.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002585 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002586 -->
# Why creating penultimate flow first does not solve the stationary case

Status: unverified failure analysis of a specific proposed extension. The valid transfer and descent identities remain intact.

Consider a full matching stationary tuple (h,q,lambda,G,f) with q>0, h_{n-1}>0, all primal interpolation inequalities, dual PSD, complementarity, and the stepsize stationarity equations. Put p=g_{n-1}, g=g_n, delta=f_{n-1}-||p||^2/2.

## 1. Last-step stationarity selects an orthogonal terminal pair

For the SAME legal primal G selected by the full stationary tuple, one has

    <p,g>=0.                                               (1)

Here is a proof that does not assume a C2 function or differentiability of W. Realize the legal finite data by a convex 1-smooth interpolating function. Change only the final step b by a small real u. Then x_n(u)=x_n-u p, g_n(u)=g_n+O(u), and

    f_n(u)=f_n-u<p,g>+o(u).

Let D be the derivative of the full certificate matrix with respect to b, holding q and lambda fixed. The old matrix annihilates the old vector realization by PSD complementarity. Therefore its evaluation on the changed gradient vectors is O(u^2), and the full polynomial identity gives

    sum lambda_ij Q_ij(u)
       =u[<D,G>-2<p,g>]+o(u).

The left side is nonpositive for both signs of u and vanishes at zero. Consequently <D,G>=2<p,g>. The full stationarity equation <D,G>=0 proves (1). This argument keeps the same G; it does not substitute an arbitrary active hard function for the stationary one.

## 2. A positive margin in the penultimate unit-step residual

The interpolation inequality Q_{n,n-1}<=0, together with (1), gives

    f_{n-1}-||p||^2/2 >= f_n+||g||^2/2,
    delta >= q/2+||g||^2/2 > q/2.                          (2)

The strictness holds because q=2f_n>0 implies g_n!=0. If B=prod_t(1+h_t) is the elementary terminal radius bound for normalized initial distance, convexity further gives

    ||g||^2>=q^2/(4B^2),
    delta>=q/2+q^2/(8B^2).                                 (3)

This is a constraint on the actual full stationary primal, including the case q>tau.

## 3. Exact obstruction to certificate inflation followed by final-step blending

Suppose one first changes only the certificate at the SAME h, perhaps increasing its level from q to q', to create a positive new penultimate star-outgoing multiplier nu'. Every such feasible certificate must satisfy, on the old matching legal primal,

    q' >= q+nu' delta.                                     (4)

Indeed its PSD identity gives q'>=2f_n+nu' delta after the other nonnegative interpolation contributions are dropped.

The largest permitted final-step convex blend then certifies the value 2q'/(2+nu'). But (2) and (4) imply

    2q'/(2+nu')
       >= q + nu'||g_n||^2/(2+nu') > q.                    (5)

A smaller blend parameter gives no better certified level. Therefore the two-stage procedure

    raise or alter the certificate at fixed h to create nu'>0,
    then use the proved final-step convex-blend bound,

cannot prove a descent from a full stationary tuple. This is an exact finite inequality, not only a first-order obstruction, and it applies regardless of q-tau.

The original penultimate-flow descent still detects genuinely nonoptimal data, and the transport operation still preserves full feasibility. Equation (5) explains why those operations cannot be bootstrapped into a generic q>tau contradiction after full last-coordinate stationarity has already been reached.

## 4. Implication for the next mechanism

The surviving problem necessarily involves an earlier stepsize change, a nonlinear suffix response, or a quantitative global consequence of the full matrix beyond terminal coordinate optimality. The exact suffix counterexample in research/max2_earlier_reset_obstruction.md forbids carrying final-step convexity backward without additional structure.

The current terminal operations are complete useful constraints, but they do not prove Huber tightness of globally optimal schedules. Further work should not keep rediscovering last-coordinate descent under different notation.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002587 -->
# Exact terminal re-selection through the fixed-prefix maximal extension

Status: The expanded self-contained package, including arbitrary zero steps and the empty horizon, is ACCEPTED as fact 1252b8823bb5a290. See max2_terminal_reselection_selected.md for the exact submitted package and max2_terminal_reselection_errata.md for the notation note. This earlier positive-step research draft is retained for history.
This is a precise positive support result for the full optimal certificate set.
It removes only incoming edges at the UNQUERIED final sample, not arbitrary
remote forward edges at queried samples. No descent or profile theorem is claimed.

## 1. The reduced primal retains the complete prefix

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a
finite positive schedule, n>=1, and let P={star,0,...,n-1}.
As usual x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Consider the full Gram primal on (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with G>=0,
||x_0||^2<=1, and objective maximize 2f_n. Keep

    Q_ij<=0 for every distinct i,j in P,
    Q_(n,j)<=0 for every j in P.                    (1)

Delete every Q_(j,n)<=0, j in P, including Q_(star,n).
No other constraint is removed. In particular all remote forward and
backward interpolation constraints inside the prefix remain.

The value of this reduced primal equals the true unrestricted W_n(h).

## 2. Exactness by an explicit globally legal extension

Take any feasible reduced transcript and realize its Gram matrix by
finite-dimensional Euclidean vectors. For j in P put

    a_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j,
    H_j(v)=a_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2,
    H(v)=max_(j in P) H_j(v),
    F(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.                       (2)

Each H_j is a quadratic with Hessian I, so H is finite, continuous and
1-strongly convex. Its quadratic growth makes the supremum defining F
finite and attained at a unique vector v(x). The optimality relation is
x in partial H(v(x)). Strong convexity gives

    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')> >= ||v(x)-v(x')||^2,

and hence ||v(x)-v(x')||<=||x-x'||. The maximizing affine functions in (2)
then show grad F(x)=v(x): the lower bound from testing v(x) and upper
bound from testing v(x') squeeze the first-order difference, with an
O(||x-x'||^2) error. Thus F is globally convex, differentiable, and
1-smooth.

For any i,j in P,

    H_j(g_i)-a_i=Q_ij<=0.

Consequently H(g_i)=a_i, and x_i is a subgradient of H at g_i, since the
i-th quadratic is active there and has gradient x_i. It follows that
grad F(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i. The star sample gives F(0)=0 and
grad F(0)=0, so zero is a global minimizer. This proves legality of the
complete prefix without invoking an external interpolation black box.

Because GD through x_n uses only g_0,...,g_(n-1), the SAME predetermined
schedule on F follows all the supplied positions through x_n.
The retained terminal inequalities say

    H_j(g_n)<=<x_n,g_n>-f_n for every j in P.

Therefore

    f_n<=<x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)<=F(x_n).                  (3)

The new actual terminal value is at least the reduced transcript's
objective. Thus every reduced feasible value is at most W_n(h).
Conversely any legal trajectory is reduced feasible, proving exactness.

The constructed F is also the pointwise maximal smooth-convex extension
of the fixed prefix. Indeed, for any legal extension K, the elementary
smooth-convex interpolation inequality between each fixed sample j and
an arbitrary sample x with gradient grad K(x) gives
K(x)<=<x,grad K(x)>-H(grad K(x))<=F(x).
Maximality is useful motivation but (3) alone proves reduced-PEP exactness.

At an optimal reduced transcript, 2f_n=W_n(h), both inequalities in (3)
are equalities. Uniqueness of the conjugate maximizer implies
g_n=grad F(x_n) and f_n=F(x_n). Hence an optimal reduced primal is itself
fully interpolable: the deleted incoming terminal inequalities hold at
optimality. This statement is about optimal transcripts, not all reduced
feasible transcripts.

## 3. The reduced dual attains the SAME value

Here are the necessary finite-dimensional details, so re-selection is
not an appeal to an unattained dual infimum.

A strict reduced primal exists when every h_t>0. Choose n+2 distinct
numbers theta_l in (0,1), take a quadratic with diagonal Hessian
diag(theta_l), and an initial vector with every coordinate nonzero and
norm less than one. The columns x_0,g_0,...,g_n are evaluations of

    1, z, z product_(t<1)(1-h_t z), ...,
                       z product_(t<n)(1-h_t z)

at the distinct nodes, times nonzero initial coordinates. These n+2
polynomials have degrees 0,1,...,n+1 with nonzero leading coefficients,
so their evaluation matrix is invertible. Thus G is positive definite.

No two positions among star and 0,...,n are equal: their distinct
prefix polynomials, each of degree at most n, cannot agree at all n+2
distinct nodes; none of those polynomials vanishes at all nodes either.
For the strictly convex quadratic with Hessian strictly below I,
direct substitution gives

    Q_ij=-1/2 (x_i-x_j)^T Hquad(I-Hquad)(x_i-x_j)<0

for every distinct pair. In particular every retained inequality is strict.

The reduced optimal value is finite by Section2 and the elementary GD
bound W_n(h)<=product_t(1+h_t)^2. Its dual attainment now follows directly
from separating an open convex set, as follows. Let E_red be the retained
ordered pairs. The open convex set of (a,b,c) satisfying, for some G>=0,f,

    a_ij>Q_ij(f,G) (ij in E_red),
    b>G_00-1,
    c<2f_n

does not contain (0,0,q), where q=W_n(h). A separating hyperplane has
nonnegative coefficients lambda_ij,r,eta, not all zero, and yields

    sum_(E_red) lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G)
             +r(G_00-1)-eta*2f_n >= -eta*q

for all G>=0,f. The strict primal point rules out eta=0. Normalize eta=1.
Arbitrary values f then force out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), and arbitrary PSD G
forces the full matrix M(r,lambda,h)>=0. Testing G=f=0 gives r<=q;
weak duality and reduced exactness give r>=q. Thus r=q and the dual
attains the exact objective value.

Extend this reduced certificate to the original full format by setting
all omitted multipliers to zero. We have proved the precise re-selection:

    At every positive h there EXISTS a tight full certificate with
        lambda_(j,n)=0 for ALL j in P.             (4)

In particular nu_n=0 can be imposed, and the remaining outgoing
terminal weights sum to exactly two. This does not say that every
optimal certificate already has this support. It does not assert that
a source vector can be held fixed during the re-selection.

This argument is the ordinary finite-dimensional separation proof only
for the reduced system just justified. It does not repeat or claim as
new the historical global minimizer/KKT interface.

## 4. Exact terminal square and monotone probability coordinates

For a certificate satisfying (4), write

    alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 >=0, j in P,
    sum_(j in P)alpha_j=1,
    y_j=x_j-g_j,  delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,
    V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in P)alpha_j y_j.            (5)

Let lambda^P denote the unchanged multipliers on ordered pairs inside P.
Direct expansion, with delta_star=y_star=0, gives the FULL identity

    M(q,lambda,h)
      =||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha,                  (6)

where

    R_alpha=q||x_0||^2
                  -2sum_(j in P)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2
                  +sum_(i,j in P)lambda^P_ij Q_ij. (7)

The prefix flow cancels all function values in R_alpha. In detail,
for each finite i<n it is

    out_i^P-in_i^P=2alpha_i,

because the removed terminal outgoing edge n->i supplied incoming flow
2alpha_i in the full certificate. R_alpha is thus a quadratic form
in (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)).

Full PSD is EQUIVALENT to R_alpha>=0: one direction follows from (6),
and the other follows by minimizing the full quadratic over the
independent formal vector g_n. This is an exact Schur complement,
not the omission of an unobserved kernel direction.

The same identity is a necessary-and-sufficient certificate
parameterization of this selected subface: nonnegative prefix
multipliers with the displayed prefix flow, alpha in the probability
simplex, and the complete PSD remainder R_alpha.

For explicit chronological coefficients define

    w_(-1)=alpha_star,
    w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, 0<=t<n.

Then 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1, and

    V_alpha=alpha_star x_0
            -sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t. (8)

This keeps all earlier gradient coordinates. In particular
lambda_(n,star)=2alpha_star<=2. The formula is not a scalar-mass
replacement for the prefix PSD condition (7).

On ANY actual matching worst case for this selected certificate,

    g_n=V_alpha,  R_alpha=0,
    alpha_j Q_(n,j)=0, lambda^P_ij Q_ij=0.          (9)

Thus re-selection gives an actual terminal gradient formula for the
matching primal. It does not impose such a formula on every legal
trajectory; away from equality the square in (6) measures its defect.

## 5. Relation to the remote-edge obstruction and source variation cost

For a two-step suffix k=n-2, the selected support eliminates remote
forward edges i->n and the local edge (n-1)->n. It does NOT eliminate
remote i->(n-1) for i<k. Those edges are within the complete prefix
and remain in (7). In particular the old three-step counterexample
at h=(2,9/4,3) already has no incoming edges at its final sample3:
its hostile edge0->2 survives this reduction unchanged.

At a full stationary matching tuple with nu_k>0, the common strict
barrier derived directly from interpolation still applies:
lambda_(k,k+1)=0 across the full optimal set, with positive old slack.
The terminal re-selection may alter earlier source coefficients and
may alter which actual matching primal supplies earlier-cut stationarity.
One cannot transport all stationarity equations to an arbitrarily
chosen reselected certificate.

For any joint change from a fixed stationary base, the exact old-primal
identity remains

    q'-q >= sum lambda'_ij(-Q_ij(old))
                  -sum_t (h'_t-h_t)<g_t,z_t(lambda'-lambda)>.

Neither the terminal square (6) nor dropping the redundant terminal
incoming inequalities cancels this inactive cost or bilinear term.

## 6. Literature check and scope

The explicit maximum-of-quadratics conjugate construction is consistent
with Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, Smooth strongly convex interpolation
and exact worst-case performance of first-order methods,
arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem4 and Remark2. Their Theorem6 uses a full-rank
quadratic transcript to establish fixed-step PEP duality. Those sections
were read in the downloaded paper. All construction, reduced-system
exactness, separation, and full square identities needed here are
written out, rather than imported as unexplained external theorems.
A targeted Matlas query returned unrelated results; no such result was used.

This is a concrete, validly scoped route to re-selection and a complete
matrix parameterization of the resulting subface. It is not a descent
theorem at a global minimizer, does not remove the surviving remote
prefix edges, and does not resolve Huber tightness or the exact DP mass
restriction.

They all mean cjet_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i, as defined for every prefix index by the statement's cjet_j. The intended formulas are H_j(g_i)-cjet_i=Q_ij, H(g_i)=cjet_i, and F(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-cjet_i=f_i. ## DOC-002589 — project/research/max2_terminal_reselection_selected.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\max2_terminal_reselection_selected.md

原始字节数：20228；原始 SHA-256：d0101210b8341c546c4364cb66bc69acca99d2a18e4ab989433a4785033c046f

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002589 -->
# Selected terminal reduction package, including zero and empty schedules

Status: ACCEPTED as fact 1252b8823bb5a290, verifier verdict correct (verification finding 5480845b8a751e16). The one selected allocation is consumed. This file preserves the exact submitted proof; its three a_i/cjet_i notation slips are recorded separately in max2_terminal_reselection_errata.md. The content-addressed fact is not hand-edited.

## Statement

Exact unqueried-terminal reduction and full-certificate re-selection for arbitrary nonnegative gradient-descent schedules.

For every integer n>=0, every L>0, and every finite h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The empty schedule n=0 is allowed.

For the following finite formulations use normalized smoothness1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, normalized sampled values f_i, formal gradients g_i, and x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Put I_n={star,0,...,n}, J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 for distinct i,j in I_n. When n=0, J_pre={star}.

(a) The finite Gram maximization of 2f_n subject to G_pr>=0, G_pr_(x0,x0)<=1, all Q_ij<=0 with distinct i,j in J_pre, and all Q_(n,j)<=0 with j in J_pre, has attained optimal value W_n(h). Here G_pr is the full Gram matrix of the n+2 formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with no rank constraint. No incoming terminal inequality Q_(j,n)<=0 is imposed. For any feasible prefix jets in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, define cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j, H_j(v)=cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2, H(v)=max_(j in J_pre)H_j(v), and F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)). Then F is an everywhere finite convex differentiable 1-smooth function interpolating all prefix jets, F(0)=0=min F, and it is the pointwise maximal such prefix interpolant. For every reduced-feasible terminal pair (g_n,f_n), F(x_n)>=f_n. At an optimal reduced transcript, F(x_n)=f_n and grad F(x_n)=g_n; hence every optimal reduced transcript is fully interpolable and obeys the deleted incoming terminal inequalities.

(b) There exist nonnegative multipliers lambda_ij on all distinct pairs in I_n, with lambda_(j,n)=0 for every j in J_pre, finite-node flow sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n), and a FULL positive semidefinite quadratic certificate
M_cert=W_n(h)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
after the GD substitution. This certificate is PSD on the original formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including every gradient at a zero step, without imposing equality between duplicated formal gradients. Thus some tight full certificate has no incoming edge at the unqueried terminal sample for every nonnegative schedule. For schedules with positive steps this follows from an explicit strict primal and a separation argument. If there are zero steps, choose the original index of each positive step as the representative of its compressed query and the original index n as the terminal representative; embedding a certificate for the positive-step compression gives the asserted full-space certificate. The all-zero and empty schedules have W_n(h)=1 and the direct certificate lambda_(n,star)=2, all other lambda zero, with M_cert=||x_0-g_n||^2.

(c) For any real certificate level q_cert and any h as above, certificates with no incoming terminal multipliers are parameterized exactly as follows. Choose alpha_j>=0 for j in J_pre, with sum_j alpha_j=1, and nonnegative prefix multipliers lambda^pre_ij for distinct i,j in J_pre whose finite prefix flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for 0<=i<n, where out_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ij and in_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ji. Define y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, with y_star=delta_star=0, and
R_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.
The prefix flow cancels all f_i in R_alpha. Set lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j, all incoming terminal multipliers zero, and retain lambda^pre. The complete identity is
M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.
Consequently full PSD of M_cert is equivalent to PSD of the entire prefix quadratic R_alpha in (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)). For n>=1, with w_(-1)=alpha_star and w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, one has 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1 and
V_alpha=alpha_star x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t.
For n=0, alpha_star=1, V_alpha=x_0, and R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2. Any actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible q_cert has g_n=V_alpha, R_alpha=0, and multiplier complementarity.

This result deletes no interpolation inequalities inside the queried prefix. It asserts existence of a selected optimal certificate, not that every optimal certificate has the selected support. It neither establishes Huber tightness nor removes earlier remote forward edges, transfers earlier-cut stationarity under arbitrary re-selection, or proves the project's asymptotic exact-profile conjecture.

## Proof

1. Normalization, interpolation necessity, and elementary bounds.

For an original instance let R=||x_0-x_star||>0. The normalized function
F_0(z)=[f(x_star+R z)-f(x_star)]/(L R^2)
is convex differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant1, a minimizer0,
and initial norm1. Its GD uses the same h and its normalized objective
is 2F_0(z_n). This also proves the converse scaling correspondence.
We may work with smoothness1 and the optimum sample zero.

For a differentiable function K with 1-Lipschitz gradient, integration
of its gradient along a segment gives
K(x+d)<=K(x)+<grad K(x),d>+||d||^2/2.
For convex K, apply this to K(z)-<grad K(x_i),z>, whose minimum is
at x_i, taking a unit negative-gradient step from x_j. Its minimum
value is no larger than the stepped value, so
K(x_j)-K(x_i)-<grad K(x_i),x_j-x_i>
    >=||grad K(x_j)-grad K(x_i)||^2/2.
This is exactly Q_ij<=0 for the actual samples, with our stated
orientation of Q.

For a normalized legal instance, put D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), D_0=1.
Since grad K(0)=0, ||g_i||<=||x_i|| and ||x_(i+1)||<=(1+h_i)||x_i||.
Thus ||x_i||,||g_i||<=D_i, and
0<=K(x_i)<=||x_i||^2/2.
In particular W_n(h)<=D_n^2<infinity.
It is strictly positive: choose the scalar quadratic K(x)=kappa*x^2/2
with kappa=1/[2(1+sum_t h_t)] in (0,1], starting at1.
Every factor 1-kappa h_t is positive, and its normalized error is
kappa*product_t(1-kappa h_t)^2>0.

2. Explicit interpolation and the maximal prefix extension.

Let any finite collection of prefix jets (x_j,g_j,f_j), j in J_pre,
satisfy every ordered prefix inequality and include the zero optimum jet.
Define cjet_j,H_j,H,F as in the statement, on the same finite-dimensional
Euclidean space. The function H(v)-||v||^2/2 is the maximum of finitely
many affine functions and hence is convex. H is continuous, finite,
1-strongly convex, and has quadratic growth. For each x, the function
<v,x>-H(v) therefore has a unique maximizer, denoted v(x).

The maximizer condition is x in partial H(v(x)). For any x,x',
the two strong subgradient inequalities for H, added together, give
<x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2.
Consequently ||v(x)-v(x')||<=||x-x'||.
The definition of F, evaluated at v(x) and v(x'), gives
0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>
    <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.
Thus F is differentiable with grad F(x)=v(x), and its gradient is
1-Lipschitz. It is convex as a supremum of affine functions and finite
by the stated quadratic growth.

For i,j in J_pre direct expansion gives H_j(g_i)-a_i=Q_ij.
The term with j=i equals zero and every other term is nonpositive;
hence H(g_i)=a_i. The active quadratic H_i has gradient x_i at g_i,
and H>=H_i with equality there, so x_i is a subgradient of H at g_i.
The unique maximizer defining F(x_i) is consequently g_i, and
F(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-a_i=f_i. This proves interpolation of all prefix jets.
In particular F(0)=0 and grad F(0)=0, so zero is a global minimizer.
The same construction, for any finite index set including the zero
jet, proves sufficiency of ALL pairwise Q inequalities for a legal
convex 1-smooth interpolant; repeated positions cause no exception.

If K is any other legal function interpolating the prefix, apply the
necessity calculation in paragraph1 between its sample at an arbitrary
x and each prefix jet j. Writing g=grad K(x), those inequalities give
H_j(g)<=<x,g>-K(x) for every j. Thus
K(x)<=<x,g>-H(g)<=F(x).
This proves the claimed pointwise maximality without importing an
interpolation or conjugacy theorem from a paper.

3. Exact value of the reduced Gram problem and primal attainment.

Realize any feasible Gram matrix by vectors in dimension at most n+2;
if its rank is zero, one may embed its zero vectors in R.
The complete prefix inequalities give the function F of paragraph2.
Its gradients at every queried prefix point equal the supplied g_i,
so the GD recursion reaches the same supplied x_n. No terminal gradient
is used to determine x_n, even when some steps vanish.

For the reduced terminal pair, the retained inequalities give
H_j(g_n)<=<x_n,g_n>-f_n for all j in J_pre. Therefore
f_n<=<x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)<=F(x_n).
If ||x_0||>0, normalization of this actual function yields
2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h).
If ||x_0||=0, all actual prefix iterates and x_n are the minimizer0,
so F(x_n)=0 and 2f_n<=0<W_n(h).
Thus the reduced supremum is at most W_n(h).
Every actual legal normalized trajectory is feasible for the reduced
Gram problem, so the reverse inequality holds.

For clarity the supremum is attained. Consider first the FULL Gram
problem, including every incoming terminal inequality. Its feasible
set is closed. Paragraph2 applied to the entire finite index set
I_n realizes every feasible point as a legal function; hence paragraph1
bounds all sampled gradients, all Gram entries by Cauchy--Schwarz,
and all values 0<=f_i<=D_i^2/2. The feasible set is therefore compact
in finite-dimensional matrix/value space. It is nonempty and has an
objective maximizer. Necessity and the interpolation construction
show its value is exactly W_n(h). The same full optimizer is reduced
feasible, so it also attains the reduced value.

At any reduced optimizer, the chain
W_n(h)=2f_n<=2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h)
has equal endpoints and W_n(h)>0. Thus ||x_0||=1,
f_n=F(x_n), and <x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)=F(x_n).
The maximizer of that conjugate expression is unique, so
g_n=grad F(x_n). The supplied terminal jet therefore belongs to the
same legal F as the prefix and satisfies every deleted incoming
inequality. This also proves the assertion for every reduced optimizer,
not just one optimizer chosen from the full feasible set.

4. Dual attainment for positive schedules, by separation.

Assume n>=1 and h_t>0 for every t<n. Let E_red be the retained
ordered pair set:
{(i,j): i,j in J_pre, i!=j} union {(n,j):j in J_pre}.
Use the matrix/value variables of the reduced primal.

There is a strictly feasible point with G_pr positive definite,
G_pr_(x0,x0)<1, and every retained Q strictly negative.
Choose n+2 distinct real theta_l in (0,1), let A_quad be the diagonal
matrix with these entries, let K(x)=<x,A_quad x>/2, and choose x_0
with every coordinate nonzero and norm less than1.
Write Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), including Poly_0=1.
The columns (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) are evaluations at the theta_l of the
n+2 polynomials 1,z Poly_0,...,z Poly_n, times nonzero initial
coordinate factors. These polynomials have distinct degrees
0,1,...,n+1 and nonzero leading coefficients. A polynomial of degree
at most n+1 vanishing at n+2 distinct nodes is zero, so the evaluation
matrix is invertible and G_pr is positive definite.
For i!=j finite, Poly_i-Poly_j is a nonzero polynomial of degree
at most n; it cannot vanish at all chosen nodes. Poly_i cannot vanish
at all nodes either. Thus all finite sample positions are distinct
from each other and from star. Direct substitution for this quadratic
gives, with I the identity matrix,
Q_ij=-<x_i-x_j,A_quad(I-A_quad)(x_i-x_j)>/2<0.
This proves the full stated strictness.

Let w_opt=W_n(h), which is finite by paragraph1. Consider the open
convex set O of tuples (s,t,c), with s in R^(E_red) and t,c real,
for which there exist G_pr>=0 and real
sample values f with
s_ij>Q_ij(f,G_pr) for ij in E_red,
t>G_pr_(x0,x0)-1, and c<2f_n.
It is open since it is a union of open orthants; it is convex by
linearity of every Q in the Gram/value variables and convexity
of the PSD cone. The point (0,0,w_opt) is not in O.
Finite-dimensional separation of a point from a nonempty open convex
set gives a nonzero linear functional, chosen nonnegative on O
relative to that point. Independent upward changes of each s_ij
and of t, and downward changes of c, force its coefficients to be
(lambda_ij,rho,-eta) with lambda_ij,rho,eta>=0.
Taking limits to the boundary of each strict inequality yields,
for ALL G_pr>=0 and ALL real sample values f,
sum_(E_red)lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G_pr)
    +rho(G_pr_(x0,x0)-1)-eta*2f_n >= -eta*w_opt.       (S)

If eta=0, some lambda_ij or rho is positive, since the functional is
nonzero. Substitution of the strict primal point constructed above
makes the left side of (S) strictly negative, a contradiction.
Hence eta>0 and we divide the coefficients by eta to set eta=1.

The f_i are unrestricted real variables in (S). Their coefficients
must vanish:
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), for 0<=i<=n.
After their cancellation, (S) says
trace(M_cert(rho,lambda,h) G_pr)-rho>=-w_opt for every G_pr>=0.
If this matrix had a negative quadratic direction, scaling the
corresponding rank-one PSD G_pr would contradict this inequality.
It is therefore PSD. Taking G_pr=0 gives rho<=w_opt.
Conversely the flow, lambda>=0, and full PSD give, for every reduced
feasible point,
2f_n<=rho G_pr_(x0,x0)<=rho
because rho>=0. Thus w_opt<=rho. Equality follows.
Setting lambda_(j,n)=0 on the omitted pairs gives the required
tight full certificate on the original positive-step formal space.

5. Zero-step compression and lifting in the ORIGINAL formal space.

Suppose n>=1 and exactly m>=1 of the original steps are positive.
List their original indices as
0<=i_0<i_1<...<i_(m-1)<=n-1,
and define the positive compressed schedule hhat_s=h_(i_s),
0<=s<m. Deleting zero steps changes no actual iterate; therefore
W_m(hhat)=W_n(h) by the same function/initial-point trajectories in
both directions.

Apply paragraph4 to hhat, obtaining a tight incoming-terminal-free
certificate lambdahat on {star,0,...,m}.
Define the injective index map
iota(star)=star, iota(s)=i_s for 0<=s<m, and iota(m)=n.
On the original formal space define
xhat_0=x_0, ghat_s=g_(i_s) for s<m, ghat_m=g_n.
Its compressed formal positions satisfy
xhat_s=x_0-sum_(r<s)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_(i_s) for s<m,
and
xhat_m=x_0-sum_(r<m)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_n.
These are polynomial identities in independent original formal vectors:
the omitted coefficients h_t are ZERO. They do not use any equality
between gradients sampled at identical actual positions.

Put lambda_(iota(s),iota(t))=lambdahat_(s,t) on mapped pairs, and
put every other original multiplier equal to zero.
At each mapped finite node the original flow equals its compressed
flow; at each unmapped node both in-flow and out-flow vanish.
The mapped terminal is n, so the original finite flow is exactly
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n). No multiplier points into original n.
Every mapped Q expression equals the corresponding compressed
Q expression after identifying the mapped values, whose coefficients
cancel by flow.

Let T_pick be the linear map selecting
(x_0,g_(i_0),...,g_(i_(m-1)),g_n)
from (x_0,g_0,...,g_n). The ORIGINAL full certificate matrix is precisely
T_pick^T Mhat_cert T_pick and is PSD because Mhat_cert is PSD.
Unmapped gradient columns are zero. No duplicate-gradient constraint
or auxiliary squared penalty is imposed or needed. This proves the
claimed original-index, full-formal-space lift, including leading,
interior, and trailing zero steps.

If m=0, including the empty schedule n=0, every actual iterate is x_0.
Smoothness at the minimizer gives 2f(x_0)<=||x_0||^2, and the scalar
quadratic x^2/2 attains equality. Thus W_n(h)=1.
Set lambda_(n,star)=2 and every other multiplier to zero.
It has exactly the required flow and no incoming terminal edges.
Since x_n=x_0 as a formal identity, its full quadratic is
||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,star)=||x_0-g_n||^2.
This treats all-zero schedules and n=0 without any Slater assertion
about duplicated points. Parts(a) and the maximal extension remain
valid independently for these schedules by paragraphs2--3.

6. Exact terminal-square and full prefix-PSD parameterization.

Take ANY certificate whose incoming terminal multipliers vanish.
The finite terminal flow gives sum_(j in J_pre)lambda_(n,j)=2.
Consequently alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 is a nonnegative probability
vector. All other multipliers lie inside the prefix, and their finite
flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for i<n.
For any such alpha and prefix multipliers define y_j,delta_j,V_alpha,
R_alpha as in the statement, taking the star quantities equal to zero.

Expanding the outgoing terminal sum gives
2sum_j alpha_j Q_(n,j)
 =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j f_j
       +2<g_n,sum_j alpha_j x_j-x_n>
       +sum_j alpha_j||g_n-g_j||^2
 =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j delta_j
       +||g_n-V_alpha||^2-||V_alpha||^2.
Substituting into the full certificate proves
M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.
In R_alpha the coefficient of each finite f_i is
(out_i^pre-in_i^pre)-2alpha_i=0; it contains no f_n.
It is the complete quadratic form on ALL original prefix vectors.
The vector V_alpha is independent of the formal terminal gradient g_n.
Hence full PSD is equivalent to prefix PSD: prefix PSD implies full
PSD from the square identity, and full PSD implies prefix PSD by
setting the free formal vector g_n equal to V_alpha.
This is true even with zero-step duplicates; it is not a test only
on legal transcripts.

Conversely any alpha and nonnegative prefix multipliers with the
stated prefix flow define a full multiplier family by
lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j and incoming terminal multipliers zero.
Their full finite flow is the required objective flow. If R_alpha
is PSD the full square identity certifies it. This proves both
directions of the parameterization for any real q_cert.

For n>=1, expand positions in
V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j(x_j-g_j).
The coefficient of x_0 is
1-sum_(j=0)^(n-1)alpha_j=alpha_star.
For t<n, the coefficient of g_t is
-h_t+alpha_t+h_t sum_(j=t+1)^(n-1)alpha_j
 =-h_t[alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j]+alpha_t.
With w_t as in the statement, alpha_t=w_t-w_(t-1), giving
the claimed chronological formula and the nondecreasing w sequence.
For n=0 the probability simplex contains only alpha_star=1,
there are no prefix pairs, V_alpha=x_0, and
R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2.

Finally take an actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible
certificate level q_cert. The certificate identity evaluated there is
M_cert=sum lambda_ij Q_ij<=0, while full PSD makes it nonnegative.
Every term lambda_ij Q_ij is nonpositive, so each is zero.
The square identity and prefix PSD then imply
g_n=V_alpha and R_alpha=0. This is an assertion about matching
actual trajectories, not a presumed rank condition on a primal.

7. Scope and proof dependencies.

The proof uses elementary smoothness, the displayed explicit convex
conjugate construction, finite-dimensional linear algebra, compactness
of the bounded finite Gram system, and a fully specified separating
hyperplane argument. No historical project KKT claim or unverified
finding is a premise. No external paper theorem is used as a black
box. In particular the separation argument is used only for genuinely
positive compressed steps, and the zero-step conclusion is obtained
by an explicit embedding into the original formal space.

Only incoming constraints at n, which is unqueried by the recursion,
are removed. Every ordered interpolation constraint inside J_pre
remains. The statement implies neither Huber tightness nor a removal
of earlier remote edges or a transfer of earlier-cut stationarity
under arbitrary re-selection; it does not decide the asymptotic
exact-profile problem.

# Quantitative final-step descent from a terminal pair in the PSD Schur range

Status: unverified candidate. It extends the concrete descent search to certificates with no star-outgoing multipliers. It is not a general q>tau descent theorem.

## 1. Full-certificate hypothesis

Normalize L=1, x_*=f_*=0, and ||x_0||=1. Let a length-n schedule h, n>=1, have final step b=h_{n-1}>0 and a full feasible upper certificate (q,lambda), using all smooth-convex inequalities Q_ij<=0 and a PSD slack matrix

    M=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]],   mu_i=lambda_{i,*}, q>0.

Let Z=A-mu mu^T/(4q)>=0 be its complete gradient Schur complement. This is the actual matrix from the full certificate, not a surrogate formed from a few minors.

Suppose there exist kappa>0, a>0 and s in {+1,-1} such that

    Z >= kappa v v^T,
    v = a e_{n-1}+s e_n.                                   (H)

The indices e_i refer to gradient coordinates. The sign s=+1 will give a decrease of the final step; s=-1 will give an increase.

Define the finite radius bounds

    B_prev=prod_{t<n-1}(1+h_t),    B=(1+b)B_prev,
    C=q/(2B^2)+1/kappa.

Choose any d>0 satisfying

    d<=a/C,
    d<=q^2/(8a B^2 B_prev^2),                               (d)
    d<b                  if s=+1.

The changed schedule, with all preceding steps fixed, is

    b_new=b-s d.

Then the actual worst-case value obeys the explicit strict improvement

    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new)
       <= q-d q^2/(8a B^2) < q.                            (D)

No Huber-tightness, vanishing star-outgoing flow, stationarity, or rank assumption on the old primal is needed. The sufficient condition is the concrete full-matrix domination (H).

## 2. Uniform proof, including nearly worst-case instances

Fix any legal function. Put E=2f(x_n), p=g_{n-1}, g=g_n, and r=a p+s g. The complete certificate identity and every interpolation sign give

    q-E >= M evaluated on the actual vectors
          >= g_all^T Z g_all
          >= kappa ||r||^2.                               (1)

Here the second inequality follows by completing the square in x_0 in the full matrix. Thus ||r||^2<=(q-E)/kappa; the formula remains valid for every instance, not only for a maximizing one.

The elementary bounds ||p||<=B_prev, ||x_n||<=B, and convexity yield

    ||g||^2 >= E^2/(4B^2).                                 (2)

The identity ||r||^2=a^2||p||^2+||g||^2+2s a<p,g> implies

    -2s <p,g> >= (||g||^2-||r||^2)/a.                     (3)

The new endpoint is x_n+s d p. Smoothness gives

    E_new <= E+2s d<p,g>+d^2||p||^2
           <= E-(d/a)[E^2/(4B^2)-(q-E)/kappa]
                    +d^2 B_prev^2.                        (4)

For every E, the scalar inequality E^2>=q^2-2q(q-E) is simply (E-q)^2>=0. Inserting it into (4) gives

    E_new <= q-d q^2/(4a B^2)
               -[1-(d/a)C](q-E)+d^2 B_prev^2.

Since E<=q and d<=a/C, the middle term is nonpositive. The other restriction in (d) then gives (D). Taking the supremum over all legal functions and dimensions proves the uniform bound.

Thus this is an actual stepsize perturbation with an explicit certified new value. It is not a first-order assertion evaluated only on a guessed active function. The elementary smoothness upper inequality in (4) also follows from Q_{new,old}<=0 by completing the square
||g_new-g-s d p||^2/2, so the argument is consistent with the full interpolation cone.

The proof provides a uniform analytic certificate for the new schedule. It does not claim an explicit closed formula for its minimal-format interpolation multipliers. Such a formula is unnecessary to conclude that a purported globally optimal schedule has been strictly improved.

## 3. A range exclusion at global minimizers

Let T=span{e_{n-1},e_n}. For a PSD matrix Z and a nonzero v in range(Z), one has

    Z >= kappa v v^T,   kappa=1/(v^T Z^dagger v)>0.

Indeed write v=Z^(1/2)w with w=Z^(dagger/2)v, and use (w^T Z^(1/2)u)^2<=||w||^2 u^T Z u. Thus any vector in range(Z) supported on the last two coordinates, with both entries nonzero, can be rescaled to condition (H). Either sign of their ratio is allowed.

A vector with only the last coordinate nonzero is incompatible with a tight positive certificate: its square would force g_n=0 on a matching worst-case instance, hence f_n=0. A vector with only coordinate n-1 nonzero likewise forces g_{n-1}=0, making x_n=x_{n-1} a minimizer. Matching worst cases exist in the finite full interpolation formulation; alternatively, the quantitative argument below rules these cases out without assuming attainment.

For the last coordinate alone, q-E>=kappa||g_n||^2>=kappa E^2/(4B^2). This uniform inequality implies E<=r<q, where r is the positive root of r+kappa r^2/(4B^2)=q. It improves the allegedly tight upper bound. For the penultimate coordinate alone, use ||g_n||<=(1+b)||g_{n-1}|| to get the same conclusion with kappa replaced by kappa/(1+b)^2.

Consequently every tight full certificate at a globally minimizing positive schedule satisfies

    range(Z) intersect T = {0}.                             (R)

In particular

    rank(Z)<=n-1.

For n=1 this forces Z=0. For larger n, it does not force the whole Schur complement to vanish: its range may involve earlier gradients in every nonzero vector.

An equivalent exact matrix formulation uses the earlier-coordinate set J={0,...,n-2}. The generalized Schur complement of Z with respect to its J block must vanish:

    Z_TT-Z_TJ Z_JJ^dagger Z_JT=0.                           (SC)

For a PSD block matrix, completing squares gives the factorization into a block supported on the J rows and a residual supported only on T. The latter is PSD and its nonzero range lies in range(Z) intersect T. Conversely, if that residual is zero, the factorized range has no nonzero vector with zero J coordinates. This proves the equivalence, including singular Z_JJ. For n=1, J is empty and (SC) means Z=0.

This range restriction retains all history-dependent entries of Z; it does not replace Z by a two-by-two principal minor.

## 4. Exact one-step checks, including nu=0 above the Huber level

For b=1+a with 1/2<a<1, there is a tight certificate with

    q=a^2,
    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a,

and all other multipliers zero. Its Schur complement is

    Z=(2a-1)[[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]]
      =[(2a-1)/a^2] (a,1)^T(a,1).

Thus (H) holds with s=+1, kappa=(2a-1)/a^2, even though every star-outgoing multiplier vanishes. Formula (D) gives an explicit strict descent by decreasing b. The matching legal quadratic has g_1=-a g_0, exactly the relation exposed by the PSD square.

For 0<b<3/2, the tight Huber certificate with lambda_{0,*}=lambda_{1,*}=lambda_{1,0}=1 has

    q=1/(2b+1), Z=(3-2b)[[1,-1],[-1,1]]/4.

Here (H) holds with a=1, s=-1. The final step increases and the uniform bound strictly improves. This is consistent with the exact one-step optimum b=3/2, where Z=0 and this descent disappears.

These checks also show why q>tau alone is neither the hypothesis nor the conclusion of this result: the range mechanism can detect a suboptimal Huber-tight schedule, and it detects a non-Huber certificate with nu=0.

## 5. Remaining obstruction

Together with the penultimate-flow descent in research/max2_penultimate_flow_descent.md, the new constraints rule out two concrete ways for a globally minimizing tight certificate to carry slack: lambda_{*,n-1}>0 and any Schur-range vector supported on the final two gradients.

They leave earlier star-outgoing multipliers, the final star-outgoing multiplier, and a Schur range intertwined with earlier gradients. There is no proved implication from (R) to q=tau, no proved capacity bound by S_bullet(n), and no exact-profile conclusion. The next mechanism would have to control that earlier-coordinate dependence without treating the final two-coordinate Schur complement as the full matrix.

# Two-step suffix: a full joint move and the remote-flow obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED. All statements are conditional on a complete certificate
or on a complete matching stationary primal, as explicitly specified below.
This is a hostile test and extension of J1, not a target theorem.

## 1. Full notation

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h>=0 and q>0.
Set Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
A full certificate means lambda_ij>=0, out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n),
and M=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij PSD after the GD
substitution. Write mu_j=lambda_(j,star), nu_j=lambda_(star,j).

Let n>=2, k=n-2, a=h_k, b=h_(k+1). Define

c_1=mu_(k+1)+sum_(l<=k)lambda_(k+1,l),
c_2=mu_n+sum_(l<=k)lambda_(n,l),
c_3=c_2+lambda_(n,k+1)>0,
r_01=lambda_(k,k+1), r_02=lambda_(k,n), r_12=lambda_(k+1,n).

The last assertion follows from terminal flow:
c_3=2+nu_n+sum_(l<n)lambda_(l,n).

For the explicit deformation in Section2 only, assume

lambda_(i,j)=0 whenever 0<=i<k<j<=n.                 (R)

Thus no forward edge from an EARLIER prefix vertex crosses into the
two final gradient vertices. All three forward edges within
{k,k+1,n} remain allowed. The condition is vacuous at n=2.

## 2. Explicit finite deformation allowing local forward edges

Assume (R), a>1, and nu_k>0. First suppose c_2>0. Choose v>0 and define

u=(v/c_2)[c_1 - b r_02(c_2+v)/((a-1)(c_3+v))].       (1)

Require u>=0 and u+v<=nu_k. Change

nu'_k=nu_k-u-v,   nu'_(k+1)=nu_(k+1)+u,
nu'_n=nu_n+v,
lambda'_(k+1,k)=lambda_(k+1,k)+u,
lambda'_(n,k)=lambda_(n,k)+v,                         (2)

with all other multipliers unchanged, and set

a'=(a c_2+v)/(c_2+v),   b'=b c_3/(c_3+v).            (3)

Keep all earlier steps fixed. All new coefficients are nonnegative,
flow is preserved, a'>1, and 0<=b'<=b.

The complete matrix identity is

M(q,lambda',h')-M(q,lambda,h)
 =(a-a')(r_01+r_02)||g_k||^2
  +[u+(b-b')r_12]||g_(k+1)||^2+v||g_n||^2.           (4)

Every coefficient is nonnegative and v>0.

Here are all mixed-entry checks. For the pair (k,n), the coefficient
change is (a'-a)c_2+v(a'-1)=0. For (k+1,n), it is
(b'-b)c_3+b'v=0. For (k,k+1), it is
(a'-a)c_1+u(a'-1)-(b'-b)r_02=0 by (1).
Every pair involving an earlier prefix index is unchanged by (R):
a change of a or b could only couple to such an index through one
of the forward edges excluded by (R). The only changed outgoing
finite masses are +u at k+1 and +v at n; the diagonal dependence
on the changed steps contributes the remaining terms in (4).
The x_0 row is unchanged because every mu_j is unchanged.

The condition in (1) is explicit. In particular, if

(a-1)c_1 c_3 > b r_02 c_2,                            (5)

then all sufficiently small v>0 have u>=0, and they also satisfy
u+v<=nu_k. If r_02=0, (1) reduces to u=v c_1/c_2>=0
without needing a strict inequality in (5). Thus adjacent forward
edges do not obstruct this move; the skip edge k->n is different.

If c_2=0, then c_1>0 by the cut flow. Choose 0<u<=nu_k,
take v=0, and instead set

a'=(a c_1+u)/(c_1+u),   b'=b.

The same changes (2) give
M_new-M_old=(a-a')(r_01+r_02)||g_k||^2+u||g_(k+1)||^2>=0.
No division by c_2 is used in this case.

These identities imply an actual strict worst-case improvement,
not just feasibility at level q. For c_2>0 let
B=product_(t<n)(1+h'_t). On every new legal trajectory with
R=||x_0||>0 and E=2f_n/R^2, one has ||x_n||<=BR and
f_n<=||g_n||||x_n||. Therefore (4) implies

q-E>=v E^2/(4B^2),

hence
W_n(h')<=2q/[1+sqrt(1+v q/B^2)]<q.                   (6)

For c_2=0 use ||g_n||<=(1+b')||g_(k+1)|| to obtain the
same conclusion with v replaced by u/(1+b')^2.
These elementary bounds use the full old PSD matrix evaluated on
the new formal vectors. They do not pretend that the old matching
primal follows the new schedule.

## 3. A stronger necessary condition at a full stationary tuple

Now DROP (R). Suppose the certificate matches a SINGLE legal primal
transcript with ||x_0||=1, 2f_n=q>0, M zero on its data, and
lambda_ij Q_ij=0. Assume positive last steps and all cut stationarity
equalities <g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0, where

z_t(lambda)=sum_(j>t)(mu_j+sum_(l<=t)lambda_(j,l))g_j
            -sum_(i<=t)(sum_(j>t)lambda_(i,j))g_i.

First, <g_(n-1),g_n>=0. This follows from the complete system,
without differentiability of W: vary only the last step by s in
both signs on the same legal function. Its new final gradient
changes by O(|s|), and f_n(s)=f_n-s<g_(n-1),g_n>+O(s^2).
The old PSD form has value O(s^2) on the changed formal vectors
because it annihilates the old data. The first-order change of the
matrix itself has zero pairing by the last cut equality, so its
new value is also O(s^2). Since all new Q_ij<=0 and the same lambda
is nonnegative, the certificate identity implies
2f_n(s)<=q+O(s^2). Taking s in both signs gives the orthogonality.

Suppose nu_k>0. Complementarity gives

f_k=||g_k||^2/2.

The descent inequality then shows y=x_k-g_k is a minimizer. If
0<a<1, Lipschitz continuity of the gradient at the two endpoints
y and x_k forces g_(k+1)=(1-a)g_k: the two Lipschitz inequalities
have lengths summing to ||g_k||, so equality in the triangle
inequality fixes the vector. At a=1 the next point is a minimizer.
For 0<a<1, the last orthogonality makes g_n perpendicular to g_k,
while x_n-y=(1-a)(1-b)g_k; cocoercivity against y then forces
g_n=0. Both cases contradict q>0. Therefore

a>1.                                                 (7)

Put d=||g_k||^2, A=||g_(k+1)||^2, Bn=||g_n||^2,
u0=<g_k,g_(k+1)>, v0=<g_k,g_n>, and
F_i=lambda_(i,k+1)+lambda_(i,n) for i<=k.
All d,A,Bn are positive when q>0.

Interpolation Q_(k+1,k)<=0 and Q_(n,k)<=0, using f_k=d/2
and last-gradient orthogonality, gives

-(a-1)u0>=f_(k+1)+A/2,
-(a-1)v0>=f_n+Bn/2.                                  (8)

The kth cut stationarity equality is

c_1 u0+c_2 v0-F_k d-sum_(i<k)F_i<g_k,g_i>=0.

Combining with (8) yields the quantitative REMOTE-flow obligation

-sum_(i<k)F_i<g_k,g_i>
 >=F_k d+
   [c_1(f_(k+1)+A/2)+c_2(f_n+Bn/2)]/(a-1)
 >0.                                                  (9)

For strict positivity, summing the cut flow gives
c_1+c_2=2+nu_(k+1)+nu_n+sum_(i<=k)F_i>0,
and f_(k+1)+A/2 and f_n+Bn/2 are strictly positive.

Thus some earlier index i<k must satisfy BOTH F_i>0 and
<g_i,g_k><0. If there is no such remote forward edge, nu_k
cannot be positive at a full stationary tuple. In particular:

- At n=2, the sum in (9) is empty, so every full positive-step
  stationary tuple with q>0 has nu_0=0. No triangular-multiplier
  hypothesis is needed.
- At arbitrary n, a positive nu_(n-2) requires a genuine earlier
  forward edge into the last two samples and an oppositely directed
  earlier gradient. Local forward edges alone cannot support it.
- This gives the exact failure point for extending a suffix-only
  multiplier argument: the missing term is the signed remote sum
  in (9), not an unspecified PSD or regularity issue.

The conclusion does not eliminate terminal nu_n, does not force
q=1/(2sum h+1), and does not resolve the exact benchmark profile.
It only narrows the full-feasible obstruction.

All elementary inequalities used above follow from convexity and
1-smoothness. In particular, f(x-grad f(x))<=f(x)-||grad f(x)||^2/2;
and <grad f(x),x-y>>=||grad f(x)||^2 at any minimizer y follows by
adding the two smooth-convex interpolation inequalities with y.
