# Whole optimal-certificate set: quantitative terminal rigidity and a common barrier

Status: UNVERIFIED, self-contained candidate calculations. No new verifier
allocation is assumed. This is the bounded face-level output requested by the
updated TASK.md. It neither proves Huber tightness nor resolves the exact
Zhang--Jiang profile comparison.

## 1. Full setting and a matching stationary instance

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Fix a positive length-n schedule h,
n>=1, and q>0. For distinct sample indices i,j in {star,0,...,n}, use

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

A full certificate has lambda_ij>=0, finite-node flow

    sum_j lambda_ij - sum_j lambda_ji = 2*1_(i=n),

and a PSD quadratic form after x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t:

    M_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
            =q||x_0||^2-<x_0,sum_i mu_i g_i>
                         +sum_(i,j) A_ij <g_i,g_j>,

where mu_i=lambda_(i,star). Let C(h,q) denote ALL such certificates. Its
gradient Schur complement is Z_lambda=A-mu mu^T/(4q)>=0.

Assume there is an ACTUAL legal convex 1-smooth function, normalized by
||x_0||=1, whose GD trajectory attains 2f_n=q. Suppose one certificate
lambda^0 in C(h,q) satisfies every stepsize stationarity equation

    D_t(lambda^0;g)=0, 0<=t<n,

where D_t is the derivative, with all formal vectors fixed, of M_lambda
with respect to h_t. This is a full matching stationary tuple hypothesis.
It does not assert that arbitrary fixed schedules or arbitrary matching
primals are stationary. A globally minimizing schedule to which the usual
full stationary interface applies satisfies this hypothesis.

Every lambda in C(h,q) matches THIS SAME actual instance: its quadratic
value is sum lambda_ij Q_ij<=0, and PSD makes it nonnegative. Consequently

    lambda_ij Q_ij=0 for all i,j,
    sum_i mu_i g_i=2q x_0,                         (1)
    Y Z_lambda=0,                                 (2)

where Y is the linear map from R^(n+1) to the ambient Euclidean space
defined by Y r=sum_i r_i g_i. To prove (1)--(2), complete the square:

    M_lambda=q||x_0-(sum_i mu_i g_i)/(2q)||^2
                    +sum_(i,j)(Z_lambda)_ij <g_i,g_j>.

Both summands are nonnegative and have sum zero. The second gives
Y Z_lambda^(1/2)=0, hence (2). No strict-complementarity assumption is made.

Write p=g_(n-1), g=g_n and b=h_(n-1)>0. Last-step stationarity implies

    <p,g>=0.                                      (3)

Here is a proof retaining the full matrix. Change only the final step by
a small signed u on the SAME actual function. Its terminal point changes
by -u p; its terminal gradient changes by O(u), and
f_n(u)=f_n-u<p,g>+O(u^2). Let v_u be the full formal-vector realization.
PSD and matching give M_lambda(v_0)=0 and M_lambda v_0=0, so
M_lambda(v_u)=O(u^2). Since the matrix is affine in the final step,

    q-2f_n(u)+sum lambda_ij Q_ij(u)
       =u D_(n-1)(lambda;g)+O(u^2).

Every Q_ij(u)<=0. Taking both signs of u proves

    D_(n-1)(lambda;g)=2<p,g>                      (4)

for EVERY lambda in C(h,q). Applying stationarity for lambda^0 gives (3).
Thus last-cut stationarity, unlike unproved earlier-cut stationarity,
survives re-selection with this same matching instance.

Define B_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), with B_0=1. Gradient Lipschitzness and
g_star=0 give ||x_i||,||g_i||<=B_i. Convexity and 2f_n=q give

    ||g||>=q/(2 B_n)>0,
    ||p||>=||g||/(1+b)>=q/[2B_n(1+b)].           (5)

The second inequality uses ||g-p||<=b||p||.

## 2. A quantitative invariant of the ENTIRE certificate set

Choose any reference certificate lambda^r in C(h,q), with source mu^r, and put

    L_face=span( {range Z_lambda : lambda in C(h,q)}
                  union {mu-mu^r : lambda in C(h,q)} ).

This is an ordinary linear span in the finite-dimensional gradient
coefficient space R^(n+1). Equations (1)--(2) imply

    L_face subset ker Y.                          (6)

Let J={0,...,n-2}, and T={n-1,n}; J is empty if n=1.
Let H_J=(<g_i,g_j>)_(i,j in J), which is the actual prefix-gradient Gram
matrix, not an assumed replacement for the full primal.

For EVERY r in L_face, including every source difference, (3) and (6) give
the exact identity

    ||p||^2 r_(n-1)^2 + ||g||^2 r_n^2
            = r_J^T H_J r_J.                      (7)

Indeed r_(n-1)p+r_n g=-sum_(i in J)r_i g_i; square both sides and use
<p,g>=0. In particular,

    r_n^2 + r_(n-1)^2/(1+b)^2
       <= [4 B_n^2/q^2] (sum_(i in J) B_i |r_i|)^2. (8)

This explicit estimate says how much earlier source coefficients must
change to permit a specified change of the two terminal source coefficients.
It is simultaneous over every optimal certificate; it does not require
the reselected certificate to be stationary on all earlier cuts.

Consequences include

    L_face intersect span{e_(n-1),e_n}={0},
    dim L_face<=n-1,                              (9)

and injectivity of mu -> mu_J on the entire source-coefficient set.
For n=1 every source coefficient is fixed across the whole set, and every
Z_lambda is zero. For larger n the conclusion allows nonzero earlier
Schur slack and source variation. It does not imply Z_lambda=0.

Applying (7) to the columns of a PSD square root of any Z_lambda gives
the additional exact identity

    ||p||^2 (Z_lambda)_(n-1,n-1)
       +||g||^2 (Z_lambda)_(n,n)
           = trace(H_J (Z_lambda)_JJ).             (10)

Thus, for example,

    (Z_lambda)_(n,n)
      +(Z_lambda)_(n-1,n-1)/(1+b)^2
    <= [4 B_n^2/q^2] (sum_(i in J)B_i^2)
                                  trace((Z_lambda)_JJ). (11)

Equations (7), (8), (10), (11) are quantitative restrictions on the full
optimal set, not a claim that its common kernel equals the observed
primal range. Additional common kernel directions are permitted.

The fixed-coordinate graph formulation is also useful. Because projection
L_face -> R^J is injective, the terminal coordinates of r in L_face are
linear functions of r_J. These functions can be extended to R^J. Hence
there are fixed vectors u,v in R^J such that

    r_(n-1)=-u^T r_J,  r_n=-v^T r_J for all r in L_face.

The vectors k_p=(u,1,0), k_n=(v,0,1) annihilate every Z_lambda, and
mu^T k_p and mu^T k_n are constant over C(h,q). In particular

    Z_JT=-Z_JJ [u v],
    Z_TT=[u v]^T Z_JJ [u v]                       (12)

for every certificate in the set, with the SAME u,v. This uses the full
Schur matrix, including singular prefix blocks.

## 3. Certificate mixing gives an explicit descent test without stationarity

The exact covariance identity explains why different certificates cannot
be inspected in isolation. For finitely many certificates lambda^a at
the same h,q, positive weights theta_a summing to one, and their average,

    Z_bar=sum_a theta_a Z_a
          +(1/(4q))sum_a theta_a(mu^a-mu_bar)(mu^a-mu_bar)^T. (13)

This follows by expanding A_bar-mu_bar mu_bar^T/(4q). Every multiplier
of the average is nonnegative, its flow is correct, and its FULL matrix
is PSD, being the average of the full matrices.

By choosing finitely many certificates whose ranges and source differences
span L_face, one gets range Z_bar=L_face. One direct justification is
that the range of a sum of PSD matrices is the sum of their ranges;
the covariance range is the span of the included source differences.

For completeness, a terminal vector in this range yields an actual
same-length descent, without a stationary-instance assumption.
Suppose for some averaged certificate

    Z_bar >= kappa (a e_(n-1)+s e_n)(a e_(n-1)+s e_n)^T,

where kappa>0, a>0 and s in {+1,-1}. Put B=B_n, Bprev=B_(n-1), and

    C=q/(2B^2)+1/kappa.

Choose a positive d with

    d<=a/C,
    d<=q^2/(8 a B^2 Bprev^2),
    d<b if s=+1.

Replace b by b-s d, leaving every earlier step fixed. Then

    W_n(h_0,...,h_(n-2),b-s d)
        <=q-d q^2/(8 a B^2)<q.                    (14)

Here is the full uniform argument. For ANY legal instance normalized by
||x_0||=1, put E=2f_n and R=a p+s g. The averaged PSD certificate gives
q-E>=kappa||R||^2. Convexity gives ||g||^2>=E^2/(4B^2).
The identity for ||R||^2 gives

    -2s<p,g> >= (||g||^2-||R||^2)/a.

The new endpoint is x_n+s d p, so smoothness gives

    E_new<=E-(d/a)[E^2/(4B^2)-(q-E)/kappa]+d^2 Bprev^2.

Insert E^2>=q^2-2q(q-E), then the displayed d bounds. This proves (14)
uniformly over dimensions and functions. The smoothness inequality can
also be written as one additional interpolation inequality plus the
nonnegative square ||g_new-g-s d p||^2/2. Thus the argument retains a
full interpolation/PSD certificate with an auxiliary old endpoint.

A vector supported only on e_n in range Z_bar contradicts tightness:
q-E>=kappa||g||^2>=kappa E^2/(4B^2) uniformly forces E<=r<q.
A vector supported only on e_(n-1) gives the same contradiction with
kappa/(1+b)^2. Consequently a nonzero intersection of L_face with the
terminal two-coordinate plane either improves the tight bound at the
same schedule or gives the genuine final-step descent (14).

For exactly TWO certificates, if their source difference is
Delta mu=A e_(n-1)+B0 e_n with A*B0!=0, the equal-weight average has

    kappa=B0^2/(16q),
    a=|A/B0|, s=sign(A/B0)

after rescaling the direction. Formula (14) is therefore an explicit
quantitative way of exploiting re-selection. It cannot occur under
the matching stationary hypothesis of Sections1--2, by (8).

## 4. A common strict forward-edge barrier two steps from the end

Assume now n>=2, set k=n-2 and a=h_k, and retain the actual stationary
instance of Section1. Suppose SOME certificate in C(h,q) has

    nu_k=lambda_(star,k)>0.

Complementarity for that certificate on the common instance forces

    delta_k=f_k-||g_k||^2/2=0.                    (15)

Meanwhile Q_(n,n-1)<=0 and (3) imply

    delta_(n-1)=f_(n-1)-||p||^2/2
                  >=q/2+||g||^2/2>0.              (16)

First a>1. To see this, the unit-step descent inequality from (15) shows
that y=x_k-g_k is a minimizer. For 0<=a<=1, Lipschitz gradient equality
between x_k and y forces p=(1-a)g_k. A detailed justification: the two
Lipschitz bounds along the segment sum to ||g_k||, so equality in the
triangle inequality forces the stated vectors. At a=1, p=0 contradicts
f_n>0. At a<1, (3) implies <g_k,g>=0; since x_n=x_k-a g_k-b p, the
constraint Q_(n,k)<=0 becomes q/2+||g||^2/2<=0, again impossible.
Thus a>1.

Define sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0 for the common actual instance. Direct expansion
of the exact GD relation x_(k+1)=x_k-a g_k gives

    Q_(star,k+1)+(1/a)Q_(k+1,k)+((1-a)/a)Q_(k,k+1)
           -Q_(star,k)
      =(1/a)||p-(1-a)g_k||^2.

Using (15) and (16),

    sigma_(k,k+1)
      =[a delta_(k+1)+sigma_(k+1,k)
                     +||p+(a-1)g_k||^2]/(a-1)
      >=[a/(a-1)](q/2+||g||^2/2)
      >=[a/(a-1)](q/2+q^2/(8B_n^2))>0.          (17)

Therefore EVERY certificate in C(h,q) has

    lambda_(k,k+1)=0,  nu_(k+1)=0.                (18)

The first is a common strict inactive edge, not a missing edge in one
bad extreme certificate. Re-selection at unchanged h,q cannot restore
that adjacent capacity. This obstruction is compatible with remote
forward edges and does not prove that a simultaneous full motion fails.

## 5. The inactive cost remains after re-selection

The precise finite joint-perturbation identity is recorded here to prevent
a representation change from hiding the cost in (17). Use the stationary
base lambda^0 and its common actual instance. For a new full certificate
(q',lambda',h'), let dh=h'-h and dlambda=lambda'-lambda^0. Define

    z_t(lambda)=sum_(j>t)[mu_j+sum_(l<=t)lambda_(j,l)]g_j
                   -sum_(i<=t)[sum_(j>t)lambda_(i,j)]g_i,
    B_mix=sum_(t<n)dh_t <g_t,z_t(dlambda)>.

Here z_t is linear in lambda, and D_t(lambda;g)=<g_t,z_t(lambda)>.
The full matrix is affine separately in h and lambda; pairing it with
the OLD Gram realization, using flow, old matching, and all old
stationarity equations, gives exactly

    <M(q',lambda',h'),G_old>
       =q'-q-sum_ij lambda'_ij sigma_ij+B_mix.

Full PSD therefore requires

    q'-q >= sum_ij lambda'_ij sigma_ij-B_mix.      (19)

In the setting of Section4 this includes the explicit charges

    sum_ij lambda'_ij sigma_ij
       >= lambda'_(k,k+1) [a/(a-1)](q/2+q^2/(8B_n^2))
                     +nu'_(k+1)(q/2+q^2/(8B_n^2)). (20)

The two edges are distinct and every omitted charge is nonnegative.
If one instead uses a reselected base certificate that is not stationary
on this same primal at earlier cuts, the linear term
sum dh_t D_t(lambda_reselected;g) must be retained. Formula (19) with
the original stationary base already includes it inside B_mix.
No unsupported stationarity transfer across all cuts has been made.

## 6. Scope

The identities and explicit bounds (7)--(14), together with the strict
common barrier (17)--(20), are new face-level quantitative constraints.
They survive arbitrary re-selection in the full nonnegative-flow/PSD set.
They allow extra kernel directions, all remote edges, and non-Huber
levels q>1/(2sum h+1).

They do not show that a global minimizer has a positive nu_(n-2), that
every other star-outgoing multiplier vanishes, that the full slack is
rank one, or that q equals the Huber value. Even Huber tightness would
still leave the exact/asymptotic DP mass restriction. The remaining
search must pay (20) by a genuine joint schedule/flow motion, or obtain
a different uniform comparison; convex-face terminology alone does
not supply that bridge.

# Angular limits of the actual three-vector continuation

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate, max22. No63-step or127-step
numerical problem is evaluated. This manuscript is appended to the complete
seed/7/15/31 construction in max_actual31_parent_problem.md for the joint
submission candidate. Its inequalities stand on direct definitions and the
original Q convention in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, not on an unverified fact.

The concrete seed has

 s=sqrt2, lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
 a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600, eps=1/100,
 K=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/K, A=1+2a+b,
 U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),eps),
 g=(U,U,V,W), x=(mU+aV+W,bU+aV+W,aV+W,W),
 f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, p=x_0,
 R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Kz+eps^2, N=||W||^2=rho^2 eta+eps^2.

The word is(a,b,a), the last residual is0, and the penultimate residual is
y_2=(a-1)V+W. The companion joint construction supplies every original
seed row and its global realizing function, then reconstructs the actual
zero-map7, rational-map15 and rational-map31 children. Here we prove a
new obstruction applying to this ACTUAL zero-residual three-vector data.

## 1. A parameterized necessary condition for every next cross map

Suppose a left child's last leaf is beta times an isometric copy J of the
displayed seed, beta>0. Thus its endpoint and penultimate residual are

 w_L=beta J(W), y_L=beta J(y_2),
 e=<V,y_2>=(a+rho-1)eta>0, E_L=beta^2 e.

Let a right child have initial point p_R, R_R=||p_R||>0, and let v_R be a
convex combination of its retained gradients. Define

 q=-<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2,
 T_R=||v_R-(<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2)p_R||.

For a bridge excess r>0, the scale is alpha=r||w_L||/R_R. Let L and Rmap
be ANY child isometries into ANY real Euclidean space satisfying
alpha Rmap(p_R)=-r L(w_L). All preceding cross maps may be different.
Put h_angle=<W,y_2>=rho(a+rho-1)eta+eps^2. Orthogonal decomposition gives

 y_2=(h_angle/N)W+t, <t,W>=0,
 ||t||=(a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N).                            (1)

Indeed y_2=(a-1)V+W and
||V||^2-<V,W>^2/N=eta eps^2/N. Here a>1 and eps>=0.
The exact bridge forces

 <alpha Rmap(v_R),L(w_L)>=r beta^2 N q.

The component of alpha Rmap(v_R) perpendicular to L(w_L) has norm
alpha T_R, because Rmap preserves inner products and takes the p_R-axis
to the negative w_L-axis. Its inner product with beta L J(t) is at least
-alpha beta T_R||t|| by Cauchy--Schwarz. Hence

 <alpha Rmap(v_R),beta L J(y_2)>-beta^2 e
    >= beta^2 e(r H-1),                               (2)
 H=(h_angle/e)q-((a-1)eps sqrt(eta)/(R_R e))T_R.               (3)

Because the weights of v_R sum to1, the left side is EXACTLY their
convex combination of ORIGINAL Q rows receiving the left penultimate
sample. All values and the optimum are retained. Thus every legal parent
must satisfy r H<=1 when H>0. The nonzero terminal angle is fully charged
by the second term of(3); it has not been discarded by setting eps=0.

For identical balanced levels A_j=lambda^j A and r_j=theta A_j, this is
theta lambda^j A H_j<=1. Any horizon-dependent seed family retaining
these anchors must therefore force H_j=O(1/r_j) or leave this geometry.

## 2. The actual31 input has no63-step isometric-copy parent

Every later left embedding retains the initial point P and gradient G_4
of the first7 child. Its first transverse map was exactly zero. The
cross pairing in that first join is

 <L0(u),alpha0 R0(v)>=-r0<u,W><p,v>/R^2,
 r0=theta A, alpha0=r0 sqrt(N)/R.

Consequently, writing D=<p,W>=rho(b-1)+eps^2,

 <P,G_4>=-r0 D m/R^2, ||G_4||=alpha0.

Use the right child's retained gradient4 alone as v_R in(3). Then

 q=r0 D m/R^4, T_R<=alpha0.                            (4)

The left child's last leaf still has(1), no matter how its15 and31
couplings were chosen. At the63-step parent the original row in(2)
is Q_(36,30).

The following rational estimates are uniform over the parameter range

 7/5<a<283/200, 2<=b<401/200,
 99/100<rho<=1, 0<=eps<=1/100,
 eta=(b-1)/(a+rho).                                    (5)

The actual seed belongs to(5); 41/29<sqrt2<99/70, proved by squaring,
gives its a,b bounds. Within(5) one has

 29/5<A<1167/200<6, 17/5<m<171/50,
 239/100<K<483/200, 1<=z<201/200,
 2/5<eta<201/478, e=(K-1)eta>1/2,
 R^2=m^2+Kz+eps^2<564943/40000<71/5,
 R>17/5>3, N<421/1000<4/9, D>99/100.

For N use rho<=1 and201/478+1/10000<421/1000. Since
12/29<theta<5/12, these bounds also give

 12/5<r0<5/2,
 alpha0<[(5/2)(2/3)]/(17/5)=25/51<1/2.

Equation(4) implies

 q>[(12/5)(99/100)(17/5)]/(71/5)^2
   =5049/126025>1/25.

Since h_angle/e=rho+eps^2/e>99/100, the positive term in(3) is greater than
99/2500. The subtracted term is less than
[(1/2)(1/100)*1*(1/2)]/[3*(1/2)]=1/600. Therefore
H>99/2500-1/600>3/80.

For a31-step child the next bridge excess is
r3=theta lambda^3 A=lambda^2 A>(29/5)^2=841/25. Thus every possible
next transverse map and every ambient dimension obey

 Q_(36,30)/E_L>(841/25)(3/80)-1=523/2000>0.             (6)

This excludes the actual rational31 child's63-step parent. It also
allows arbitrary different15 and31 maps in the two children, provided
the fixed zero-map7 child and the same last leaf are retained. It does
not transfer the obstruction from a different residual family. The
successful31 construction and(6) concern different next-parent depths.

## 3. Different first maps and nonidentical horizon-dependent leaves

Changing the first map removes(4), so(6) must not be asserted for that
larger class. Instead a convex combination of three retained gradients
produces another uniform obstruction at a later finite depth.

Allow each three-step leaf to have its OWN parameters in(5), its own
seed vectors, and the displayed legal projection jets and values.
Build balanced binary concatenations with arbitrary child isometries.
For masses A_L,A_R prescribe a source-matched bridge by

 r(A_L+A_R+r)=A_L A_R, r>0,
 A_parent=A_L+A_R+r.                                  (7)

Its scale is r||w_L||/R_R and all child jets are retained. Identical
masses give r=theta A_L and A_parent=lambda A_L. There is no reset,
extra coordinate, changed inherited value or deleted terminal gradient
in this defined family.

In the RIGHT child of a proposed new join consider its first two leaves.
The first leaf has initial point p0, endpoint W0 and initial norm R_R.
Let the second leaf's parameters be a_b,b_b, with A_b=1+2a_b+b_b.
The first bridge gives the EXACT vector relation

 (a_b+b_b)G_4+a_b G_6+G_7=-r_first W0.

After dividing by A_b its positive coefficients sum to1. The resulting
convex combination is

 v_R=-tau W0, tau=r_first/A_b.                        (8)

All subsequent left embeddings retain(8), its norm and its pairing with
the initial point. It is independent of the first transverse map.
Both first-leaf masses are in(29/5,1167/200), so their ratio is between
14/15 and3/2. Substitution r=tau A_b in(7) gives

 A_b tau^2+(A_first+A_b)tau-A_first=0.

This increasing polynomial has negative value at2/5 and positive value
at1/2, using those two mass-ratio bounds. Therefore2/5<tau<1/2, and(8)
implies

 q=tau<p0,W0>/R_R^2>99/3550,
 T_R<=||v_R||=tau||W0||<1/3.

The LEFT child's final leaf can have different parameters in(5).
For that leaf h_angle/e>99/100, while the negative term in(3) is less than
[(1/2)(1/100)*1*(1/3)]/[3*(1/2)]=1/900. Hence

 H>9801/355000-1/900>1/40.                             (9)

The last inequality follows from9801*1800>47*355000. This bound is
uniform over all the leaf choices, all the earlier maps and dimensions.
For this larger class a legal new parent must have r<40.

The large bridge is forced at a fixed balanced depth. Equation(7)
implies0<r<min(A_L,A_R). Implicit differentiation yields
partial r/partial A_L=(A_R-r)/(2r+A_L+A_R)>0, and the analogous right
derivative is positive. The mass join is increasing as well. Induction
therefore places every depth-j mass above lambda^j(29/5). The join of
two depth4 children (each63 steps) has

 r>theta lambda^4(29/5)=lambda^3(29/5)>80.

Equations(2),(9) make the weighted original-row sum greater than E_L>0.
At this127-step parent the rows are Q_(68,62), Q_(70,62), Q_(71,62), with
the positive weights from(8). No such parent is legal.

Thus even nonidentical boxed leaves chosen separately for the full
target horizon, with completely new maps at EVERY internal node, cannot
iterate this source-matched family indefinitely. This respects the
original non-anytime quantifiers: the parameters may depend on the full
schedule and target horizon; the constant1/40 is uniform over(5).
Changing the leaf angles outside(5), changing the actual atomic vectors,
using longer atomic trajectories, or a rule that does not retain these
anchors lies outside the result. It is not an upper bound on W_n(h).

## 4. The true31 objective and its exact benchmark comparison

The companion full31 construction has true-distance normalized gap
kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2, kappa=A^2 N/R^2. It is a legal positive witness,
but its exact benchmark ratio is less than1/4, as follows analytically.
The bounds from(5) give

 A<1167/200, N<421/1000,
 R^2>(17/5)^2+239/100=279/20,
 kappa<573355269/558000000<21/20.

Moreover(21/20)^8<3/2 since2*37822859361<3*25600000000.
Since lambda>12/5 and A>29/5, lambda^3 A>50112/625>80.
Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives, for p_exp=log_2 3,

 B_31<=1+4*31^p_exp<1+4*32^p_exp=973.

Therefore the actual full-word witness ratio satisfies

 B_31*kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2
   <973*(3/2)/6400=2919/12800<1/4.                    (10)

This bounds ONLY THIS WITNESS, not the supremum W_31(h[3]). No objective
tail was appended, and no boundary-sign hypothesis for a tail was
silently imported. A new continuation must alter a substantive geometric
ingredient and independently account for any final function-gap
conversion against the exact B_n sequence.

## DOC-002594 — project/research/max31_seed_angle_surplus_tradeoff.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002594 -->
# Legality prices larger terminal angles

Status: UNVERIFIED joint continuation of the actual31 construction and its
angular obstruction. This is a textual proof, with no new optimizer or
fixed-candidate replay. Its purpose is to distinguish changing a seed
parameter from changing the actual geometric mechanism.

## 1. The legal three-vector seed family

Take

 353/250<a<1413/1000, 501/250<b<401/200,
 0<=rho<=1, eps>=0, E=eps^2,
 z=b-1, K=a+rho, eta=z/K, m=a+b, A=1+2a+b,
 U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),eps),
 g=(U,U,V,W), x=(mU+aV+W,bU+aV+W,aV+W,W),
 y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The actual fixed a,b from the rational31 construction lie strictly in
this range. Its slight width also permits different leaf words.
Put e=(K-1)eta, R^2=m^2+Kz+E, N=rho^2 eta+E and
kappa=A^2 N/R^2.

These exact projection jets satisfy every original interpolation row
with the optimum0 if and only if

 0<=E<=(1-rho)e.                                      (11)

To see this, residuals are
y0=(m-1)U+KV+eps e3, y1=zU+KV+eps e3,
y2=(K-1)V+eps e3, y3=0, with energies m-1,z,e,0.
Here e3=(0,0,1) is a coordinate vector. At residual0 the nontrivial
gradient vertices0,V,W give normals-(m-1),-a,-a-(1-rho)z+E;
at residual1 they give-z,0,-(1-rho)z+E;
at residual2 the vertices0,U,W give-e,-e,-(1-rho)e+E.
All remaining normals vanish. Since0<e<z and rho<=1, (11) is necessary
from the last displayed row and sufficient for every listed row.
As in the complete joint construction, the convex hull of0 and these
gradients realizes the data by its quadratically regularized support
function, and p=mU+aV+W makes0 a closest minimizer. Thus R is the true
distance, and kappa is the actual core gradient-energy ratio.

## 2. Positive surplus forces rho>19/20

Set Delta=A^2 N-R^2. The coefficient of E in Delta is A^2-1>0.
Using the largest legal E=(1-rho)e bounds every legal Delta above by
Delta_max(rho), where direct expansion yields

 (a+rho)Delta_max(rho)=Dlin(rho),
 Dlin(rho)=z[A^2((a-1)+(2-a)rho)
                -(a^2+a-1+(a+2)rho)]-m^2(a+rho).       (12)

This expression is affine in rho. Its slope is
z[A^2(2-a)-(a+2)]-m^2, which is strictly positive: using
A>29/5, 2-a>1/2, a+2<7/2, z>1, m<171/50 gives the lower bound

 (29/5)^2/2-7/2-(171/50)^2>0.

At rho=19/20, the bracket in(12) is

 B=A^2(9/10+a/20)-(a^2+39a/20+9/10).

Here A<5831/1000, so A^2<34001/1000 and
A^2(9/10+a/20)<33004/1000. Also a>353/250 gives
a^2+39a/20+9/10>5647/1000. Thus B<27357/1000.
If B is nonpositive then Dlin(19/20)<0 directly. If B is positive,
z<201/200 gives z B<27494/1000. On the other hand
m>427/125, m^2>11669/1000, a+19/20>1181/500, so
m^2(a+19/20)>27562/1000. In either case

 Dlin(19/20)<-68/1000.                                (13)

The positive slope of Dlin therefore implies Delta<0 for rho<=19/20.
In particular, any legal leaf with kappa>=1 must have rho>19/20.
This deduction used the FULL terminal normal constraint(11), including
the positive normal charge E, rather than treating the extra coordinate
as a free norm increase.

There is a uniform cost on the excluded side. For rho<=19/20,
a+rho<5/2, so(12)-(13) give Delta<-17/625. All legal leaves satisfy
R^2<16: m<171/50, K<483/200, z<201/200, E<=e<z imply this bound.
Hence

 rho<=19/20 ==> kappa<1-17/10000=9983/10000.            (14)

## 3. A uniform angular obstruction for every nonnegative-surplus leaf

Allow nonidentical legal leaves with the a,b range above. Assume for
now that each leaf has rho>19/20, which is forced if each has kappa>=1.
Keep the actual isometric source-matched joins: for child masses A_L,A_R
the bridge excess r is the positive solution of
r(A_L+A_R+r)=A_L A_R, and the scale is r||w_L||/R_R.

In any right child with at least two leaves, the first bridge gives a
convex combination v_R=-tau W0 of its gradients4,6,7, where W0 belongs
to its first leaf and2/5<tau<1/2. The coefficients are the second leaf's
(a_b+b_b,a_b,1) divided by its mass1+2a_b+b_b. Their source identity,
and the range of tau, follow from the exact bridge and the mass bounds
29/5<A_leaf<1167/200. These facts are unchanged by EVERY preceding map.

For the first and last leaf data separately, (11) and rho>19/20 imply

 R^2<71/5, R>17/5,
 D=<p,W>=rho z+E>19/20,
 K-1>4/3, e=(K-1)eta>1/2,
 eta<43/100, E<201/4000, N<49/100.

For example R^2<(171/50)^2+(483/200)(201/200)+201/4000<71/5.
Also eps sqrt(eta)/e<=sqrt((1-rho)/(K-1))<sqrt(3/80)<1/5.

We give the original-row argument again for completeness. If the final
left leaf has scale beta, its endpoint is beta J(W) and penultimate
residual beta J(y2), where y2=(a-1)V+W. Put h_angle=<W,y2> and
q=-<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2, T_R=||v_R-(<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2)p_R||.
The new bridge sends the p_R-axis to the negative left endpoint-axis.
The axial component of the weighted cross normal is beta^2 r h_angle q.
The perpendicular component is at least
-beta^2 r(a-1)eps sqrt(eta)T_R/R_R, because the part of y2 perpendicular
to W has norm(a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N), and the right gradient combination's
perpendicular norm is alpha T_R, alpha=r beta sqrt(N)/R_R.
Subtracting the receiving energy E_L=beta^2 e gives exactly

 sum weights Q >= E_L(r Gamma_ang-1),
 Gamma_ang=(h_angle/e)q
             -(a-1)eps sqrt(eta)T_R/(R_R e).           (15)

No interpolation row is deleted, and the positive weights sum to1.
Now q=tau D/R_R^2>19/710 and h_angle/e>19/20. Also
T_R/R_R<=tau sqrt(N0)/R_R<7/68, using N0<49/100. The previous estimate
on eps sqrt(eta)/e and a-1<1/2 prove

 Gamma_ang>361/14200-7/680>1/70.                       (16)

The last rational inequality is equivalent to361*4760>117*14200.
It remains valid when the right initial leaf and the left final leaf
are different, since all component bounds are uniform.

Balanced source-matched masses at depth j exceed lambda^j(29/5), by
monotonicity of the positive bridge root; partial r/partial A_L is
(A_R-r)/(2r+A_L+A_R)>0 since r<min(A_L,A_R). Thus a join of two depth4
children (a127-step parent) has r>lambda^3(29/5)>80. Its weighted original
rows Q_(68,62),Q_(70,62),Q_(71,62) therefore obey

 sum weights Q/E_L>80/70-1=1/7>0.                     (17)

This excludes that parent even with nonidentical positive-surplus leaves,
arbitrary earlier maps and horizon-dependent rho,eps choices. More
precisely it excludes any such subtree all of whose leaves have rho>19/20.

## 4. Mixed leaves have a concrete, nonzero price

Every legal balanced depth-J tree, J>=5, in THIS same three-vector family
must therefore contain a leaf with rho<=19/20 inside each of its disjoint
depth5 subtrees. There are2^(J-5) such subtrees. Hence at least one leaf
in32 must incur the uniform core deficit(14). This is a necessary density
condition, not a claim that paying it is sufficient for interpolation.

The normalization cost is exact. For a legal node, its true initial
distance is R_L by the retained positive-cone identity, its endpoint norm
squared is r^2 N_L N_R/R_R^2, and A_parent*r=A_L A_R. Consequently

 kappa_parent=kappa_L*kappa_R,
 kappa_root=product_(all leaves)kappa_leaf.             (18)

No lost leaf factor may be omitted. If M_plus denotes the supremum of
kappa over this legal atomic family, the density condition gives

 kappa_root <= M_plus^(2^J-2^(J-5))
                  *(9983/10000)^(2^(J-5)).             (19)

The explicit actual seed in the full joint manuscript has kappa>1, so
M_plus>1; replacing any further deficit factors by M_plus preserves(19).
A zero endpoint makes kappa_root=0 and satisfies(19) directly; the axis
arguments only concern nonzero endpoint nodes.
For an elementary finite bound, R^2>m^2>11, A<6 and
N<=eta+e<1+z<3 imply M_plus<10. The sharper value is left open; the
coarse bound does not claim decay of(19). In particular this result does
NOT exclude all mixed-leaf policies. It identifies the exact normalization
cost that any such horizon-dependent escape must pay. The original
objective-profile target is still unresolved, including the conversion
from these core energies to a final function gap.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002595 -->
# A legal multi-cut construction with exact remote-history transfer

Status: verifier-accepted as fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a. Worker: max3.
The fact statement is the verified construction. This readable research copy
expands two residual compact products from the submitted proof: s_j m_j^2
means s_j m_j^2, and q z_term means q z_term.

This is an actual one-dimensional convex 1-smooth function construction,
not a free choice of curvature at each iterate and not a PEP reformulation.
It keeps remote ray offsets and function-value areas. It gives a lower-bound
functional stronger than the verified single-cut functional K_n, but no
uniform comparison to U_n^ZJ has been proved.

## Inputs, feasibility, and quantitative output

Take any finite nonnegative schedule and select k checkpoint steps. In
chronological order write the schedule as

\[
(\block_0,b_0,\mathcal{L}_1,b_1,\ldots,b_{k-1},\mathcal{L}_k),
\]

where each block_i is a possibly empty list of nonnegative ordinary steps,
s_i is its sum, and b_i is a nonnegative checkpoint step. This preserves
the individual steps and their order; their sums suffice only because the
constructed derivative will be constant during each ordinary block.

For k>=1 define nonterminal gradient magnitudes m_0,...,m_{k-1} and
nonnegative offset sums O_0,...,O_k as follows:

\[
m_0=\frac1{1+s_0},\qquad O_0=0,
\]

and, for i=0,...,k-2,

\[
m_{i+1}=\frac{(b_i-1)m_i-O_i}{1+s_{i+1}},\qquad
O_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i.
\tag{1}
\]

Also set O_k=O_{k-1}+s_{k-1}m_{k-1}; its last value is only bookkeeping.
Require

\[
m_i>0\quad(0\leq i<k),\qquad
m_i\geq m_{i-2}\quad(2\leq i<k).
\tag{2}
\]

Define the terminal distance budget and earlier terminal-ray slope by

\[
z_term=(b_{k-1}-1)m_{k-1}-O_{k-1},\qquad
m_{\rm old}=\begin{cases}0,&k=1,\\m_{k-2},&k\geq2.\end{cases}
\]

The terminal feasibility requirement is

\[
z_term\geq(1+s_k)m_{\rm old}.
\tag{3}
\]

Choose any final slope magnitude

\[
q\in\left[m_{\rm old},\frac{z_term}{1+s_k}\right].
\tag{4}
\]

Put e_term=sum of s_i m_i^2 over 0<=i<k with i congruent to k modulo2.
There is a convex differentiable 1-smooth f:R->R with minimizer0 and
f(0)=0 such that GD from x_0=1 with the exact supplied schedule has

\[
2f(x_n)=2 e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2.
\tag{5}
\]

Thus this expression is a legal lower bound on W_n(h). Scaling f by L
preserves the normalized statement at any L>0. The best q within (4) is

\[
q_{\rm opt}=\max\left\{m_{\rm old},\frac{z_term}{2s_k+1}\right\}.
\tag{6}
\]

Indeed the quadratic in (5) is concave, its unconstrained maximum is at
z_term/(2s_k+1), and this point is no larger than the upper endpoint in (4).

For k=0 take z_term=1, e_term=0, m_old=0 and s_k=s_0. Equations (4)--(6) then
give the usual exact Huber value 1/(1+2s_0). For k=1 and b_0>=1, the
terminal old slope is0, and (5)--(6) give exactly
(b_0-1)^2/[(1+s_0)^2(1+2s_1)]. Therefore the maximum over all feasible
selected chains includes every term of K_n in fact b98bb807d514e80d.

## Explicit construction on the two rays

Write a signed point as x=(-1)^i r at stage i, where r>=0 is its distance
from0. On each ray construct a radial derivative magnitude Q_+(r) or Q_-(r),
starting at Q_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Let A_+ and A_- denote the largest distances
previously visited on those rays, and let w_+,w_- be their derivative
magnitudes. Initially all four numbers are0.

At a nonterminal stage i the starting distance is a_i. On its active ray
write A_old,w_old for the previous record and slope and d_i=A_old-w_old.
Choose the magnitude m_i and extend that ray's derivative by a slope-1 ramp
from (A_old,w_old) to

\[
(r_i,m_i),\qquad r_i=A_{\rm old}+m_i-w_{\rm old}=d_i+m_i,
\]

followed by a constant segment of value m_i up to a_i. Conditions (2)
give m_i>=w_old; for the first visit to a ray its old slope is0. The
recurrence will give

\[
a_i=d_i+(1+s_i)m_i=r_i+s_i m_i,
\tag{7}
\]

so a_i>=r_i>=A_old. This extension does not change any previously visited
data. During block_i every iterate remains on this flat segment, since its
partial step sums lie between0 and s_i. Thus the stage ends at signed
distance r_i with gradient sign (-1)^i and magnitude m_i.

At the checkpoint b_i the next signed point has opposite sign and distance

\[
a_{i+1}=b_i m_i-r_i=(b_i-1)m_i-d_i.
\tag{8}
\]

The previously unvisited amount will be nonnegative by the next-stage
feasibility condition. The active ray's new outer record is a_i and its
slope there is m_i. Hence its offset on its next visit is

\[
d_{i+2}=a_i-m_i=d_i+s_i m_i.
\tag{9}
\]

Initially d_0=d_1=0. Write O_i=d_i+d_{i+1}. Equations (8)--(9) imply
O_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i and
a_{i+1}-d_{i+1}=(b_i-1)m_i-O_i. Equating this last quantity with
(1+s_{i+1})m_{i+1} gives exactly (1). Also a_0=1 and d_0=0 imply the
stated m_0, completing the induction that verifies (7) for each nonterminal
stage. Positive m_i means nonterminal stages do not reach0. The terminal
case z_term=0 is permitted only with zero old slope and causes no difficulty.

At terminal stage k, the starting distance satisfies a_k-d_k=z_term. Extend
its ray by a slope-1 ramp from its old record to slope q and then by a flat
segment to a_k. Condition (4) makes the ramp endpoint d_k+q no smaller
than the old record and no larger than a_k-s_k q. Thus every final-block
iterate stays on its flat segment. The final distance is a_k-s_k q.

For k=0 the same construction begins directly from the initial origin datum,
with a_0=1 and q given by (4).

After the last stage extend each radial derivative constantly to infinity.
Define the global derivative by f'(x)=Q_+(x) for x>=0 and
f'(x)=-Q_-(-x) for x<=0, and define f(x)=integral_0^x f'(t)dt.
Each radial derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, with
Q_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Across0, for x<=0<=y,

\[
0\leq f'(y)-f'(x)=Q_+(y)+Q_-(-x)\leq y-x.
\]

Consequently f' is globally continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.
This proves differentiability, convexity, 1-smoothness, and a minimizer at0.
The earlier stage calculations prove the exact original GD recursion.
In particular no interpolation or dimensional-realizability gap remains
within the candidate construction.

## Retained function values give the exact objective

On one ray, a ramp from magnitude w to v adds area (v^2-w^2)/2. Its
following nonterminal flat segment has length s_i m_i and adds s_i m_i^2.
Therefore at the outer record after any nonterminal stage i, the radial
function value equals

\[
m_i^2/2+\sum_{j\leq i,\ j\equiv i\ ({\rm mod}\ 2)}s_j m_j^2.
\tag{10}
\]

This identity follows by induction on successive visits to that ray: the
squared-gradient contributions telescope and the flat-segment areas remain.
At the final stage the new ramp endpoint has value e_term+q^2/2. The distance
from that endpoint to the final point is z_term-(s_k+1)q, on which the derivative
equals q. The final value is consequently

\[
e_term+q^2/2+q[z_term-(s_k+1)q]
=e_term+q z_term-(s_k+1/2)q^2,
\]

proving (5). The initial distance is exactly1, so the normalization is exact.

## Exact two-cut illustration

For h=(1,5,1,9,1) select checkpoint steps5 and9. Then s_0=s_1=s_2=1,
m_0=1/2, O_0=0, m_1=1, O_1=1/2, z_term=15/2, m_old=1/2,
e_term=1/4, and q_opt=5/2. All feasibility conditions hold. Formula (5) gives

\[
W_5(1,5,1,9,1)\geq2(1/4)+(15/2)^2/3=77/4.
\]

The actual points are 1,1/2,-2,-1,8,11/2. On the positive ray the
derivative is r on [0,1/2], 1/2 on [1/2,1], r-1/2 on [1,3], and5/2
on [3,infinity). On the negative ray its magnitude is min(r,1).
Direct integration gives f(11/2)=77/8, consistent with the formula.
By contrast the exact K_5 value is max{1/35,4/23,1/3}=1/3.

## Interface still missing for the project target

The construction works for each chain satisfying (2)--(4), and these
conditions can be checked from the selected block sums and step values.
It does not establish the existence of a sufficiently strong feasible chain
for every schedule. The assertion that maximizing (5) over feasible chains
always gives (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ would be a new theorem-strength obligation.
No such assertion is assumed. The terms -O_i and e_term are genuine retained
history contributions; discarding them changes the construction and is not
a permitted constant-level approximation without a separate proof.

The one-step calibration agrees with the exact value1/3. H3 agrees with the old C51 finite draft. H4 remains numerically Huber. H5 and H6 show a non-Huber finite regime. The statuses in the table are retained; all numerical values are observations, not exact upper bounds. H6 has original full dual matrix minimum eigenvalue -8.303257217559616e-5, so its dual is not a certificate despite the small primal-dual objective gap.

B_n is the exact finite benchmark recurrence evaluated at70 decimal digits, not an asymptotic envelope and not a certified interval. Full recurrence arrays and maximizing split indices are saved in each raw file.

The full all-pair model, scaling, dual conversion and analytic calibration are documented in max3_c58_full_pep_model.md. CLARABEL0.11.1/CVXPY1.9.3 were used through the existing public wrapper, one thread. The source actually used for all five calls is preserved as max3_c58_full_pep_batch_used.py; max3_c58_full_pep_batch.py differs only by the later guard that refuses closed ledgers.

## Raw files and hashes

Each has a separate `_increments.json` companion, with full Gram in coordinates(x0,g0,d1,...,dn), d_i=g_i-g_(i-1), the increment principal block, the exact integer forward/inverse coordinate transforms, and the dual coefficient matrix in the same coordinates. These are floating-point coordinate conversions of the raw arrays, not new solves. No eigenvalue or coefficient edits were made.

## Exact finite reconstruction after discovery

The C59 proof candidate b4f25156d6599097 defines a new rational-coordinate core and an analytic auxiliary coordinate from the H6 output. Its full4160-pair integer audit passes; an explicit joint conjugate interpolant has unique minimizer0 and normalized payoff >56/2711>1/50>4/M6. The raw optimizer itself is not asserted to be exactly legal. The new defining data, audit script and proof are public in max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json, max3_c59_h6_exact_primal_repair.py and max3_c59_h6_legal_primal.md.

An exact increment-Gram certificate also shows that this repaired finite trajectory cannot be represented with at most SIX jumps in every gradient coordinate, under any orthonormal coordinate choice. In particular it does not automatically belong to the separate two-jump primal cone. Its dominant negative queried correlation survives the auxiliary repair.

C59 remains UNVERIFIED because no routine verifier call is allocated. It excludes a1/M6 upper bound for this finite word only. The asymptotic changing-horizon family and the original all-schedule profile remain unresolved.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002597 -->
# C58 complete-PEP extension: model and scaling audit

This is numerical discovery under the one-time max3 allocation in
`max3_full_objective_extension_budget.json`. Every attempted call is
recorded before model construction; its status and elapsed time are saved
afterward. Imports are conservatively included in the measured time.
Post-solve array serialization and residual diagnostics are not solver time.
No eigenvalues or multipliers are repaired or truncated. No hidden solver
fallback or retry is performed. The public runtime invokes CLARABEL0.11.1
through CVXPY1.9.3 with one computational thread.

## Complete mathematical model

For the actual supplied schedule h of length n, take independent formal
vectors x0,g0,...,gn. Let xi=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t gt and let the optimum sample
have x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Optimize over the full symmetric Gram matrix
and all n+1 function values, with no rank or support restriction:

    maximize 2f_n,
    Gram >=0, Gram_(x0,x0)<=1,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0
          for EVERY distinct ordered pair in {star,0,...,n}.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the equivalence with the actual
smooth-convex worst case. No terminal inequality is deleted here.

In the original formal basis, let A_ij be the symmetric coefficient
matrix for the quadratic part of Q_ij, and let Fcoef have entries+1 at
i and-1 at j when these are finite. The script constructs the full sparse
arrays directly. Both star directions and every finite direction occur.

## Exact change of coordinates used for conditioning

Let M=1+sum h and D=diag(1,1/M,...,1/M). The variables in the solver are

    Ghat=Gram(x0,Mg0,...,Mgn), fhat=Mf.

The original Gram is D Ghat D. The solver constraint is M Q_ij<=0,
with coefficient matrix M D A_ij D, and it maximizes2fhat_n. This is an
invertible change of variables and a positive constraint/objective scaling.

If the solver returns raw pair multipliers lambda and raw norm multiplier
rho, the ORIGINAL pair multipliers are the same lambda and the original
dual objective is q=rho/M. The complete original coefficient matrix is

    C_orig=q e0 e0^T+sum lambda_ij A_ij.

The scaled stationarity matrix is M D C_orig D. It is compared directly
with the RAW dual multiplier of the PSD constraint. Value flow is checked
as Fcoef^T lambda-2 e_n, using the original objective. The saved data include
both scaled and original Grams, all raw pair labels/multipliers, both
objective matrices, PSD duals, function values, all original Q residuals,
all flow residuals, complementarity, eigenvalues, and scaling metadata.

## Analytic one-step calibration

For h=(1), choose multipliers lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=
lambda_(1,0)=1 and all others zero. Their value coefficients cancel2f1.
The full coefficient identity at q=1/3 is

    (1/3)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
      =(1/3)||x0-(3/2)(g0+g1)||^2+(1/4)||g0-g1||^2.

This proves the upper bound1/3 directly. The scalar Huber function with
threshold1/3, initial point1, and step1 has g0=g1=1/3, x1=2/3 and
f1=1/6, attaining the bound. Thus the calibration target is exactly1/3.

## Schedules and benchmark

For each case actual_Hm the script independently sets

    c=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), j=0,...,m-1.

It does not truncate, reorder, or replace any step. Binary floating-point
evaluation of the displayed exact algebraic word is saved, along with the
formula. The script compares with 1/(1+2sum h), 1/(1+sum h), and B_n from
the full finite primitive/dominant recurrences in acceptedb98bb807d514e80d.
The latter are evaluated with70 decimal digits, saving both entire
recurrence tables and maximizing split indices. These decimal tables are
not claimed as rigorous interval certificates.

The raw JSON is saved to both the worker's own research directory and the
PUBLIC project research directory before any summary is printed. Each
summary contains the file hash. A negative eigenvalue remains visible,
and an inaccurate solver status is retained verbatim. Finite numerical
agreement is neither an exact certificate nor an asymptotic result.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002598 -->
# C59: a fully specified legal H6 adversary above 4/M6

Status: **exact finite reconstruction and joint proof candidate; UNVERIFIED**.
No new fact is claimed and no verifier submission is allocated. The only
accepted mathematical predecessor is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for full
smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate construction.

This is a finite diagnosis of the actual H6 word, not an asymptotic theorem.
The word here uses epsilon=1/6. It is not the length-63 subword obtained by
fixing epsilon=1/m for a much larger horizon m.

## 1. Statement and exact input object

Put

    c=(7/6)(1+sqrt(2)), a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<6,
    h=H6, n=63, S=sum_(i<63)h_i, M6=1+S.

There is a completely specified convex differentiable 1-smooth function
on R^66 with unique minimizer0 and an actual GD trajectory for this h
such that

    2(F(X_63)-F(0))/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2
             > 56/2711 > 1/50 > 4/M6.              (1)

In particular, this finite word cannot have a full valid upper certificate
at level1/M6. No assertion is made about eventual large-m feasibility.

The rational construction data are the lower-triangular integer matrix T
of order65 and the64 integer values v_i in

    research/max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json.

The PUBLIC file's SHA256 is

    7376e26ca81edc6438c0563e008c845ce439e424bd42f9f65c37a07867d6b113.

Its fields `coordinate_integer_rows_lower_triangular` and
`function_value_numerators` are part of the definition, with the common
denominator D=10^15. For reproducibility the complete defining integer
data are also appended to the shared proof finding. No numerical solver
claim is used as a premise of the construction below.

Let the rational vectors in R^65 be

    v_start=T[0,:]/D,  q_i=T[i+1,:]/D (0<=i<=63),
    u_i=v_i/D.

Define the core positions by the EXACT algebraic GD recursion

    z_0=v_start,
    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i q_i.                            (2)

Here q_63 is not used in (2), but will be the core terminal gradient.
The original numerical Gram is not treated as an exact Gram. These
rational vectors define their own positive semidefinite Gram by dot
products, regardless of any properties of the source solver output.

## 2. The finite exact checks, with their integer certificate

For these core data include the zero optimum jet. Define

    Q^core_ij=u_i-u_j+<q_i,z_j-z_i>+||q_i-q_j||^2/2,
    delta^core_i=u_i-||q_i||^2/2,

with the star's position, gradient and value all zero. The following
strict bounds are verified by integer comparisons of the defining data:

    Q^core_ij < eta=1/500000000000 for EVERY i!=j,
    delta^core_i > -eta for every finite i,
    ||v_start||^2 < 1001/1000,
    u_63 > 21/2000,
    200<S<256,  h_i>1 for every i.                  (3)

The first line checks all65*64=4160 ordered pairs, including both optimum
directions and both terminal directions. Here are the exact formulas used
to audit (3), so the finite part is not a feasibility-tolerance assertion.

The data file gives each step as

    h_t=(A_t+B_t sqrt(2))/54432

with integer arrays A_t,B_t. These are exactly the recursively defined
steps: arithmetic in Q(sqrt(2)) with a=(-5/7,5/7) and c=(7/6,7/6)
produces those integer pairs. Let

    G_ab=sum_k T[a,k]T[b,k].

Thus <q_i,q_j>=G_(i+1,j+1)/D^2 and
<q_i,v_start>=G_(i+1,0)/D^2. Set Hden=54432 and define integer arrays

    R_ij=Hden G_(i+1,0)-sum_(t<j) A_t G_(i+1,t+1),
    I_ij=                  -sum_(t<j) B_t G_(i+1,t+1).

Then <q_i,z_j>=(R_ij+I_ij sqrt(2))/(Hden D^2).
When j=star, set both of these numerators to zero. When i=star, every
term containing q_i is zero. Set v_star=0 and the corresponding gradient
Gram entries to zero. The exact pair numerator formula is

    Qa_ij=2 Hden D(v_i-v_j)+2(R_ij-R_ii)
                   +Hden(G_(i+1,i+1)+G_(j+1,j+1)-2G_(i+1,j+1)),
    Qb_ij=2(I_ij-I_ii),
    Q^core_ij=(Qa_ij+Qb_ij sqrt(2))/(2 Hden D^2),    (4)

using the displayed star conventions. The missing diagonal terms for a
star index in (4) are zero.

For the radical, use the rational interval

    Lrad/10^50 < sqrt(2) < (Lrad+1)/10^50,
    Lrad=141421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694.

Its validity follows by squaring the two positive integer endpoints.
For each pair an upper-bound numerator with denominator
2 Hden D^2*10^50 is

    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*(Lrad+1)  if Qb_ij>=0,
    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*Lrad      if Qb_ij<0.          (5)

The data file contains all4160 integer triples from (4)--(5). Every one
satisfies `upper_numerator*500000000000 < denominator`. The largest
upper bound occurs at the directed pair (31,30) and is approximately
1.371014491112536e-12; the exact numerator and denominator are retained
in `audit.max_Q_upper_numerator` and `audit.max_Q_upper_denominator`.
This decimal is descriptive, whereas the integer comparison proves the
inequality used in (3).

The other checks in (3) use the same radical interval for S and each h,
and use, without a radical,

    delta^core_i=(2v_i D-G_(i+1,i+1))/(2D^2),
    ||v_start||^2=G_00/D^2.

The minimum core delta is already positive in these data (approximately
8.5986e-11), but only the weaker stated lower bound is needed. The audit
script is PUBLIC at `research/max3_c59_h6_exact_primal_repair.py`.
Its single finite integer check completed in under0.1second and used no
optimization, search, eigenvalue clipping, or large symbolic computation.
This is a transparent finite arithmetic component of an unverified proof
candidate; it is not a substitute for the verifier's acceptance.

## 3. One controlled auxiliary coordinate repairs all pairs

Choose

    kappa=1/512, t0=1/8,
    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i.

Every factor is positive since h_i<=S<256. For any initial segment,
the elementary product inequality

    product_t(1-a_t)>=1-sum_t a_t  (a_t>=0, sum a_t<1)

follows inductively from (1-a)(1-b)>=1-a-b. It gives

    1>=xi_i>=1-kappa S>1/2.

For i<j, positivity and h_i>1 imply

    xi_i-xi_j>=xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa/2.

The distance to the optimum sample xi_star=0 is also greater than
kappa/2. Thus these bounds cover every distinct pair, including star.

Define the66-dimensional jets and values

    X_i=(z_i,t0 xi_i),
    G_i=(q_i,t0 kappa xi_i),
    f_i=u_i+t0^2 kappa xi_i^2/2
       =u_i+xi_i^2/65536, 0<=i<=63,
    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                      (6)

Their GD recursion is EXACT by (2) and the recursion for xi. The full
interpolation residual separates by coordinates into

    Q_ij=Q^core_ij
          -t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2.      (7)

This is a polynomial identity, not a claimed separability of the final
function. The second term is the residual for the single quadratic
auxiliary coordinate at the displayed samples. Since1-kappa>1/2,
its magnitude is strictly greater than

    t0^2 kappa^3/16=2^(-37)>eta.

Combining with (3) proves Q_ij<0 for ALL4160 ordered pairs simultaneously.
The same common values and coordinates are used in every pair.

## 4. One global function and the true initial distance

For each finite sample put

    Y_i=X_i-G_i, delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2.

Equation (6) gives

    delta_i=delta^core_i+t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2
             >-eta+t0^2 kappa/16>0.                (8)

Add the star with Y_star=0,delta_star=0, and define

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in {star,0,...,63})
                              [<Y_i,v>-delta_i],
    F(X)=sup_v[<X,v>-H(v)].                         (9)

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and (7), this is one globally defined finite
convex differentiable function with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating
every value and gradient in (6). Therefore its GD trajectory is the one
already specified, with the actual ordered word H6.

The finite set of strict gaps in (8) makes the star's zero affine function
uniquely active in a neighborhood of v=0. Hence H(v)=||v||^2/2 there and
partial H(0)={0}. The maximizing-vector condition for (9) identifies the
minimizers of F with partial H(0), as in the accepted predecessor. Thus
argmin F={0}; no remote or nonclosest minimizer is used in normalization.

The true squared distance and terminal value satisfy

    R^2=||v_start||^2+1/64 < 1001/1000+1/64
                              =8133/8000,
    2F(X_63)=2u_63+t0^2 kappa xi_63^2 >21/1000.

Consequently

    2F(X_63)/R^2> (21/1000)/(8133/8000)
                 =56/2711>1/50>4/(1+S),

which proves (1), subject only to the explicitly supplied finite integer
checks on the defining data. If a unit initial norm is desired, use
F_unit(x)=F(Rx)/R^2 and initial point X_0/R; the smoothness remains1,
the steps remain h, and the normalized payoff is unchanged. Multiplication
of the function by any L>0 supplies the corresponding L-smooth instance
with steps h_i/L.

## 5. A concrete feature of the surviving geometry

The rational core gradients have the exact inner product

    <q_35,q_36>
      =-14715168270572693173715883215/10^30
      <-14/1000.

The extra coordinate changes this by at most t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24),
since0<xi_i<=1. Thus

    <G_35,G_36><-1/100.

Both indices are queried. The actual object retains substantial signed
gradient correlation; it is not a Huber common-gradient line, a mutually
orthogonal gradient transcript, or an all-gradients acute-cone trajectory.
The auxiliary coordinate repairs small interpolation errors in an already
strong correlated core, in contrast to attempting to repair the grossly
illegal fixed scalar C55 template. No claim is made that this description
is a scalable generating rule.

There is also a basis-independent distinction from a bounded-jump
coordinate construction. Let d_i=G_i-G_(i-1),1<=i<=63, and let J be their
Gram matrix. If an orthonormal coordinate system makes every coordinate
of the gradient list change at most k times, then

    2 sum_(i<j)|J_ij| <= (k-1) trace(J).             (10)

Indeed each scalar coordinate of the jump list has at most k nonzero
entries. Cauchy--Schwarz gives (sum|a_i|)^2<=k sum a_i^2, hence
2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|<=(k-1)sum a_i^2. Sum over coordinates and apply the
triangle inequality to the resulting off-diagonal entries. Since J is
unchanged by orthogonal changes of physical coordinates, (10) applies to
every such representation.

For the exact rational core, the integer increment Gram is saved PUBLICLY
in `research/max3_c59_exact_increment_gram.json`. Its entries follow
directly from the rational vectors already defined, with denominator D^2.
Exact integer summation gives

    trace(J_core) approximately 0.44249400386221016,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij| approximately 2.306374594722243,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij|-5trace(J_core)>9/100.       (11)

Only the last, rational inequality is used. The auxiliary jump vector is
a_i=t0 kappa(xi_i-xi_(i-1)); all entries are negative and
(sum|a_i|)^2<t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24). Its contribution is aa^T. By the
triangle inequality, adding aa^T can lower the left side of (11) by at
most

    2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|+5sum_i a_i^2
           <=5(sum_i|a_i|)^2<5*2^(-24).

Thus the full repaired J still satisfies
2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|>5trace(J). Equation (10) rules out every representation
in which all coordinate gradients have at most SIX chronological jumps.
In particular this actual finite adversary is outside the common two-jump
primal cone. The large core margin in (11) ensures this distinction is
not caused only by the tiny auxiliary quadratic coordinate.

## 6. Scope and next open interface

This candidate is finite, exact in its definitions, and includes all
function values, gradients, positions, actual GD equations, and the true
distance. It does not rely on a numerically feasible Gram or a nearly
positive semidefinite dual. The source solver arrays are preserved
unchanged; the rational data and added coordinate are separately labeled
new objects.

The estimate rules out1/M6 for this one actual word. It does NOT refute an
upper bound valid only at sufficiently large horizons, establish a common
pattern for the changing parameters epsilon=1/m, or prove the all-schedule
Zhang--Jiang profile. In particular the length63 block changes physically
as the enclosing horizon changes. The missing scalable interface is an
analytic rule for the correlated core that controls ALL interpolation
pairs and distance under those parameter changes. Repeating this finite
data set, extrapolating its numerical eigenvectors, or fixing only one
turning pair would not provide that rule.

No routine verification allowance was used. This remains a complete
finite proof candidate, not an accepted fact.

# C60: a nonlinear two-state legal trajectory with dense turning gradients

Status: **new explicit construction and scoped calculations; UNVERIFIED**.
No solve or verifier call is used. The numerical allocation is closed.
This branch follows the finite C59 reconstruction by seeking an analytic
correlated core. It does not assume that the C59 data belong to this family.

The construction works for every finite nonnegative schedule, including
zeros and arbitrarily large entries, with one fixed globally convex
1-smooth function. Its all-pair law and true closest-minimizer distance
are explicit. The quantitative payoff on the critical H_m remains open.

## 1. The global function, its gradients, and its minimizers

In R^2 let

    C_par={(t,s): s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},
    F_par(X)=sup_(g in C_par) [<X,g>-||g||^2/2].      (1)

The set is closed, convex and nonempty, and contains0. Completing the
square shows that the maximizer in (1) is the Euclidean projection of X
onto C_par. It exists and is unique because the squared-distance objective
is coercive and strictly convex. Thus F_par is finite and convex.

For completeness write p(X) for that projection. Its elementary normal
condition is

    <X-p(X),g-p(X)><=0 for every g in C_par.

Applying this twice and adding gives

    ||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>.

Thus p is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizers in (1) gives

    0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>
        <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.

Consequently F_par is differentiable, its gradient is p, and it is
1-smooth. This proof supplies the needed global property directly.

The minimizers are exactly

    argmin F_par={(0,y):y<=0}.                      (2)

Indeed F_par>=0 by the feasible vector0. For X=(0,y), y<=0, every g=(t,s)
in C_par has <X,g>=ys<=0, so the maximum in (1) is0. Conversely, if
the vertical coordinate y>0, taking g=(0,s) with small s>0 makes the
objective positive. If the horizontal coordinate x is nonzero and y<=0,
take g=(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with sufficiently small nonzero t of the sign of x:
the linear term xt dominates the terms of quadratic and quartic order.
This again makes the objective positive. These cases prove (2).

This F_par is not a convex quadratic: its minimizer set is a ray, whereas
the nonempty minimizer set of an unconstrained convex quadratic is an
affine subspace.

## 2. Coordinates that make every interpolation pair explicit

For a real r and v>=0, put

    u=v+r^2,
    X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2), -v/sqrt(2)),
    G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).                           (3)

Then X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any (t,s) in C_par,

    <X-G,(t,s)-G>
      =u[r(t-r)-(s-r^2/sqrt(2))/sqrt(2)]
      <=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0.

The normal condition therefore proves that G(r) is the actual gradient
of F_par at X(r,v). Direct substitution into (1) gives

    F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                (4)

For any two sampled states r_i,v_i and r_j,v_j, with the actual values
from (4), the complete signed interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
        =-(u_j/2)(r_i-r_j)^2<=0.                   (5)

The first equality after Q follows by substituting
f_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2. The second uses the displayed parabolic
coordinates. This proves EVERY ordered pair simultaneously for one
function; no local, adjacent, or cycle relaxation is being used.

For an initial nonzero r_0 and v_0>=0, the closest minimizer in (2) is

    X_star=(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)),
    R_0^2=dist(X_0,argmin F_par)^2
            =r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.                (6)

The optimum sample fits the same formulas with r_star=0, v_star=v_0,
u_star=v_0. Thus (5) includes both of its directions. Choosing the origin
instead would generally overestimate the true initial distance; (6) is
the normalization used throughout.

## 3. The exact GD transition, with no interpolation optimization

For ANY step h>=0, starting from state(r,v), define

    v_plus=v+h r^2,
    r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus
                      =r(1+v+r^2-h).              (7)

The cubic's left side is strictly increasing from minus infinity to
plus infinity because its derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1. Thus (7)
defines exactly one real r_plus, by an explicit scalar algebraic rule.
There is no nearest-feasible-trajectory problem hidden in this definition.

The vertical coordinate of X-hG is -v_plus/sqrt(2), and its horizontal
coordinate is the right side of (7). Hence

    X(r_plus,v_plus)=X(r,v)-h G(r).

This proves actual GD for the same global function. In particular h=0
leaves the state unchanged, and every finite positive step gives finite
new state. All v_i remain nonnegative, regardless of gradient sign changes.

For a supplied finite nonnegative schedule h_0,...,h_(n-1), iterate (7)
from any r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Its exact normalized payoff is

    P_par(h;r_0,v_0)
       =[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]
                        /[r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2].  (8)

The function (1), all values(4), gradients(3), positions(3), and initial
distance(6) are fixed together. Thus (8) is an actual lower witness for
W_n(h), albeit not yet a profile-strength one on the critical schedules.

Some immediate identities expose the changing geometry:

    u_plus-u=(h-1)r^2+r_plus^2,
    X_plus,horizontal=X_horizontal[1-h/(1+u)].      (9)

In particular, for schedules with all h>=1, the normal parameter u is
nondecreasing, but r and the horizontal gradient can turn repeatedly.
The Hessian direction changes with r; this is not a fixed linear spectral
transcript. The vertical state accumulates h r^2 rather than discarding
previous turns.

## 4. Exact dense-jump calibration on a different schedule

Take any sigma in(0,1), v_0>=0, and define

    U_0=1+v_0+sigma,
    rho=(1+sigma)/(1-sigma),
    h_i=2U_0 rho^i/(1-sigma),
    r_i=(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),
    v_i=U_0 rho^i-1-sigma.                         (10)

These v_i are nonnegative. Substitution into (7) gives
v_(i+1)-v_i=h_i sigma and
r_(i+1)(1+v_(i+1)+sigma)=r_i(1+v_i+sigma-h_i),
so (10) is an exact trajectory of F_par. Its payoff is

    P_par=rho^n/U_0+sigma/(2U_0^2).                 (11)

This calibration is legal and can have large payoff. It is a geometric
step sequence, NOT the critical ruler word, so (11) proves no claim about
H_m or the original profile. It only checks that the mechanism supports
correlated turns without a feasibility repair.

There are n nonzero collinear gradient jumps in (10):

    G_i-G_(i-1)=(2(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),0), 1<=i<=n.

Their Gram J has trace4sigma n and twice the absolute off-diagonal sum
4sigma n(n-1). For any Gram realizable with at most k nonzero jumps in
each orthonormal coordinate, Cauchy--Schwarz coordinate by coordinate
implies2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|<=(k-1)trace(J). Hence this trajectory requires
some coordinate with at least n jumps in every orthonormal realization.
Dense chronological correlation does not require a large ambient dimension.

For a completely rational parameter example, sigma=1/4 and v_0=0 give
rho=5/3, h_0=10/3, h_1=50/9, and r_i=(-1)^i/2. Formula (11) is then
an exact expression, not a numerical simulation.

## 5. Two simple parameter limits do not yet give the desired mechanism

First fix a finite schedule and fix v_0=A>=0. Let r_0=t tend to zero.
Induction in (7) gives

    v_i=A+O(t^2),
    r_i=t product_(j<i)[1-h_j/(1+A)]+O(t^3).

At each induction step, the inverse cubic has nonzero derivative1+A
at zero, and its cubic term is O(t^3), giving the displayed expansion.
Thus the payoff tends to the ordinary scalar quadratic value

    [1/(1+A)] product_(j<n)[1-h_j/(1+A)]^2.         (12)

The implicit constants here depend on the entire fixed finite schedule.
They have NOT been bounded uniformly along growing H_m. Consequently
(12) is a calibration and an explanation of why an infinitesimal core
does not itself add a new mechanism; it is not a class obstruction.

Second put S=sum h_i and sigma=r_0^2. If v_0>=S, no horizontal sign flip
occurs and |r_(i+1)|<=|r_i|. Indeed h_i<=v_i and the right side of (7)
has the sign of r_i. Evaluating the increasing cubic at r_i shows that
its excess over that right side is h_i r_i(1+r_i^2), with the appropriate
sign, so the claimed decrease in absolute value follows. Hence

    v_n<=v_0+S sigma,
    P_par <= [1+v_0+S sigma+(3/2)sigma]
                                  /(1+v_0+sigma)^2. (13)

Along any growing-horizon choices with
v_0/[S(1+sigma)] tending to infinity, this gives S P_par tending to zero.
Thus placing the initial state arbitrarily far down the flat minimizer
direction also loses the desired scale. This rules out only the displayed
parameter regime; intermediate coupled regimes remain open.

## 6. The natural geometric calibration is small on the entire critical word

One tempting choice is to match the geometric growth ratio rho in(10)
to the physical critical parameter c_m. This means

    m>=128, epsilon=1/m,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
    sigma=(c-1)/(c+1), r_0=sqrt(sigma), v_0=0.       (14)

For THIS particular starting rule, the full actual H_m trajectory obeys

    P_par(H_m;r_0,0)
              < (2500/m^2) 2^(-2^m).              (15)

The same is true with the opposite initial horizontal sign, by symmetry.
This does not bound the family over other choices of r_0,v_0.

Here is a proof that tracks all later queried states, rather than inferring
the answer only from the first cancellation. Elementary parameter bounds are

    12/5<c<5/2, 2/5<a<3/7,
    2/5<sigma<3/7, 7/5<b0=1+a<10/7.

For the lower bound on a, use 1+sqrt(2)<17/7 and
(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>=127/129, giving a>889/2193>2/5.
The other inequalities follow by direct comparison of positive fractions.
Since c is greater than1+sqrt(2),

    0<sigma-a
      =epsilon[2lambda(lambda-1)+lambda^2 epsilon+c+1]
                                               /[c(c+1)]
      <2epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2).                 (16)

For the last bound the numerator in brackets is less than12, using
lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/128, while c(c+1)>204/25>8.

At the first actual step b0, (7) gives

    14/25<v_1=b0 sigma<30/49<5/8,
    r_1(1+v_1+r_1^2)=r_0(sigma-a),
    0<r_1<2/m.                                    (17)

We now prove that the entire remaining trajectory stays in the region

    1/2<=v<=3/4, |r|<=1/10.                        (18)

Inside this region the exact step rule gives explicit amplitude bounds.
For a level-zero step b0, the numerator factor
1+v+r^2-b0 is positive and at most1+3/4+1/100-7/5=9/25.
The denominator1+v_plus+r_plus^2 is at least3/2, so

    |r_plus| <= L0 |r|, L0=1/4.

For a level j>=1 step b_j=1+a c^j, one has b_j>=2-epsilon>1.76,
whereas1+v+r^2<=1.76. The numerator is therefore negative, and

    |r_plus| <= Lj |r|, Lj=(2/7)c^j.               (19)

Indeed its absolute value is less than a c^j and its denominator is at
least3/2, while a<3/7. These bounds use the current state in (18) but
do not require the next state to be in that region.

Let P_j be the product of these scalar bounds over the full word H_j,
with P_0=1, and put A0=2/7 and beta=c^2/14<1/2. For j>=1,

    P_j=beta^(2^(j-1))/(A0 c^(j+1)),
    theta_j=P_j Lj=beta^(2^(j-1))/c<1.             (20)

This follows either by multiplying the known counts of each ruler level
or by P_(j+1)=P_j^2 Lj and P_1=1/4. Every prefix product in every H_j
is at most1: it is true for H_0; in the right child the left-child and
central-step prefactor is P_j Lj=theta_j<1, and the induction applies
inside both children. At j=0 that prefactor is1/4.

For the accumulated vertical drift, let E_j be the weighted prefix sum

    E_j=sum_(i in H_j) h_i [product_(t<i)L_(level(t))]^2.

Then E_1=b0<3/2 and, for j>=1,

    E_(j+1)=(1+theta_j^2)E_j+P_j^2 b_j.            (21)

The two needed infinite bounds are elementary. Since c>2,beta<1/2,

    sum_(j>=1)theta_j^2
        <(1/4)sum_(j>=1)2^(-2^j)<=1/8.

Also b_j<c^j for j>=1: both a and c^(-j) are less than3/7. Therefore

    sum_(j>=1)P_j^2 b_j
       <sum_(j>=1)49*2^(-2j-5)=49/96.

For this estimate insert(20), use A0^(-2)=49/4, c>2,
beta^(2^j)<2^(-2^j), and2^j>=j+1. Finally
product(1+theta_j^2)<=1/[1-sum theta_j^2]<8/7,
as follows by expanding the finite products and bounding their elementary
symmetric sums by powers of the total sum. Passing to the limit preserves
the bound. Equations(21) consequently imply

    E_j<(3/2+49/96)(8/7)=193/84<3                 (22)

for every j.

In the suffix of H_m after its first step, every prefix product is at
most4, because reinserting that first L0 multiplies it by1/4. The suffix
weighted drift sum is at most16E_m<48. Hence, as long as(18) has held,

    |r_i|<=4r_1<8/m<=1/16<1/10,
    v_i<=v_1+48r_1^2<5/8+192/m^2<3/4.

The lower bound v_i>=v_1>1/2 follows from v_plus=v+h r^2. These strict
bounds prove(18) throughout by a first-exit argument: the same estimates
control a proposed first exiting step, which is impossible.

At the terminal sample the entire suffix product is4P_m. From(17),(20),

    r_n^2<(64/m^2)P_m^2.

The numerator of(8) is less than2r_n^2 by(18), and the initial denominator
sigma(1+sigma)^2 exceeds98/125>3/4. Thus

    P_par<3r_n^2<(192/m^2)P_m^2
          <(2352/m^2)2^(-2^m),

which proves(15). For the exact benchmark, accepted factb98bb807d514e80d
gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3), so this particular parabolic witness divided
by U_n^ZJ tends to zero. Only this fixed starting rule is excluded.

The responsible state variable is visible in(17): matching the geometric
ratio makes the critical first step almost annihilate the horizontal
coordinate. The subsequent normal state remains near its post-first-step
value, and the complete ruler-product/drift bounds above prevent recovery.
This is a statement about the entire actual legal trajectory, not a
one-pair dual test or a comparison with an independently reset right child.

## 7. The concrete remaining critical-word problem

For the actual critical word fix, within each horizon m,

    c_m=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j).

Let T_h denote the explicit two-state transition (7). The full block map
is defined EXACTLY by

    Phi_0=identity,
    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j composed with T_(1+a_m c_m^j)
                                     composed with Phi_j.

The central task in this branch is to select explicit r_0(m),v_0(m), or
prove a useful uniform upper bound for this mechanism, while controlling
the exact payoff (8) under Phi_m. The model is already an actual legal
function for every such selection, so no separate interpolation optimization
is required. What is missing is the QUANTITATIVE estimate on that nonlinear
recursion. Neither (11), the small-amplitude calibration, nor (13) fills it.

An open subgoal is to compare the increase in v across both copies of a
dyadic block with the horizontal sign and gain at its central bridge.
The state v must remain shared between the copies; restarting it, freezing
the curvature, or replacing the two copies by independent instances would
change the function and lose the construction.

## 8. Source grounding and verification scope

Jiang, Yue and Zhou, arXiv:1911.12545v2 (2021), Theorem2.2, gives the
Euclidean projection onto S={(x,y):||x||^2<=y}: inside S it is the point,
and outside it is(x/(1+mu),y+mu/2), where mu>=max(0,-2y) is the unique
solution of ||x||^2/(1+mu)^2=y+mu/2. The paper's surrounding definition
of S and full proof were read. It supplies a closely matched projection
mechanism, not a GD complexity result for the present function. Its
cubic-regularization convergence theorem is not invoked.

Sections1--6 derive the two-dimensional specialization and all required
properties directly. No external theorem is used as a black box, and no
unverified earlier project result is a premise. The finite C59 data merely
motivated seeking dense turning gradients. Every new statement here remains
a draft until accepted by the verifier; no claim is made that this family
solves the all-schedule exact-profile problem.

## DOC-002600 — project/research/max3_c61_projection_objective_universality.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\max3_c61_projection_objective_universality.md
